# ACCESSION: the build contract

*What gets built for the vertical slice, how, and how each part is shown to work. Written 23 October 2026 from [`DECISIONS.md`](DECISIONS.md) and Julian's answers to it, then revised after five adversarial reviews (consistency, engineering, rules, testability, and the 3D view's revertibility). Those reviews found rules an implementer would otherwise have guessed, and fixing them took the file past DESIGN's 10,000-word cap, to about 16,000; it is written for the build, not for reading through. Precedence: this file, then `DECISIONS.md`, then `DESIGN.md`, then the drafts in `design/`, which are reference material. Where this file fixes a rule or a number, the code follows this file; changing either means changing both in the same commit, and a lint checks the numbers.*

## 0. The principle, applied to building

Every result must be understandable by and explainable to a person. In the code that means four things:
1. **The core is deterministic,** so any bug, death or test failure can be replayed exactly from a seed and a list of actions, in a browser, by anyone.
2. **Every event carries its cause,** recorded where it happens, and every sentence the player reads about the rules is generated from the same data the rules run on.
3. **Every claim a test or bot makes comes with its numbers and a replay:** sample size, interval, seeds, build.
4. **The drawing never decides anything.** Both board views draw the same view model; the rules never ask a renderer a question.

---

## 1. What the slice contains

The slice answers one question: do three departments with different rules, drawn from one grammar on generated museum layouts, read as a museum and play as a roguelike for a stranger on a phone, in about 30 minutes?

**In:**
- **The rules:** the nine universal systems (§8), as the player is taught them.
- **Three hand-authored departments,** trimmed from the worked samples:
  - S.9 *Animals Not Yet Seen by the Day Staff*;
  - A.4 *Things Kept in Duplicate, Before the Repeal*;
  - M.11 *Long Galleries, Considered as Telegraph Lines*.

  Between them they bring five department-owned modules (§5.4) and three Centrepiece forms: a treasure and two fights.
- **Three layout programmes:**
  - enfilade (M.11);
  - case labyrinth (A.4);
  - sculpture court (S.9).

  The last two share the quadrangle scaffold, all are at Cabinet size (5–7 rooms), and each has an authored Intro gallery.
- **A run** of six departments on three floors of two, drawn from the three, with two Stair Halls and a short Exit (§8.9).
- **Two board views:** top-down (the reference) and the 3D view, behind one setting (§6).
- **Saves:** autosave after every action, the visitor's card, and an archive download.
- **Meta:**
  - the death label and its recap;
  - Your Collection, with one accession per department visit whose Certificate you earned;
  - the Catalogue of exhibits seen.
- **Sound,** with a visual twin and a caption for every sound that carries information.
- **Tooling:**
  - the test layers 1–8 of the playtesting plan (§10);
  - bots;
  - the single-file build.

**Out of the slice:**

| Feature | Status |
|---|---|
| Light, lanterns and lux | Department-owned systems, not universal (D5); none of the three slice departments owns light, so every tile is lit |
| Tiptoe | Cut (DESIGN default 5) |
| Search and hidden panels | Cut for the slice by this file; hinged panels are not generated |
| Staff guards, Custody and escort | Department-owned; S.9's guards are exhibits |
| Coins, shops, NPCs | After the gate |
| The Loan | Kept for v1 by D11; deferred past the slice gate by this file |
| Fire, damage kinds, forgeries, equipment attribution | Cut or after the gate |
| The sketch verb and classes | After the gate; the visitor has no class |
| The generation pipeline, the persona harness, the sequencer at pool size | After the gate |
| Patron membership, Directives, heat | After the gate |

**Size, to calibrate expectations:** the drafts estimated 15,000–21,000 lines for a slice of this kind, ±40%. This repository's history says plans grow.

---

## 2. Architecture and repository layout

Plain JavaScript, classic `<script>` tags, no bundler for the game. Each file defines one global namespace object (`const Rng = (() => { … })();`), as in `grift-city/`. The same files load in the browser, in Node tests (through a `vm` context) and in the bots.

```
accession/
  index.html               development entry: one <script> tag per file, in dependency order
  css/game.css, bag.css, coach.css
  js/core/                 PURE: no DOM, no clock, no Math.random, integers only (§3)
    rng.js, int.js         string-keyed streams (hash → mulberry32); integer helpers with stated rounding
    state.js               canonical serialisation, hashing, cloning, the plain-data check
    numbers.js             the numbers of record (§8.10), the only home of rule constants
    registry.js, reg/      the grammar's API and the nine systems; one fragment per owner in reg/ (system,
                           waking, modes, combat, …) holding its entries, clause templates and event schemas (§5.1)
    clauses.js             Behaviour lines, filled from the clause templates at the department's depth, with
                           M4's module parts and slot, and a Centrepiece's line and strip by phase (§5.3)
    grid.js, vision.js     tiles, line of sight, walking distance; what you see, your ring
    path.js, clock.js      paths for taps; the clock's budget and reads (rules/clock.js has its turn step)
    roles.js               exhibit numbers from role and depth
    layout/                build kit, programmes, curation, analysis, validation, repair, fallback; the Intro's
                           stamp (stamp.js) and M4's validators (lv6.js, treasure.js, runner.js, lv-m4.js)
    rules/                 step.js (the only mutator); actions.js (the action table, §4.2); actors.js (actor
                           fields, costs, the scheduler); archetypes.js (archetype and modifier hooks, with one
                           file each in arch/ and mods/); options.js; causes.js (events and their causes, §3.4);
                           modules.js (the module form, its interpreter and the hooks the core asks, §5.4; one
                           file a module in modules/); surfaces.js (clouds, dust, and M4's fog and rain, §8.1);
                           ai.js (an exhibit's turn);
                           then one file per system (wake, noise, combat, specials, ranged, health, doors, …);
                           M4's progress.js (Certificates, the grade, the offer), perks.js (one file a perk in
                           perks/), hall.js, centrepiece.js, viewing.js, fixtures.js (reading any fixture) and
                           treasure.js
    text.js, text/         sentences keyed by event kind and cause, refusals, the recap; one fragment per
                           owner in text/, as in reg/ (§4.3)
    labels.js              labels, odds readouts, the strip
    packs.js, dept.js      the pack registry and shared species; a department visit's starting state
    populate.js            placing a department's exhibits, and the validators a populated plan must pass (M4)
    panels.js              the wall panels and their banners (M4)
    setup.js               the parts every state has, and a state for one room (tests, scenarios, arenas)
    run.js                 run structure: doors, sequencer, Stair Halls, the Exit (M5)
    viewmodel.js           derive(state, ui) → a plain description of what to draw (§6.1)
  js/view/                 RENDERERS: read the view model, never the state
    views.js               the renderer registry and fallback
    topdown.js             TopDownView, Canvas 2D: the reference
    board3d.js             Board3DView, three.js: optional (§6)
    art.gen.js             sprite grids and palettes, generated from art/ by tools/build-art.js
    sprites.js             sprite canvases per palette, and the 64×64 close-ups (Scale2x twice)
    pictograms.js          pictograms as 8×8 shapes, drawn as SVG or on the board
  js/ui/                   DOM: top bar, strip, label panel, doors, halls, menus, settings, captions; bag.js,
                           the Bag (your kit and tote, quickslots, aiming); teacher.js, the Orientation's steps
                           (pure: it reads the view model and events, never the state); coach.js, its coach line
  js/input.js              pointer and keyboard → intents (§7)
  js/store.js              autosave, the visitor's card, the archive (§4.4)
  js/audio.js              synthesiser and tracker (M6)
  js/main.js               boot and wiring
  content/                 department packs, one file each (S9.js, A4.js, M11.js), and each pack's parts by
                           owner (<acc>-intro.js, -cp.js, -panels.js: its Intro stamp, its Centrepiece and its
                           panels, M4); shared species in no department (watch.js, the Night Watchman); the

    build-art.js           art/ → js/view/art.gen.js; art-sheet.py renders a contact sheet
    core-context.js        loads the core into a hardened vm context for tests and tools
    lint-core.js           the determinism lint on acorn's syntax tree (§3.3); vendor/acorn

    lint-registry.js       ids, caps, Behaviour-line coverage, ledger coverage

    battery.js             the M3 sim battery: monkeys, arenas, wander and soaks, every check after every

    hash-compare.js        the Node side of the hash page's check of pending runs (§3.5)
    replay.js              replays: where they start, playing them, the golden runs' player (§3.5, §3.6)
    shrink.js              crash cards: ddmin over an action list, the state's JSON-path diff (§10.2)
    core-hash.js           coreHash and vmHash (§3.6, §6.1)

    stats.js               the statistics of §10.3; plain Node, never loaded into the core
    layout-sweep.js        the 1,000-seed layout sweep (§11, M1), with the Intro's measures (§5.5); --validators sweeps the departments' validators (§5.5)
    dept-p95.js            what Dept.create costs: its p95 in CPU-ms over 420 departments, against its budget (§10.2)
    sim.js, report.js      headless bots and paired comparisons; reports from their output, with replays
                           chosen by rule and crash cards, and the linked-build check (§12)
    arena.js               archetype arenas: the stamp format, building one for a seed, judging its goal (§10.3)
    policies/              the bots: lib.js (what a policy sees, its helpers, the registry), cards.js, a file
                           per arena, wander.js (the battery's department visitor), README.md (generated
                           from the cards)
    hash-template.html     the cross-browser hash page (§3.5)
    remove-3d.sh           reverting the 3D view (§6.3); revert-test.sh, the revert test; vendor-three.sh
    pool.js                check-all's pool and step timer (§10.2)
    browser/*.mjs          Playwright scripts: smoke, screens, clicks, the keyboard alone, a finger alone
                           (scenes.mjs: the scenes those three share, then M4's scene files, scenes-m4-*.mjs),
                           the Orientation by each input alone
                           (check-orient), kills, 3D checks, the phone strip's metrics (check-lines), a
                           long label read to its end by each input alone (check-longlabel)
  test/                    *.test.js, golden/ (expected hashes; retired.js, the retired runs and their
                           builds; recipes/, how replays are made), behaviour/ (scenarios, invariants and
                           _harness.js), arena/, sweep/ (the checks m4-sweep.test.js runs over one shared
                           build of departments), run.js (m3-battery.test.js is check-all's short sims),
                           timings.json (generated: how long each test file and pool job takes, §10.2)
  dist/accession.html      the single-file build (committed, as elsewhere in this repo)
  check-all.sh             every lint and test; the gate before each commit
  DEVELOPMENT.md           milestone status and the validation log (§12)
```

**Rules for the layers:**
- `js/core` may not read anything outside `js/core` and `content/`.
- `js/view` reads only the view model and art.
- `js/ui` reads the view model and calls `text.js` for sentences.
- Only `main.js` and `input.js` call `step()`.
- A test loads `js/core` alone in a bare `vm` context with no `window`, which proves the first rule.

**Browsers:** current Safari (iOS and macOS), Chrome, Firefox and Edge. `dist/accession.html` runs from `file://` and from any static host.

**Hosting.** The canonical build is served by GitHub Pages from this repository's `main` branch (DESIGN default 42), including `dist/builds/` and `dist/accession-hash.html`; Julian switches Pages on once in the repository settings. Device checks, Home Screen installs and replay links use that URL. Until it exists, builds go to Julian as private Artifact pages.

---

## 3. The determinism contract

### 3.1 State and the one mutator

- **The whole game is one plain object, `state`:** `null`, booleans, strings, safe integers, arrays without holes, and objects with no `undefined` values (an empty curio slot is `null`). It holds no floats, functions, `Map`s, typed arrays or cycles. After every step, in every test run, a check confirms that the state is plain data of this kind.
- **Anything the core iterates is an array in a stated order** (actors in spawn order, modules in pack order, tiles in row-major order). The core never iterates an object's keys, so a state that has been through JSON behaves exactly like the original.
- **`step(state, action) → events` is the only function that changes `state`.** It mutates in place for speed and returns the list of events it caused. Bots that need to branch clone the state with `State.clone()`, a JSON round trip. `step()` never saves; `main.js` does (§4.4).
- **Free actions** (examine, open the label panel, look at the map) never call `step()`. Reading a label, which can identify a consumable kind, is a 0-tick action through `step()`, so the knowledge it grants is in the state and in the replay.
- **Rendering, sound and animation timing** live outside the state.

### 3.2 Random streams

- **Derivation.** A stream is named by a path string. Its seed is `hash32(runSeed + '/' + path)`, and its generator is mulberry32, as in `prototypes/layout/proto.js`.
- **Storage.** The current 32-bit state of every stream that outlives one call is stored in `state.rng[path]`, created on first use, so a snapshot resumes every stream exactly. Layout and loot generation run in one call, so their streams are local and never stored. On leaving a department, its `combat/`, `ai/` and `module/` streams are deleted. When an actor is destroyed, its own `combat/` and `ai/` streams are deleted (after its `onDied` effects, in `Combat.die`, §8.1), so Night Watchman returns and splits do not grow the table. A soak test checks that `state.rng` never holds more than 150 keys.
- **Integer draws only.** The draws are `int(lo, hi)` (by modulo, whose bias is below 10⁻⁷ at the sizes used), `chance(permille)`, `d100`, `pick`, `shuffle` and `weighted`.
- **Streams are keyed per purpose and per actor**, so removing one exhibit or switching off one rule does not reshuffle anything else. That is what lets "does this matter?" be answered by rerunning the same seeds with one thing switched off:

| Path | Used for |
|---|---|
| `doors` | the door sequencer |
| `seals` | the per-run seal shuffle |
| `deck` | the consumable deck |
| `layout/<acc>@<pin>/d<depth>/a<k>/{knobs,embed,openings,aspace}` and `…/a<k>/room/<roleKey>/curate` | layout, local; k is the reseed attempt. This extends `design/layout.md` §12.1 with the depth |
| `…/a<k>/room/<roleKey>/populate` and `…/a<k>/staff` | what is placed in one room, in this order: its cases (each case's glass, then lock, then whether it is on loan), then its items (each one's place, then a weapon's, garment's or instrument's tier; in a treasure's hall, the treasure case's lock, drawn for its overridden glass, in place of the treasure's place, M4, §5.5), then its consumables; staff-door grades, in row-major order; local. One holder serves the whole department while it is made, so a room's stream runs on from the cases to the items to the consumables rather than starting again for each. The port replaces the prototype's department-wide `/populate` stream |
| `…/a<k>/items` | which of the pack's weapons and garments, then which of its curios and instruments, are placed (each list shuffled; from M4, of those no gallery list names, §8.5), then which room each such item, each Restoration kit and each consumable goes in; local. Added in M3 (§3.2 had no department-level placement stream), for Julian to confirm |
| `place/<acc>/d<depth>` | the stand-in placement of exhibits (`Populate.exhibits`), for a pack with no gallery list; local |
| `…/a<k>/room/<roleKey>/exhibits` | M4's population: the tile each exhibit of the room's galleries takes, in the list's order, one draw an exhibit (a mimic: each spacing level's tiles shuffled in turn, the first taken that keeps the placement rules as the case it becomes and whose disguise, with those of the mimics before it, cuts nothing off); a swarm's further bodies, a Wader's round, a station's line, what a warden guards and the Intro stamp's marks draw nothing; local (M4) |
| `…/a<k>/weather` | which cases hold weather, and which of those fog and which rain: two draws a real case (none for a mimic's disguise), in the fixtures' order, whether it holds weather (`WEATHER_SHARE_PCT` in 100), then fog (`WEATHER_FOG_PCT` in 100) or rain; drawn whether or not `weather_cases` is on, so paired runs share their rooms; local, on a holder of its own (`weather_cases`' init, given the attempt's key by `Modules.init`; M4) |
| `perks/<acc>/d<depth>` | the perks a Certificate offers (§8.7, M4): which of a family's perks fill its slots, where it has more than its slots, one pick at a time; local, so the run's seed, the department, the depth and the perks you hold give the offer, and the state stores nothing |
| `combat/<actorUid>` | every hit and damage roll made *by* that actor |
| `ai/<actorUid>` | every choice that actor makes, including ties between equally short steps, and a mimic's disguise (its case's glass, lock and loan, drawn as a real case's are) |
| `module/<moduleId>/<acc>/d<depth>` | one module's own draws |
| `cosmetic/…` | renderer only, derived from the layout key, never stored and never read by the core |
| `policy/<name>/<seed>`, `arena/<id>/variant`, `bootstrap/…` | the tools' own: a policy's choices, an arena's variant for a seed, a bootstrap's resamples (§10.2, §10.3). Each is on a holder of its own, never in the state, so none of them moves a game stream |

- **`…/a<k>`** is the layout attempt that made the plan (k from 0; a certified fallback counts as attempt 5), and a department's placement streams are named under it. From M4 an attempt is populated as soon as its plan passes layout's checks, and then checked by every validator a rule registers (`Populate.addValidator`, §5.5); a plan its gallery list cannot fill (`population`) or a validator's rejection fails the attempt, as a failed layout check does, and the next attempt starts afresh.
- **Role keys** name rooms without dots: `intro`, `s1`, `s2` … (section galleries in tour order), `study1` …, `centre`, `coda`, `staff`.
- **Actor uids are stable names, not indices:** `<acc>.<depth>.<roleKey>.<n>`, n counted from 1 in each room in placement order, for example `M11.3.s2.4`. The player is `you`. A body created mid-run (a splitter's half, a summoned trolley) is `<parentUid>/<k>`, with k from the parent's counter (`kids`, from 1); the Night Watchman is `<acc>.<depth>.watch.<k>`; the Exit's recalled exhibits are `exit.7.<k>`.
- **Item uids are stable names too,** since replays carry `take(uid)` and `use(uid)`: `you.w` and `you.g` for the weapon and garment you start with; `<acc>.<depth>.<roleKey>.i<n>` for an item placed in a room, n counted per room in placement order; `<parentUid>/<k>` for a stack split from another, with k from the run's counter (`run.items`).
- **Removing an exhibit** (`without`, §3.6) marks it `gone` after every exhibit has taken its uid and its tile and before anything else is placed; a removed exhibit keeps its tile for placement, so no uid, tile, stream or item of anything else changes. A body made during a visit that `without` names (a Night Watchman, or a splitter's half by its `<parentUid>/<k>`) is made and at once marked `gone`, with no event; a splitter still loses that half's share of its health and counts it, so every later half keeps its uid.
- **`<pin>` is the layout version.** A layout code change bumps it. It is 3 from M4, whose Intro grows to its stamp (§5.5); version 2 was M1's. Within M4 the version stays 3 while the packages that build it change its plans: version 3 is not released until M4's acceptance, no replay or save holds a plan of it, and each such change sets the golden department runs pending. So S.9's plans changed without a bump when D-PACKS edited its Intro stamp (the low cabinets of its stretch row, §5.5), and again when D-ITEMS curated a treasure's hall (§5.5), A.4's and M.11's staying as they were byte for byte (decided in M4's build). From M4's acceptance, a layout code change bumps it again.

### 3.3 What the core may not do

`tools/lint-core.js` reads each file in `js/core` and `content/`, and, from `check-all.sh`, `tools/arena.js`, `tools/monkey.js`, `tools/replay.js` and `tools/policies/`, which run beside the core in the same hardened context (the monkey and the replay also on the hash page, which has no hardening), with string literals, template text and comments removed, so "Long-Handled Date Stamp" in a label or `'window'` as a knob value is fine. It fails on:
- the identifiers `Date`, `performance`, `crypto`, `setTimeout`, `setInterval`, `requestAnimationFrame`, `window`, `document`, `globalThis`, `localStorage`, `indexedDB`, `navigator`, `fetch`, `Intl`, `parseFloat`, `WeakRef` and `FinalizationRegistry` (whose timing is the garbage collector's);
- `Math.X` for any X outside `imul`, `abs`, `min`, `max`, `floor`, `ceil`, `trunc` and `sign`, and any other way of reaching `Math` (`Math[…]`, aliasing `Math`);
- the members `localeCompare`, `toLocaleString`, `toLocaleDateString`, `toLocaleTimeString`, `toLocaleUpperCase`, `toLocaleLowerCase`, `toFixed`, `toPrecision` and `parseFloat` (so `Number.parseFloat` too), however reached, a destructured one included;
- a call through `.constructor` (which reaches the Function constructor from any function);
- the `**` operator;
- `.sort()` and `.toSorted()` with no comparator, with `undefined` or `null` for one, or with one that returns a comparison (`(a, b) => a > b`, true or false) rather than a number, whose order differs between engines, and `for … in`;
- in a policy file (`tools/policies/`, one that calls `Policies.add`, not `lib.js`), state of its own: a `let` or `var` outside a function (or in a function called where it is written at the top), or a `const` there holding an object, an array or a `new` one that is not frozen, since the sim loads the file once and shares it between runs (§10.2).

`WeakMap` and `WeakSet` stay allowed: they cannot be iterated, so nothing can depend on their order (the invariants use one as a cache).

**Integer helpers** live in `js/core/int.js` and state their rounding: `idiv(a, b)` (floor division), `ceilDiv(a, b)`, `round10(num, den)` (to the nearest 10, halves up) and `pct(a, p)` (`idiv(a × p, 100)`). Rule arithmetic uses them rather than `/`. The plain-data check in §3.1 is the backstop for anything that slips through.

**Order.** `Array.prototype.sort` is always given a comparator that breaks ties on a stable key. Actors are only ever appended during a department, never removed or reordered (a dead one is marked `gone`), so array order is spawn order; they are compacted when you leave.

### 3.4 Events carry causes

- **Every event is a plain object:** `{k, turn, tick, …, cause}`. Every rule makes its events with `Causes.emit(state, ev, k, fields, cause)`, never by hand: it stamps the turn and tick, copies the fields and the cause (so nothing outside the state reaches into it), adds the event to the list `step()` returns, keeps it in the cause records if its kind is recorded, and then calls M4's module hook (`Modules.onEvent`, empty in M3). A field or cause value that is not plain data (NaN, an infinity, a fraction) stops the game with an error when it is emitted, rather than being copied as null; a field left undefined counts as absent. The same holds for the causes an actor keeps in its story.
- **`cause` says why, in data:**
  - a wake event names the trigger, the source and the distance;
  - a damage event names the attacker, the attack, the hit chance, the roll, the damage roll, the Padding and the result; whatever made it, its cause may also say `saved` (an invulnerable run kept you at 1 HP) and `side` (it came through an `old_repair` exhibit's repaired side);
  - a module event names the module and the rule within it.
- **`text.js` turns events into sentences.** No sentence about the rules is written anywhere else.
- **Each actor keeps its own story:** `woke: {turn, cause}` for its most recent waking and `hunted: {turn, cause}` for when it began hunting you, set where those happen. An actor created awake (a patrol, the Night Watchman, a summoned body) gets `woke` at its creation, with the cause `awake`, `patrol`, `night_watch` or `summoned`. Of an exhibit a room or a department starts awake (`Setup.settleActors`), a patrol (a `patrol_guard` with a round, `route.posts`, M4) gets `patrol`, told "it walks its round"; otherwise only one whose trigger stands for the wake clause (`awake`) gets `awake`, told "it never sleeps"; any other, which a room for tests or an arena stamp's `mode=` made awake but which can sleep, gets `placed`, told "it was set out awake when the room was made". Neither has an event: a log-only reader takes them from the starting state. The death recap ("what killed you, why it was awake, and when it woke") reads them from the killer, so it never depends on how much else happened since.
- **Every event kind has a cause schema** in the registry (its required fields and their types). `Causes.check(e)` checks an event's fields against its kind's schema and its cause against its cause's, allowing no field outside them, and names the first problem (`damaged.cause.roll: missing int`); `moved`, `closed` and `refused` have their fields checked but not their cause. A test runs it on every event of monkey runs in the test room and the three departments, and checks that no sentence from `text.js` contains "undefined", "NaN" or "[object".
- **The state also keeps the last `CAUSE_RECORDS` (64) cause records,** for the recap's narrative.
- **Sim runs can log every event in full.**

### 3.5 Hashes and the cross-engine check

- **The state hash** is a 32-bit FNV-1a over canonical JSON (sorted keys, no whitespace).
- **Golden runs** live in `test/golden/golden.js` (as JavaScript, so a page opened from `file://` can load them). Each is a seed plus a policy or an action list (a 10,000-action monkey run, and one replay per milestone) with the expected final hash and hashes at fixed checkpoints. M3's acceptance added `m3Replay` (309 actions of the wander policy on M.11 at depth 3, seed 116, with a use and a bash put in at their first chance: it applies a Restoration kit, breaks a case, hears the bell, opens a case, and dies to the Night Watchman in the night watch; retired when layout version 3 changed its department, it plays in M3's build, `dist/builds/7aeba871.html`, to its pin and its story) and one 1,000-action monkey per department at depth 3 (`m3MonkeyS9`, `m3MonkeyA4`, `m3MonkeyM11`). A run is one of two kinds: a monkey, `{kind: 'monkey', seed, actions, make}`, which plays that many monkey actions in the test room (`make: null`) or a department (`make: {dept, depth}`), folding a checkpoint in every 1,000 actions; or a replay, `{kind: 'replay', start, options, actions}` (§3.6), whose checkpoints are the state's hash after every 100th action and whose hash is its final state's. `node tools/golden.js --add <name> <file>` adds one, pinned, or unpinned and pending; the file may hold a golden entry, a replay as the tools write it (a replay without `kind` is read as a replay), or a crash card holding one.
- **Clone-stability.** `test/golden.test.js` plays every golden run three times: twice continuously, and once with `state = State.clone(state)` after every step. The three must give the same checkpoint hashes, and a pinned run must give its recorded ones. When a play throws, or the second or the cloned play differs, the test names the first step where it happens and prints the crash card (§10.2) of the visit that step is in. Beside the department golden runs (M3's acceptance), `test/m3-backbone-dept.test.js` also plays a 300-action monkey (every action kind a player has, weighted, the one the golden runs use) in each department at depths 2 and 5 (the seeds too), with your health 999, once continuously and once with a clone after every step. It compares their hashes every 10 steps (a difference, once there, stays) and checks plain data after every step; a difference is played again step by step, so its failure names the first step that differs, its action and events, and the first field of the state that differs. A scenario can ask the harness for a clone check too (§10.2).
- **Pending runs.** While a stretch of work changes the state on purpose (in M3, each wave of the build; in M4, the stretch to each checkpoint), a golden run may carry `pending` with its reason. A run is pending by name, `node tools/golden.js --pending "reason" <name…>`, so the runs a stretch does not move stay held to their pins (in M4 the test room's monkey is never pending, which the golden test checks); a name that is not a run of the file is refused. A pending run is still played three times and must agree with itself and with its clone, but it is not held to its pin, and **its story is still checked** (M3's replay's, and a recipe's, below). The hash page prints `PENDING <name>: hash H, checkpoints …` for it, and its title is PENDING when every pinned run passes; `tools/browser/check-hash.mjs` then plays each pending run in Node through `tools/core-context.js` on the same tree and passes only if the page's hash and every checkpoint equal Node's, so the check that browsers and Node agree never lapses. A re-pin (`node tools/golden.js`) ends the stretch: it writes the new hashes with no `pending`, and the log records the old hash, the new one and why. Only that bare command re-pins every run (`--record` pins only the run it records, once its story holds, and `--retire` only the run it retires, in its build): `node tools/golden.js --help` prints the usage, and an option or word the tool does not know, or two of `--add`, `--against`, `--pending`, `--check`, `--retire` and `--record` at once, is refused with a message; neither writes anything. `--check` also checks every recipe's story and plays every retired run in its build. Julian's browser rows stay "pending" while any run is.
- **Retired runs.** A run that a change retires on purpose (M3's replay, retired when layout version 3 changed its department) moves to `test/golden/retired.js` with `node tools/golden.js --retire <name> --build dist/builds/<coreHash>.html`, the release build it plays in (§3.6). The tool names that build's core from the build alone: its scripts, paired in order with those of the `index.html` git has where the build was added, must hash to the coreHash it is stamped with. The run is pinned by playing it in that core, and the record keeps its definition, that pin, and the build (its file, its coreHash and the path of each of its scripts up to its core's last), so the golden test, and `--check`, play it in the build's own core with this tree's monkey and replay player, with no git, and hold it to its pin, and M3's replay to its story there too. M3's five runs are kept: the golden test fails on one that is neither in the golden file nor retired. Release builds are kept, in every copy of the tree the tests run in (the revert test's included).
- **Recipes.** A replay may be made from a recipe, `test/golden/recipes/<name>.js`: where it starts (a carry included, §3.6), its switches, a script that gives its next action from the whole state (and may ask a policy of `tools/policies` for one, on that policy's own stream for the start's seed, as `tools/sim.js` plays it), a cap on its length, and the story the replay must show, in words. `node tools/golden.js --record <name>` records it twice on this tree (the two must agree), asks its story, and writes it, pinned, or pending with a reason (`--pending "reason"`; a run pending before keeps its reason); a story that does not hold writes nothing. A landing that changes what a recipe's replay does records it again in its landing commit, and its story must still hold. The golden test checks the story of every run with a recipe at every commit, pending or not, and fails on a recipe whose run is not in the file.
- **Finding a divergence.** A golden run also stores a hash after every step (4 bytes a step, in base64, as `steps`), written at each re-pin. On a mismatch, Node (the golden test and `node tools/golden.js --check`) and the hash page play the run again against those hashes and print, in the same words, the first diverging step, its action, and the events of that step; the hash page shows them as a copyable text block with a Download button, so Julian can send it back. A pin without per-step hashes can name only the first diverging checkpoint. A re-pin prints the first step at which the new hashes leave the old ones, and `node tools/golden.js --against <commit>` plays each run on this tree and on that commit's core (its files from git, in a temporary directory) and prints the first step at which they differ, with each side's action and events, and the first fields that differ when the starting states already do. `check-hash.mjs` also loads the page with planted runs (a replay with one action changed, a monkey whose pin has one step changed) and passes only if the page names each one's first diverging step in Node's words, and its Download button saves them; among them is a replay with switched options, pinned in Node, which the page must pass, and the same replay pending, whose hash and checkpoints on the page must equal Node's. It also checks that the hash page and the game carry this tree's `coreHash` (and the game its `vmHash`).
- **Where they are checked:**
  - Node runs them on every `check-all.sh`;
  - `build-single.py` also writes `dist/accession-hash.html`, with the core and the golden runs inlined; Chromium runs it through Playwright;
  - Julian opens the hosted copy (§2) in Safari on the Mac and the iPhone, and in Firefox, once per milestone. It prints PASS, the first diverging checkpoint, or PENDING with its hash. This environment has only Chromium; WebKit cannot be installed here.

### 3.6 Replays and counterfactual reruns

- **`coreHash`** is a hash of the source of `js/core` and `content/`, except `viewmodel.js`, stamped into each build. A change to the UI does not change it. Sim output also records `vmHash` (§6.1). Both are FNV-1a, written as 8 hex digits, over each file's path and text (line endings as `\n`), in path order (`tools/core-hash.js`); `build-single.py` stamps them into the game's head (`<meta name="accession-core-hash">`, `accession-vm-hash`) and onto the hash page.
- **A replay is** `{coreHash, layoutPin, start, options, actions}`. `start` is the run seed, or a snapshot where logging restarted. Until M5 builds the run, a start is `{seed, room: 'T0'}` (the test room), `{seed, dept, depth, carry?}` (one department visit, whose visitor is `carry`, `Dept.create`'s `options.carry`: `'median'`, `'start'` or `{grade, perks, tier, items}`, the median visitor when absent, §5.5) or `{state}` (a snapshot, which keeps its own options), and a tool may register another (an arena's) with `Replay.starter`. A carry on any other start is refused, so it is never silently dropped. `tools/sim.js --carry` gives a department run its visitor, and the run's replay keeps it in its start, so the replay rebuilds the visit alone. `node tools/replay.js <file> [--log] [--inv]` plays a replay file (a sim run's from an arena too, its room rebuilt by `tools/arena.js`, whose hash it compares with the one the start records), or a crash card holding one, and prints what happened. `options` holds:
  - `firstRun` (which adds the Orientation room);
  - `rulesOff` (switch off a rule by id: a modifier in M3, a module from M4);
  - `without` (remove an actor by uid);
  - `sequence` (pin the departments).

  These are the hooks the paired bots and the reports use. A visit keeps its own switches in `state.options` (`Options.make`: `rulesOff`, `without`, and `invulnerable`, the sims' and tests' option that keeps your HP at least 1, §10.2), and a rule asks `Options.off(state, id)`.
- **Replay length.** The save record carries the run's replay so far (§4.4). Loading a save in a build with a different `coreHash` starts a new replay from the loaded snapshot, and the death label then says that the replay starts at turn N.
- **A replay link** encodes the replay in the URL fragment. Opening it in any build with the same `coreHash` plays it back with a scrubber.
- **Release builds** (one per milestone and every slice-gate build) are kept as `dist/builds/<coreHash>.html` (`python3 tools/build-single.py --release`), so old replays keep working.

---

## 4. State, actions, events and saves

### 4.1 The state, in outline

```
state = {
  v, coreHash, runId, seed,             save format version, core hash, run id, run seed
  rng: { path: uint32 },
  tick, turn,
  options: { rulesOff, without, invulnerable },   the visit's switches (§3.6)
  run: { grade, seals, knownSeals, deck, items,
         floor, slot, depth, sequence, offered, certificates, perks, collectionPicks },
  dept: { acc, pin, depth, programme, w, h, tiles, rooms, roomOf, ltour,
          entrance, exits, lectern, viewTile,
          clock: { B, G, stage, count, pausedSince, nightBells, watch: { k, back } },
          fixtures: [ case | staff | plaque | lectern | sheet | panel ],
          surfaces: [ { kind, x, y, size, until, by } ],
          highlights: [ { uid, room } ], planRead, entryGrade,
          modules: [ { id, …moduleState } ],   in pack order
          walked,                       a department visit's rooms you have stood in, by id (M4)
          cert, offer,                  this visit's Certificate, {how, turn}; a perk offer waiting behind another choice, [{id, rank}] (M4)
          view,                         the views of the Centrepiece in the current row, {n, turn}: their count and the last one's turn (M4, §8.7)
          perk,                         the perks' own parts, each absent until its perk first writes it: `duck` and `sure`, true while Duck and Cover's discount, or its surprise attack, waits; `closed`, Close the Distance's free steps toward each exhibit, [{uid, n}]; `filed`, the exhibits Single File has counted, by uid, in order; `wind`, Second Wind's {turn, total} once it has come (M4, §8.7)
          seen },                       the map as you last saw it: one character a tile, ' ' where never seen
  actors: [ actor ],                    in spawn order; actors[0] is you
  items:  [ item ],
  you:    { kit: { weapon, garment, curio: [null, null], instrument }, tote: [ uid … ] },
  causes: [ last 64 cause records ],
  over:   null | { k: 'died' | 'left', turn, by, door, cause },   the visit's end
  ui:     { pendingChoice }             a seal, perk, door or hall choice waiting for an answer, {k, options}
}

actor = { uid, sp, arch, role, mods, modParams, trigger, group,     copied from the species at spawn
          size, x, y, hp, maxHp, acc, eva, pad, dmin, dmax, speed,
          next, notBefore, mode, pips, stim, pipTurns, sawYou,
          home, target, lastKnown, guards, route, plan, facing,
          statuses: [ { id, until, turns, from, perm? } ], telegraph, acts,
          woke, hunted, kids, gen, gone, ax }

item   = { uid, kind, ref, where, form, tier, level, prims, charges, max, rechargeAt, ck, seal, n, from }
case   = { k: 'case', x, y, room, num, glass, lock, hits, st, loan, holds, mimic, weather? }
staff  = { k: 'staff', x, y, grade }
plaque = { k: 'plaque', x, y, ck }
lectern = { k: 'lectern', x, y }                  (M4, §8.7)
sheet  = { k: 'sheet', x, y, of }                  (M4, §8.7: of, the piece whose footprint tile it covers)
```

**A part M4 adds is absent from a state until the rule that owns it first writes it, and its absence reads as its default** (`null`, `false` or `[]`), through its owner's accessor, never directly (`Progress.cert(state)` and `Progress.waiting(state)`, `Dept.walked(state)`, and the Centrepiece's, viewing's and the perks' own). A status carries `perm` only when it lasts for the rest of the visit (`Status.apply` with turns `'perm'`: no boundary ends it, and it ends with the visit). So the test room, which meets no M4 rule, is untouched by M4's parts, and `dept.walked` is a department visit's alone: it holds the room you start in, and after each of your actions `Step.step` calls `Dept.walk(state)`, which adds the room you stand in, once, in the order entered.

**Every state has every part,** made by `Setup.base` (a department's by `Dept.create`, a room's for tests, scenarios and arenas by `Setup.room`), with plain-data defaults, and with your health your grade's (§8.1), so a room made at a grade above I gives you more. A room for tests is given exactly what it holds: an item given at the tote or in a slot is carried or worn (a weapon or garment taking the place of the one you start with), an exhibit's statuses, target, last-known tile and `sawYou`, and your statuses, are as given (statuses settled as the room is made), and a field `Setup.room` does not know, a slot given twice, an item of the wrong kind for its slot or a status not in the registry is an error, never silently left out. A room that is no department may name itself (`place`, kept as `dept.place`: the Orientation's "practice room", §8.9), which only sentences read; no department has one.
- **The run:** `grade` (I at the start), `certificates` (each Certificate earned, `{acc, depth, how, turn}`, M4), `perks` (the perks you hold, `[{id, rank}]`, in the order you took them, M4), `seals` (null until the run's seals are shuffled, then `{sol: [4 kind indices], eph: [4]}`), `knownSeals` (those identified), `deck` (the cards left of each kind, in `DECK`'s order, full at the start), `items` (the counter for split stacks' uids, §3.2); the rest is the run structure of M5.
- **The department:** its rooms keep their id, role key, role and number; `clock` (none in a room for tests unless asked): `stage` 0 while open, 1 after the bell, 2 in the night watch, `count` the clock turns since entry, which a sealed hall stops, `pausedSince` the turn a hall sealed (M4), `nightBells` the night-watch bells rung so far, and `watch`, with `k` the Night Watchmen so far and `back` the count at which the next enters; `fixtures`: the cases and staff doors in row-major order, then each fixture added after them in the order it was added (while the department is made, a mimic's disguise, the lectern once its Centrepiece is placed, then the identifying labels, and then the wall panels, `{k: 'panel', x, y, key, room}`, §5.2; during a visit, the dust sheet's, §8.7); `surfaces`, in the order laid (§8.1); `highlights`, each Highlight's uid and room (in M3 the two Restoration kits, §8.5); `planRead`, the turn a Floor Plan was read here, or null; `entryGrade`, your grade when you came in; and from M4 `cp`, the Centrepiece and its hall (absent until the Centrepiece is placed, and read through `Hall.cp`): its pieces' uids, its hall's room key, its openings `[{x, y, ch}]` (staff doors included; `ch` the tile there, or the tile under its shutter while the hall is sealed), its form, the hall's own parts, `sealed` (null, or `{turn, by, why, at, last, seen}`: when the shutters closed and what called for them, the turn of the last boundary that judged the seal, the turn from which its quiet turns count, and what your memory held at each opening as they closed, §8.4, §6.1), `waiting` (`{turn, by}`, present while a seal waits on an occupied opening) and `lifted` (`{turn, why}`, present from the shutters opening until you next stand outside the hall at a turn's end), and the Centrepiece's own `touched` (`false`, or `{turn}` once a treasure's guardian's case was first opened or bashed, from which its treasure's seal counts, §5.5), `sheet` (null, or `{turn, uids}` once a completed view has put it under its dust sheet: that turn and the pieces it covered, §8.7) and `treasure` (null, or `{turn}` once its treasure was carried out of its hall, §5.5); and from its placement (`Centrepiece.place`, §8.7) `at` (its footprint's top-left tile, where a fight's Signature piece drops), `signature` (its Signature piece, the id of an item of its pack; absent with none) and `gains` (the special each phase gives, by phase, `null` for none; absent when no phase gives one); and the Ordinance's `hook` (the uids of the originals destroyed outside its hall that its gallery hook has counted, in order; absent until the first counts, §5.5, M4 ENSEMBLE-PHASES); and from M4 `perk`, the perks' own parts (absent until a perk first writes one, §8.7, and read through `Perks.charges`): Duck and Cover's `duck` and `sure` (true while its discount, or its surprise attack, waits), Close the Distance's `closed` (`[{uid, n}]`, the free steps taken toward each exhibit this visit, in the order first taken), Single File's `filed` and Second Wind's `wind` (above).
- **An item:** `kind` (weapon, garment, curio, instrument, kit or consumable); `ref` (its pack item's id; `restoration` for a Restoration kit; null for the kit you start with); `where` (`{at: 'floor' | 'case', x, y}`, `{at: 'tote'}`, `{at: 'kit', slot}` with slot weapon, garment, curio0, curio1 or instrument, or `{at: 'gone'}`); a weapon's or garment's `form`, or which instrument an instrument is (`fork`, `pin_driver` or `pole`); a weapon's, garment's or instrument's `tier` (1–4, drawn from depth when it is placed, never from the pack, §8.1); `level` (Restoration levels; a curio's rank); `prims` (`[{id, cls}]`); an instrument's `charges`, `max` and `rechargeAt` (the turn at whose boundary it next gets a charge back; null when it is full); a consumable's `ck` (kind), `seal` and `n` (count); `from`, the department it was first taken in; and a Lamp's `lit` (an `observing` curio, §8.5), present only while it is lit.
- **A case:** `room` (its room's role key), `num` (its accession number: null only for a mimic's case, which is its tell), `glass` (1–3: Plate, Laminated, Security), `lock` (the grade it needs, 1–7), `hits` (bashes taken), `st` (`shut`, `open`, `broken`, `dissolved` or `revealed`), `loan` (on loan elsewhere: a label with no object), `holds` (an item uid or null), `mimic` (the uid of the mimic it is, or null); and from M4 `weather` (`fog` or `rain`: the weather it holds, given as the department is made by `weather_cases`, §5.4; absent from a case that holds none, and never changed during the visit: once the case is opened, broken or dissolved its weather has got out, and the case is no longer said to hold it).
- **You** start with a Balanced weapon (`you.w`) and a Standard garment (`you.g`) equipped, T1 at a run's start and in a room for tests, and in a department of the visitor's tier (§5.5: the median visitor's at its depth, unless the carry says otherwise), and your derived stats are those `Kit.derive` gives for what you wear (§8.1).

**Tiles change only in these ways,** and a test watches every tile through monkey runs: a door opening or closing (`+` and `'`, `S` and `s`); a case opened or dissolved (`v` to `u`), broken (`v` to `k`), or a mimic revealed (`v` to `.`); a mimic's disguise (`.` to `v`, only when the department is made); shards (`.` or `,` to `x`); from M4, a hall's shutters closing over its openings and opening again (an opening's tile to `Z` and back), and a Centrepiece's dust sheet (its footprint, floor or shards, to `f`, §8.7). So no rule ever writes over an exit, the entrance, a bench, an opened or broken case or a wall. A shutter, `Z`, is not walkable and blocks sight, and a noise passes it with `NOISE_DOOR` extra, as through a closed door.

**Actors** are made by `Actors.spawn(state, rec)`, which appends one with every field set to plain data (`null`, never `undefined`):
- **From the species,** at spawn: `arch` (an exhibit with none is a plodder), `role`, `mods`, `modParams` (a modifier's own values, such as `old_repair`'s side), `trigger` and `group` (from M4's population, a copy group's instance in its room, `<group>@<roomKey>`, M4 plan DE5). Rules read the actor, never the pack. `speed` is its archetype's or a modifier's (§8.1). An exhibit a room for tests names by a pack or shared species takes that species' archetype, role, modifiers, `modParams`, trigger and group unless it gives its own, so its label never says what it is not.
- **Waking and modes** (§8.2): `mode`, `pips`, `stim` (the turn of its last stimulus), `pipTurns` (the turn each trigger last added a pip), `sawYou` (it has seen you since it last woke), `home`, `target`, `lastKnown`, `guards` (the tile a tethered exhibit guards).
- **Scheduling** (§8.1): `next`, and `notBefore`, the earliest tick of its next action after it marks a special, which only the scheduler reads and clears.
- **The story** (§3.4): `woke` and `hunted`; `kids` and `gen` count the bodies split from it and its generation (each split moves both halves one generation on); `gone` marks an actor destroyed or removed, which is never taken out of the list during a visit.
- **`ax`** is plain data that only its archetype's file reads or writes, except `ax.specials` (the specials it has, in the order it gained them) and `ax.rot` (`{last, at, woke}`: the special it marked last, its `acts` then, and the turn of that waking), which `Specials` reads and writes too (§8.1), `ax.perform` (`{pips, turn}`: the pips a station had when the chime woke it, and that turn, M4), which `Wake.perform` and `Wake.resleep` write (§8.2), a Centrepiece's piece's `ax.phase` (its Centrepiece's phase, which its pieces share, §8.7; `Specials.cadence` reads it), and `ax.copyOf` (the uid of the original a Centrepiece's piece copies, M4: absent until `CopiesWeaken.addGroup` writes it, and read by `copies_weaken` alone, §5.4); `acts` counts its actions since it last woke, the one it is taking included; `telegraph` is its pending special, `{ sp, tiles, from, via, gross, effect, turn, tick }`: the marked tiles in row-major order, each once and fixed when shown, and for each the tile its damage comes from and the reflector that sent a beam back before it reached that tile, or `null`; with `offYou: true` for a damage mark made off your tile and `due` (a turn boundary's tick) for a due line, each absent otherwise (§8.1).
- **Kept for M4, always `null` in M3:** `route` (set by M4's population: a Wader's round, `{posts, i}`, the tiles it walks between in order and the one it walks to next; a station's line, `{dir}`, N, E, S or W, which a station placed with none, in a room for tests, takes from its archetype's init by population's own rule, §5.5), `plan` (M4: a clockwork exhibit's next move, as its arrow shows it, §8.1: `{k: 'step', to}`, `{k: 'strike', at}`, `{k: 'mark', sp, tiles}`, `{k: 'resolve', sp}` or `{k: 'wait'}`; chosen after each of its actions and when it wakes; a station's arrow is its line, worked out when shown, so its `plan` stays `null`) and `facing` (there is no facing, §5.4).
- **You** carry the same fields, and also `still` and `lastAct` (your last action that cost time sets your ring, §8.2), `seated` (on a bench), `regen` (the regeneration store), `ringMax` (present only while a free or cheaper action has run since the last turn boundary: the largest ring of your actions since then, §8.2) and your derived stats (`weaponForm`, `reach`, `quiet`, `breaker`, `acc`, `eva`, `pad`, `dmin`, `dmax`), which `Kit.settle` keeps up to date at the end of every step.

**The snapshot stores the generated layout itself,** not only its seed (about 1,000 tiles: a few kilobytes). A save therefore resumes correctly even if the layout code has changed since.

**Size.** An actor has `size` 1 or 2 (a Centrepiece is 2×2), anchored at its top-left tile. Distance, adjacency and line of sight use the nearest of its tiles; it is observed if any of its tiles is in sight; it moves only where all its tiles are free.

**Scheduling.** Sleeping exhibits are not scheduled: they act only once woken, and waking sets `next` to now plus one action (§8.1).

### 4.2 Actions

**One small vocabulary**, the same for people, bots and replays:

| Kind | Actions |
|---|---|
| Moving | `move(dir)` (8 directions; into an exhibit it is an attack, into a closed door it opens it); `wait`; `rest` (one turn of a repeated wait); `close(x,y)` (a door beside you) |
| Fighting | `attack(uid)` (beside you, or 2 tiles away with a Reach weapon) |
| Cases | `open(x,y)`; `bash(x,y)`; `push(x,y,dir)` (reserved: cut from M4, after the gate, Q7) |
| Items | `take(uid)`; `drop(uid)`; `equip(uid)`; `unequip(slot)`; `use(uid[, x, y][, how][, on])` (an instrument at a tile, the Window Pole with `how` push, pull or take; a kit on an item; a consumable); `throw(uid,x,y)` |
| Reading and viewing | `read(x,y)` (any fixture with a reader, 0 ticks); `view` (one turn of the Centrepiece viewing, M4) |
| Places | `sit` (on a bench); `leave(i)` (on exit tile i) |
| Choices | `choose(index)` (a seal, a perk, a door, a hall offer, an accession) |

**The action table** (`js/core/rules/actions.js`). Each action kind is defined once, by the file of the system that owns it: `Actions.define(id, {legal, candidates, apply})`, where `legal(state)` lists the legal actions of that kind, `candidates(state)` a superset with illegal ones, and `apply(state, action, ev)` returns the cost in ticks or a refusal code, checking everything before it changes anything. The kinds are closed, in the order `wait, rest, sit, move, attack, close, open, bash, push, take, drop, equip, unequip, use, throw, read, view, choose, leave` (`Actions.ORDER`); `push` is reserved and has no handler, so `step()` refuses it as unknown; `view` is defined in `rules/viewing.js` (§8.7): it is legal only on the lectern's reading tile while the Centrepiece stands, is not under its dust sheet and sleeps, every piece of it, and the visit has no Certificate yet, and is refused, in this order, `no_centrepiece` (none was placed: every department until its core is ready, and the test room), `sheeted`, `already_earned`, `no_centrepiece` (none of it stands), `not_at_lectern` and `not_asleep`. A pending choice is answered by `choose(index)`, defined in `actions.js`, which hands it to the function defined for its kind (`Actions.defineChoice`) and costs what that function says (choosing a seal costs nothing: reading the Catalogue Card was the cost; nor does choosing a perk, §8.7); the choice is cleared unless the function asked a new one, and once none waits, `Progress.afterChoice` raises an offer that waited behind it (M4). `read(x, y)`, defined in `rules/fixtures.js`, reads the fixture on that tile through its kind's reader (`Fixtures.addReader(k, fn)`, registered from the reader's own file: the plaque from `rules/consumables.js`, M4's lectern and panel from theirs), which gives the words read and what reading does, or a refusal; every read is within `READ_RANGE` (a disc) and in your line of sight (`label_far`), and a tile with nothing readable is `label_none`. `Actions.free(kind)` names the kinds that cost no time: `read`, `choose` and `leave`.

**Paths are a UI feature.** A tap on a distant tile becomes a sequence of `move` actions, issued one at a time and stopped by the interrupt rule (§7.1), so a replay never contains a path.

**Illegal actions are refused before they cost anything:** `step()` returns a single `refused` event with a reason code (`corner`, `behind_glass`, `rooted`, …), and neither the state nor any random stream changes; `Text.refusal(code)` gives its sentence. `step()` refuses, in this order: when the visit is over (`visit_over`); when a choice is waiting and the action is not `choose` (`choice_pending`); an action with no handler (`unknown_action`); one a status forbids (`Status.blocks`: while fixed you may only wait, rest or answer a choice already waiting, `fixed`; while rooted you may not make a move that would change your tile, `rooted`, though bumping a door, an exhibit or a case, which leaves you where you are, still works, so a bump into a case is refused as glass whether you are rooted or not, as one into a mimic in its disguise is); one a refuser refuses (`Actions.addRefuser(fn)`: a rule's own refusal, such as M4's shutters' `sealed`, a step into a shutter or a take of an item lying under one, and the dust sheet's `sheeted`, a view, a blow at a piece it covers, a step into it or an instrument aimed at it, and, for a treasure's guardian, an opening, bash or take of the case it guards, an instrument aimed at that case or a Solution thrown onto or beside it (§8.7), registered from that rule's file and asked in the order added, the first code winning; a refuser only reads); and whatever the action's handler refuses.

**`Step.legal(state)`** lists every legal action: each handler's legal actions in `Actions.ORDER` that no status forbids and no refuser refuses (only `choose`'s while a choice waits, and none once the visit is over). A test asserts that `step()` refuses exactly the actions it leaves out, over every handler's candidates, and monkeys draw from it.

### 4.3 Events

Event kinds (`k`):
- **Movement:** `moved`, `opened`, `closed`.
- **Fighting:** `attacked`, `missed`, `damaged`, `died`, `telegraphed`, `special`.
- **Health and statuses:** `waited`, `regenerated`, `healed`, `status`.
- **Waking:** `noise`, `pip`, `woke`, `hunting`, `alerted`, `lost`, `returned`.
- **Cases:** `case_opened`, `glass_cracked`, `glass_broke`.
- **Items:** `took`, `dropped`, `equipped`, `used`, `identified`.
- **Clock and alarms:** `stage`, `alarm`.
- **Progress:** `certificate`, `grade`, `perk`, `viewed`.
- **Centrepieces:** `phase`.
- **Modules:** `module`.
- **Places and system:** `entered`, `left`, `refused`.
- **Surfaces:** `surface`.

The list is closed and lives in the registry (`Registry.EVENT_KINDS`); `tools/lint-registry.js` fails if it and this list differ. `alerted` sends an exhibit that is not hunting to look at a noise, or, when its search ends and it may not go back, or other actors block its way home, to wander (a patrol, M4, back to its round); `healed` is any gain of health but regeneration; `status` is a status put on or ended; `surface` is a surface laid (a plaster cast's dust, cause `died` by the cast; a thrown Fumigant's cloud, cause `item`), with its kind, centre, side and turns, so the log says why sight is blocked; unequipping is `equipped` with no item; `phase` (M4) is a Centrepiece entering a phase, with the piece, the phase, and the Composite's stage or the special gained where they changed, its cause damage (`damaged`), or, within the phase it is in (entering none, its `phase` the one it is in), galleries walked (`walked`: the Composite grew a limb), the phase it entered (`limb`: the Composite grew its next limb, told after that phase's own event), your leaving its hall while it hunted you (`left`, naming the limb it dropped) or a fallen piece's special taken on (`inherited`); `module` is one rule of a module acting, with the module's id and the rule's name, its cause keyed by the module's id. Each kind has a schema (its fields and their types: uid, uids, int, str, bool, tile, tiles, obj, with `?` for an optional field), and so does each of its causes, `Registry.defineEvent(k, …)` and `Registry.defineCause(k, causeK, …)`, in the fragment of the rule that emits it (§5.1).

**Sentences** are keyed by kind and cause, because many rules emit the same kind for their own reasons:
- `Text.define(k, fn)` gives a kind's default sentence, `Text.defineCause(k, causeK, fn)` the sentence for one of its causes, and `Text.defineRefusal(code, fn)` a refusal's, each in the owner's fragment `js/core/text/<part>.js`; `Text.provide(name, fn)` supplies the clock dial's sentence and the stage chip's words (§7.2; `Text.clock`, `Text.stage`), the death recap, the confirmations' words (§7.1; `Text.confirm`, in `js/core/text/confirm.js`) and a long rest's line (§7.1; `Text.rest`).
- `Text.event(e, nameOf)` uses the cause's sentence, and falls back to the kind's.
- **Words a sentence uses:** `Text.count(n, noun)` writes a count as it is read ("1 turn", "3 turns", never "1 turns"), and `Text.grade(n)` a grade in Roman numerals, as the badge prints it ("Grade IV", §8.7); no sentence prints a registry id (a module's, a perk's, a department's), a perk being named by its name in `Registry.PERKS` and a module by its rule's own sentence.
- `Text.log(events, nameOf, visible)` chooses what reaches your log: it leaves out steps, closed doors, the announcement of a blow (its hit or miss is logged), waits and regeneration, and pips, statuses, doors and module events of exhibits out of view (an event about no exhibit is always told, so a module event of a rule about an exhibit that names none has no sentence and is never told, and a joined-up scenario's sentence check fails on it); an exhibit that wakes straight into a hunt gets one sentence; exhibits of one name that stir alike in one step (a swarm's bodies) get one sentence, which says how many more did the same; and events that one step makes for one reason, whose kind and cause have a group (`Text.defineGroup(k, causeK, fn, key)`, by the cause's owner: an alarm waking every sleeper that can hear, a chime waking every station, an original's spares losing heart), get one sentence where the first of them came (the group's key gives the reason they share, as the original whose spares they are, or null for an event told alone, as an exhibit made awake among those the alarm woke); and an event that a later event of the same step tells as well (`Text.toldBy(k, causeK, fn(e, f))`, by the cause's owner: a station's line marked at the chime, which its mark's own sentence tells with its tiles, damage and turns) is left out whenever that later event is told, so the log tells it once; and an event a rule tells only in view (`Text.seenOnly(k, causeK, fn(e))`, by that rule's owner: a station's chime) is left out while the actor it is about is out of view (M4's first integration review).

lint-registry fails if a kind or a (kind, cause) pair has no sentence. A test checks that every `damaged` and `woke` event has a non-empty cause.

### 4.4 Saves

The design in [`DECISIONS.md`](DECISIONS.md) (Autosave) is the contract. The mechanics:
- **Only play mode saves.** `store.js` is used by `main.js` in play mode only; replays, the hash page and tests never open IndexedDB or localStorage.
- **After every `step()` that returned at least one event,** `main.js` has `store.js` serialise the state and write it to IndexedDB, in one transaction, into the older of two slots.
- **The record** is `{runId, seq, v, coreHash, savedAt, checksum, payload, replay}`:
  - `seq` is device-wide and never decreases (kept in the meta store);
  - `checksum` is the FNV-1a of `payload`;
  - `replay` is the run's action log so far (§3.6);
  - `savedAt` is display only.
- **Writes are coalesced:** at most one routine write is in flight, and the next carries the newest state.
- **The same record is mirrored to localStorage.**
- **It is written again on `visibilitychange` to hidden and on `pagehide`,** started at once even while another write is in flight (IndexedDB orders the transactions).
- **Meta changes go with their snapshot.** Changes an action causes in the meta store (an accession, a Catalogue entry) are written in the same IndexedDB transaction as the snapshot that contains that action.
- **`navigator.storage.persist()`** is requested after the first completed department, with a sentence saying why.
- **On load,** every slot in both stores is read. The record with the highest `seq`, a valid checksum and a loadable `v` wins. If it is not the newest record, the game says so in one sentence ("The last move before the page closed could not be recovered; you are one turn earlier.").
- **Death is a tombstone, not a deletion.** One IndexedDB transaction writes `{runId, seq, dead: true}` to both slots together with the meta update (the death label, the record). The death label is shown only once that transaction completes, and localStorage is cleared afterwards. On load, a tombstone outranks every record of its run, so a stale localStorage copy can never bring a dead run back.
- **No manual save or load.**
- **Versions.**
  - A save from an older `v` is migrated by a listed function or retired with a sentence naming the version.
  - A build that finds a record with a newer `v` than it knows leaves storage untouched and says "This save belongs to a newer version of the game."; it can still play replays.
  - The meta store (Collection, Catalogue, record) has its own version and migrations, and is never retired.
- **The visitor's card** is a short text code of the meta store (compressed, base64url, with a checksum), shown on the Entrance Hall screen with Copy and Paste buttons.
- **The archive** is a JSON file download of the meta store plus the current snapshot. Import reads both.
- **If IndexedDB is unavailable,** localStorage alone is used, and the settings screen says so.
- **Installable.** `dist/accession.html` carries the `apple-mobile-web-app-capable` and theme metas, an apple-touch-icon and an inline manifest (`display: standalone`). The canonical build is hosted at one URL (GitHub Pages, DESIGN default 42), and the persist() sentence and Settings explain Add to Home Screen, which keeps Safari from deleting the save after seven days without a visit. M5 checks the metas; Julian's iPhone checklist covers an installed launch.

---

## 5. Content: the grammar registry and the department packs

### 5.1 The registry

`js/core/registry.js` is the one machine-readable grammar. It holds:
- the **nine universal systems**, each with an icon id and its one sentence (§8);
- the **slice subsets** of triggers, archetypes, modifiers, specials, statuses, surfaces, item primitives, instruments, fixtures, glasses, wake causes, refusal codes and modules;
- the **8 consumable kinds**, their seals, and (M4) the **8 perks** (`Registry.PERKS`: each perk's name and challenge family, §8.7; `Perks.add` registers the rules of an id defined there and no other);
- the **event kinds** and their schemas.

**Fragments.** `registry.js` holds only the systems and the API; every entry lives in a fragment, `js/core/reg/<part>.js`, owned by the package that implements and tests it (`reg/waking.js` for the triggers it builds, `reg/arch-b.js` for the kiter, and so on), so no two packages edit one file:
- `Registry.define(table, id, entry)` adds an entry; each id has one owner, and a second definition throws;
- `Registry.defineEvent(k, schema)` and `Registry.defineCause(k, causeK, schema)` add event and cause schemas (§4.3);
- `Registry.order(table)` gives a table's ids in definition order, so the core never iterates an object's keys (§3.1).

**Every entry carries:**
- `built`: `'M3'` or `'M4'`, the milestone that builds it (§11);
- `ready`: `false`; `'unit'` once its scenarios pass with the other systems' hooks stubbed or driven directly; `true` once the joined-up scenarios pass;
- `tests` (scenario files under `test/`) and `inv` (its invariant file, `test/behaviour/<inv>.inv.js`);
- for an id with a clause: its parameters (`params`), its Behaviour-line clause template (§5.3), the event kinds the clause accounts for (`covers`), and its pictogram.

`tools/lint-registry.js` fails the build when:
- there are more than ten universal systems;
- an id is unknown or duplicated, or (from M3) a `built: 'M3'` id, or (from M4) a `built: 'M4'` id past `ready: false`, is referenced by no pack, scenario test or arena fixture. A pack references the archetype, trigger and modifiers of each of its species, that archetype's special, inflicted statuses and wake clause, its role's clause (if the role has one), the modules it holds and those its species are flagged with, its items' primitives, instrument (with an instrument's `charges`) and weapon or garment clause, and the consumable kinds it labels; a scenario test references an id when one of the files its entry names in `tests` names it in quotes (`'id'` or `'TABLE.id'`); an arena fixture references one when a stamp in `test/arena/` names it as a word. So an id only the core reaches (a refusal code, a glass, a seal) is referenced by the scenario that makes the core reach it;
- a pack uses an id not in the registry, or an item primitive outside §8.5's set;
- a Behaviour-line clause has no test: an entry at `'unit'` or above names no tests, or a test or invariant file that does not exist, or an entry at `true` names no joined-up scenario file (`test/behaviour/*.test.js`); with `--strict` (M4's acceptance, §11), any `built: 'M3'` or `'M4'` entry is below `true` or lacks its tests or invariant (`--strict=M3`, M3's acceptance, checks M3's entries alone, and `--strict=M4` M4's); with `--strict --follow-ups` (the joined-up scenarios' done condition), such an entry passes while `test/behaviour/follow-ups.json` (`{"TABLE.id": "the follow-up"}`) holds an open follow-up for it, and a follow-up for an entry that is at `true`, or is not an entry of a milestone `--strict` checks, fails;
- a refusal code at `true` is made to happen in none of the `test/behaviour/refusals*.test.js` files its entry names (each owner's `refusals-<owner>.test.js` makes its codes happen by a real action at depths 1–7, as `refusals.test.js` does M3's): none has a case with that code (`code: '<code>'`; a code named only in a comment or a list is not made to happen);
- **the word rules**, over every template a player reads: the clause of every entry past `ready: false`, every module's panel sentence (shown on its panels and in the log while the module is on, whatever its readiness), and every clause on the Behaviour line of a species that population can place (its archetype `built: 'M3'` or past `false`), whatever that clause's own readiness (a placeholder no one reads waits for its owner; `--strict` applies them to every entry it checks): a `built: 'M4'` template has a digit outside `{}`, since a number is a parameter filled from its row, or a number in words that no tied phrase says ("twenty turns", "twice", "up to four", "one limb": a number word from two to a hundred, "twice", "double", "thrice", "triple", or "one" before a rule noun); or a template says a phrase that states a number in words ("two tiles a turn", "half speed", "two halves", "double damage") without tying it to the row it states, in its entry's `phrases` (`{phrase: 'N:ROW'}`; an M3 entry's ties are in the lint, its fragment being frozen), or the row no longer holds what the phrase says (a `_PCT` row its share, any other its count; a phrase of an M4 template's own, "two galleries", states the number its number word names);
- a clause template names a parameter, special or status its entry does not define, or a pack species' line cannot be filled at some depth 1–7;
- an event kind or a (kind, cause) pair has no schema or no sentence, or the kinds differ from §4.3;
- a species has more than one special trigger;
- a department owns more than two modules, or a species is flagged (`flags`, the modules whose rules it carries) with a module its own department does not hold (a shared species with any), or a module's `keywords` (the words that name its rule) are not a list of words;
- a pack's Intro teaches anything but its signature module (from M4, which builds the modules and the Intro's panels; M3's lint does not check it). The signature is the module with `role: 'signature'`, else the pack's first. The Intro fails when its intro panel says another module's panel sentence or one of its keywords, or its stamp places an exhibit flagged with another module (the teaching instance too, whose line would then state that module's rule: so M.11's Intro has a station of its own, flagged with the chime alone, §5.5), a weather case (unless `weather_cases` is the signature), more than 3 exhibits, or one that is neither fodder nor a skirmisher nor the signature's teaching instance (flagged with it) (design/layout.md §11.1);
- an image or vendored file is missing from the asset ledger.

### 5.2 What a department pack holds

A pack is data: `content/S9.js` registers one object with `Packs.add({…})`, and each part that has its own owner is given by a file of its own after it: the Intro stamp (`content/S9-intro.js`, `Packs.part('S9', 'intro', {…})`), the Centrepiece (`-cp.js`, `'centrepiece'`) and the panels (`-panels.js`, `'panels'`). A part is given once, to a pack already added; `Packs.get` and `Packs.list` give each pack with its parts merged in, and `Packs.sprites()` lists every record drawn with a sprite (species, Centrepieces, their pieces and attachments, and the shared species; a Centrepiece of pieces is drawn as its pieces alone, so its record is not listed, M4) for the art test. It holds:
- **The card:** accession, title, family, challenge claim (primary and secondary types), house rule, and placard blurb.
- **The programme and its knobs,** the Intro stamp (an ASCII map with a legend), and the gallery list with role keys: `galleries: [{key, where?, name, contents}]` in the sample's order (M4). `key` is `intro`, `centre` (the hall), `coda`, `last` (the sample's study room) or `s1`, `s2` … (its section galleries, in order); a section gallery's `where` names a programme element, a tag the plan gives its rooms (`central`, `band`, `row`). `contents` lists exhibits in placement order, `{sp, count?, case?, near?, guards?, dir?, apart?}`: `count` how many (a swarm counts swarms); `case: true` in an empty case of its room; `near: 'case'` beside a real case; `guards: 'case'` for a tethered warden; `dir` (N, E, S or W) for a station's line; `apart: true` for a herald standing out of call of every exhibit (CP1 review); and items, `{item, case?}`, for the item placement. The stamp's legend names an exhibit by `{sp, guards?, dir?}`. `Populate.check(pack)` lists a list's faults (a key twice, a species or item not the pack's, a mimic in the hall or in a case, an exhibit more than one tile across (population places one-tile exhibits; a Centrepiece by its own rule), a spare whose gallery has not exactly one of its original, a copy group outside `COPY_GROUP`, an option on the wrong archetype; and in its stamp, an exhibit on a stretch row, which would stand there as often as the Intro is tall, and a copy group its marks make that a gallery could not hold: each spare with exactly one of its original, `COPY_GROUP` in all, counted on the stamp's map), and population refuses a pack with any.
- **The palette:** material, accent, wall and floor colours, checked against the reserved semantic colours.
- **Modules,** `[{id, role, params}]`: the role `signature` or `secondary`, and the module's parameters.
- **Species:** the archetype, at most two modifiers, at most one special trigger, a role, the skin (name, silhouette, sprite ids), the label (tombstone fields and a paragraph of 40–70 words; the M2 paragraphs taken from the samples are kept as written, nine of them shorter or longer, and `test/m3-content.test.js` pins which), and, where a rule reads them, a `group` (the exhibits an `ally_hit` trigger listens to), `modParams` (a modifier's own values, such as `old_repair`'s side), `flags` (the modules whose rules the species takes part in, each one its own department's: `Packs.add` refuses a foreign flag) and `copyOf` (the species of the same pack a spare copies).
- **Items:** equipment and the Signature piece, with their primitives and labels, each `{ id, kind, form?, instrument?, prims, name, tomb, paragraph }`: `kind` is `weapon`, `garment`, `curio` or `instrument`; `form` a weapon's (`light`, `balanced`, `heavy`, `reach`) or a garment's (`light`, `standard`, `heavy`); `instrument` one of `fork`, `pin_driver`, `pole`; `prims` a list of `{id, cls}`, a primitive with its magnitude class S, M or L. Every primitive must be one the slice implements (§8.5). An item has no tier: its tier is drawn from depth when it is placed (§8.1), and an instrument's charges and recharge come from its row in §8.10.
- **Local names** for the 8 seals, and identifying labels for the 8 consumable kinds: `consumables: { names: [8 local names, one for each seal: the 4 Solutions' seals, then the 4 Ephemera's], labels: { <kind>: paragraph } }`. Since the seals are shuffled every run, a local name never names a kind or a seal family, nor a thing that one of its pack's labels describes (its main noun is in none of them); a label (40–70 words) names its own kind, no other, and no local name, and says its kind's effect in words, never another kind's.
- **The Centrepiece:** form, core, attachments, phases, gateway label, and lectern position. Its attachments (`attachments`, M4: the Ordinance's Senior Countersigning Clerk, the Great Board's Repeater Arm and Board Dish) are species of their own, each `{id, sprite, archetype, role, modifiers, trigger, flags?, name, tomb, paragraph}` with where its core sets it out in its hall (the Clerk's `at`, the Board's `stands`, §5.5); lint-registry lints each as a species, and `m4-lines` holds each line whole to the strip's budget.
- **Panels** (M4, `content/<acc>-panels.js`, `Packs.part(acc, 'panels', …)`): `{intro: {name, text}, sections: [{gallery, name, text, module?}]}`, the intro panel and the section panels, each section panel naming the section gallery it introduces, and the secondary's naming the pack's secondary module (`module`). Their prose (from the samples' panels, trimmed) states no rule and says nowhere where anything stands (plan D5.5; `test/m4-panels.test.js` lints it: no number by a rule word, which `test/m3-content.test.js`'s lint finds in every pack string, panels among them; no module's sentence written in, and no word of the rule of a module its department does not hold, or on the intro panel of any but the signature, §5.1's Intro rule; no claim of a place, the samples' own planted). **What a panel says** (`js/core/panels.js`; plan D7.1, settled; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable): its prose, then, while its module is on, that module's panel sentence, generated (`Clauses.sentence`, §5.3), so the rule a panel states is the rule the code runs: the intro panel's module is the signature, a section panel's the one it names, and with the module off it says its prose alone. Its **banner**, which takes the strip's place on first entering its room until your next action (the interface's, M4's UI-MODULES; `Panels.inRoom`, `Panels.banner`), is its prose's first sentence and, while its module is on, the module's sentence: at most 50 words at every depth.
