It is read like a plaque (`read(x, y)`, free, within `READ_RANGE` and in sight), which gives its whole text and changes nothing (`FIXTURES.panel`). **Where each stands** (`Panels.place`, after the consumables; it draws nothing): a fixture on a wall by the first entrance of the room it introduces, the intro panel in the Intro and each section panel in a section, one a room. The secondary's panel stands at the first section in tour order where its module is met: one holding an exhibit flagged with it, or, for `weather_cases`, any real case, since any may hold weather (decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable; so neither the weather panel's prose nor its sentence says that the room it stands in holds weather; read from the flags and the cases, never from whether the module is on, so the panels stand alike under any `rulesOff`); none in its sections meeting it, in its gallery's room. Every other section panel stands in its gallery's room, or, with the secondary's there, in the first section in tour order that has none. So M.11's weather panel stands in its first section, which held a case in all 700 M.11 departments of the sweep (weather in it in 294), and the Line's in the next; S.9's Waders' Round in the court, where the Waders are. A room's first entrance is its tiles you reach first (walking as an exhibit does, staff doors walls, at the least walk from the entrance), and its panel is on a wall tile with no fixture beside a tile of the room, read from a tile of its first entrance (within `READ_RANGE`, in sight), preferring one beside no tile of another public room, then the nearest (where every wall readable there holds a fixture already, the identifying labels' by its doorway, the nearest wall beside a tile of the room you can walk to: 1 panel in the sweep's 2,100 departments); a section's by a doorway out of the Intro can be read from the Intro's side of it too. `test/sweep/panels.check.js` holds every claim here on the shared sweep's 2,100 departments. **For now panels are core-only** (decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable): their label (LABELS-M4, `Labels.ofPanel`), view-model entry (VM-2D), banner and reading scenes for the keyboard, a mouse and touch (UI-MODULES) are deferred, as the lectern's were, so nothing in the browser shows a panel or names one yet (`test/m4-panels.test.js` checks it).
- **The audio motif** (M6).

**The Intro stamp** (`intro`; layout version 3, `js/core/layout/stamp.js`) is `{map, legend, stretch, cut, invariants}`:
- **`map`:** the Intro drawn west to east, a string a row, north first. Each row is 1 + `INTRO_WIDTH` (15) characters: column 0 is the Intro's west wall, `#`, or `E` on the one row the entrance is in (the entrance row); columns 1–14 are its floor columns, so a column is the plan's x. A floor column holds a stamp tile (floor `.`, a closed door `+`, a vitrine `v`, low furniture `f`, a plinth `p`, a column `o`, shelving `H`, a partition `|`, a bench `b`, a balustrade `~` or a pool `w`) or a letter the legend names. Walls, exits, staff doors, the entrance and the lectern are the generator's.
- **`legend`:** each letter the map uses beyond the stamp tiles, as a record. The letter is written as the record's `tile` (a stamp tile), or as itself if it is a stamp tile, else as floor; every tile holding it is one of `plan.intro`'s marks, and what else the record says (the exhibit, case or item it stands for) is population's.
- **`stretch`:** rows, by index, repeated to fill the Intro's height. The Intro's rows north of its entrance row are filled by the stamp's rows north of its entrance row, its stretch rows there repeated as evenly as they can be (zero or more times, the northern ones taking one more first), and likewise to the south. A stamp that cannot fill a side exactly does not fit: the layout attempt fails LV4 and reseeds.
- **`cut`:** the letters of zone Z. Every walk from the entrance to an onward opening must cross a tile holding one (LV4's `cut(Z)`, `design/layout.md` §11.2: a walk with Z's tiles removed reaches no onward opening), so a partition drawn wall to wall in every row, stretch rows included, with Z its only gap, runs wall to wall whatever the Intro's height. A walk steps diagonally between two tiles that can be walked (§8.2), so a zone of floor drawn on a diagonal is crossed without stepping on it, and does not cut.
- **`invariants`:** the further predicates of §11.2, of which only `{near: letter, k}` is built: every such walk passes within k tiles (in steps, ignoring what stands between) of a tile holding the letter, checked as `cut` is with those tiles removed. Any other is refused, so a stamp never claims a check that is not made.

Every field is required and no other is read: a stamp that breaks any of this is refused with the first thing wrong (`LStamp.check`). A department whose pack gives no stamp, and a plan made without a pack, take the fixture stamp (`LStamp.FIXTURE`): a partition wall to wall at column 7 whose door on the entrance row is the cut, so the 6 columns west of it are the vestibule, and two free-standing plinths east of it. The plan gives the stamped Intro to population as `plan.intro`: its rectangle, the cut's tiles, the marks and its onward openings (`LStamp.shape`).

**Shared species** belong to no department: the Night Watchman (`content/watch.js`) is registered with `Packs.addShared`, and an actor's species is looked up in its department's pack, then its Centrepiece, then the shared species (`Packs.speciesFor`), so labels, the view model and the lint treat it as any other. A piece of a Centrepiece of pieces (an ensemble, M4) reads as its Centrepiece's record with the piece's own id and, where the piece gives them, its own name, sprite, tombstone and paragraph, so its label names it while its rules and Behaviour line are its Centrepiece's. An attachment of a Centrepiece (M4: the Ordinance's Clerk, the Great Board's Repeater Arm and Board Dish) is found by its own id, as the record it is, its rules and line its own.

**Numbers come from depth, never from the pack.** A pack names roles and magnitude classes (S, M, L), and the role curves (§8.10) turn them into numbers at the department's depth. A pack may override a number only through a named field the lint lists in the department's report, so every override is visible: `node tools/lint-registry.js --report <acc>` prints every number the pack holds (its parts merged) and every value it gives a module or a modifier (a module's `params`, a species' `modParams`), each with where it is.

### 5.3 Behaviour lines are generated

- **A label's Behaviour line is assembled from clause templates,** one part for every registry id the species uses (`Clauses.of(sp, {depth, rulesOff, modules})`; `Clauses.line` joins them):
  - the archetype's clause ("Walks one tile at a time and strikes from beside you for {dmg}.");
  - its special's clause and each status it inflicts, set inside the archetype's clause where it says `{special}` and `{status:<id>}`;
  - its role's clause, when the role has a rule of its own (`CLAUSES.role_<role>`): only the brute's, "Its blows take {COST_ATTACK_HEAVY} ticks, not {COST_ATTACK}." (§8.1); a role whose numbers only fill other clauses (`role:HP`, `role:DMG`, a curve) adds none;
  - a Centrepiece's form's rule, by its record's `form` (M4; `CLAUSES.cp_fight` or `cp_treasure`, §8.7): its phases and that they never go back, at each threshold or below, and what its form makes of it: a fight seals you in its hall with it while it hunts you there, and its fall drops its Signature piece where it first stood and earns the Certificate unless you have it; a treasure's guardian's death opens its hall and earns nothing (each form's entry also gives the short sentence its strip shows, below);
  - each modifier's clause, unless `rulesOff` switches the modifier off;
  - the wake clause ("Wakes when you stay inside your ring or make noise near it", or, for a species that gains no pips, "Wakes only when it is opened, bashed, dissolved or struck"), joined to the special trigger's clause; a trigger that stands instead of it ("It never sleeps.") replaces it;
  - each module the species is flagged with (`flags`, §5.2) that is on: the module's part, its registry entry's clause (`Registry.MODULES[id].clause`, filled from its `params`, with its own `special` and `inflicts` set inside it), after the modifiers (M4).
- **A rule a module gives an exhibit is written once, in the module's part** (M4; Q3, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). An archetype's clause holds only what is true with the module off. Where the module changes what the archetype's clause says, the clause holds a slot, `{module:<id>|<fallback>}`, set as `{special}` is: the module's part goes there when the module is on for the line, and the fallback, what the exhibit does without it ("{module:unobserved_motion|Walks a tile at a time}, and strikes …", the Grazer's; a number in words there is tied as anywhere, so not "one tile"), when it is off; a species flagged with a module its clauses have no slot for carries the part as a sentence of its own. A module is on for a line when its rules are defined (`Modules.defined`: until then nothing acts, and the slot reads its fallback), `rulesOff` does not name it, and the visit runs it: a label gives the visit's modules (`Modules.active`), so in a room without the module (a test room, the Orientation) the line says what the exhibit does there. So with its module off an exhibit loses its department rule, and its line says what it does instead: a Grazer walks, a Wader only chases, a station never strikes. lint-registry reads a clause in both forms: with every slot at its fallback, so the fallback's words meet the word rules, and with each module's clause in its slot, where the clause must still have its shape (a sentence, or a continuation) and the words must join (no word run into the next, no full stop inside a sentence, no space before a stop); a module's clause that a flagged species' clauses have no slot for must be a sentence of its own, and a module's clause holds no slot.
- **Parameters are filled at the department's depth:** `N:KEY` is a number of record, and `N:KEY[i]` one of its values; `curve:KEY` is a role curve at the depth; `cp:KEY` is a Centrepiece special's gross at the depth (`Specials.cpGross`, §8.1); `role:HP` and `role:DMG` are the species role's health and damage range at the depth; `mod:NAME` is a value the pack gives the modifier (`modParams`); `lock:GLASS[i]` is the locks a case of glass i + 1 can have at the depth (§8.3: its glass's range, never above the depth + 1), so a case's label never names a lock its department cannot hold; `spare:DMG` is the damage a spare of the species' copy group deals at the depth once it has lost heart (§5.4: each end of its role's damage less `COPY_WEAKEN_PCT`% of itself, at least 1 and never below 1; a spare's own role, or for an original its spares', the widest span if they differ; for a Centrepiece of pieces, its own copy group, the `centrepiece` role; M4), so a spare's line states the blow it is left with. So "strikes from beside you for 4–9" names the numbers a blow can roll.
- **Event kinds that need no clause** are the ones any exhibit may cause or suffer (`Clauses.UNIVERSAL`: moving, doors, noise, pips, waking and mode changes, damage and death); blows, specials and statuses are covered by the clauses of the archetypes that make them.
- **The panel shows it under the title,** above the literary paragraph, and the strip shows it in runs 1–3 (DESIGN default 24).
- **Length.** The longest line in the packs, with depth 7's numbers, must fit the strip at 390×844 at every text size and leave the board at least 40% of the window's height (`check-screens`); a clause that would break this is reworded, never cut. At the largest text that leaves room for 14 rows of a Behaviour line, the rows M11.1's 507-character line takes at depth 7 (the board keeps 341 px, against the 337.6 px it must; a row more takes 22.3 px). `test/m4-lines.test.js` (M4) fails, in Node, any gallery line, at any depth and with any of its modifiers and modules switched off, and any Centrepiece's strip in any of its three phases at any depth, with each of its modules and modifiers on and off, that takes more rows (a Centrepiece's whole line is held to no budget: its label shows it, and scrolls), judged by the strip's own metrics as Chromium measures them there: each character's advance and each pair's kerning in the strip's font, the box's width, and where Chromium breaks a line (after spaces, after a dash, after a hyphen not before a digit). The model gives Chromium's rows for every line measured, the closest calls among them, and the test fails when the strip's CSS changes or a line uses a character never measured. The metrics are this Chromium's for the strip's font (Inter, as system-ui here; a system whose system-ui is wider, as DejaVu Sans is, gives the longest lines a row more, and the board falls below 40%), so the browser check `check-lines` measures them again at every check-all and fails on any difference (`node test/m4-lines.test.js --measure` does so by hand, and `--write` takes a new measure into the test).
- **A Centrepiece's line by phase** (M4; Q10, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). A Centrepiece's record lists its `phases`, one record a phase, each naming the registry entries whose clauses join its line from that phase on (`parts: ['TABLE.id', …]`; a phase part's clause continues "From phase 2: …", lower case and with no full stop, in both forms of its slots, which lint-registry checks for every phase part a pack names, so a `CLAUSES` entry written for one is not a sentence of its own; a module's words come into it through a slot, so they go with the module, and a part that then says nothing, its module off and its slot's fallback empty, is not on the line). Its label shows the whole line (`Clauses.line`), each later phase's rules after "From phase k:"; `Clauses.phaseParts(sp, phase)` gives the parts that hold in a phase.
- **A Centrepiece's strip** (M4; the plan's amendment after ARCH-CP-CORE, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). A Centrepiece's whole line takes up to 23 of the strip's 14 rows at depth 7 (measured on its lines as the plan describes them; its form's rule alone takes 7), so its strip never shows it. In each of its three phases (one for its full health and one past each threshold of `CP_PHASE_PCT`, whatever its record lists) its strip (`Clauses.strip(sp, {depth, rulesOff, modules, phase})`, which throws without the phase) shows four things only:
  - "Phase k of 3.";
  - its form's short sentence, the `strip` of its form's entry, a sentence over the clause's parameters that lint-registry reads as a template: a fight's "Its fall earns the Certificate, unless you have it.", a treasure's "Killing it earns nothing: carry the Lamp out." (M4 has one treasure, S.9's);
  - what its line adds for that phase itself, its "From phase k: …" (phase 1's: what it has from the start) as a sentence of its own, with its modules and modifiers on or off as for its line, and nothing where the phase adds nothing; what an earlier phase added still holds, and is on its label;
  - "Its label has the rest.", true in every phase, since its archetype's rule, its form's whole rule, its earlier phases' parts and its wake are there.

  This departs from "reworded, never cut" for a Centrepiece's strip alone, which must fit in every phase as a gallery line fits whole; a gallery line's strip is its whole line (`Clauses.strip` gives `Clauses.line`). Its label's panel, which a long line overflows, scrolls with every input (§7.2).
- **A module's panel sentence** is its registry entry's `sentence`, a template over the same `params`, filled at the depth (`Clauses.sentence(id, {depth, params})`; `mod:NAME` reads the pack's params for the module); only the parameters it names are read, so one only its clause uses, which needs a species (`role:DMG`, `spare:DMG`), is left alone.
- **Each clause is checked four ways:**
  1. **An invariant.** Each clause has a predicate over the state before a step, its events, and the state after, in the invariant file its entry names (`test/behaviour/<inv>.inv.js`). The file exports `(C) => ({id, applies(actor, state), check(pre, events, post, ctx)})`, or a list of them, one per clause; `check` returns null, or a message saying what broke the clause. `tools/invariants.js` loads every file and checks each invariant after every step of every monkey, soak, scenario and bot run, once for each actor its `applies` accepts (an actor already gone when the step began is skipped; one created during it has no `before`); a violation prints the clause, species, seed, step and replay.
  2. **Scenarios** in `test/behaviour/`, written as functions of the clause's parameters and run for every species and depth that the three packs and the Exit produce, with pack overrides applied; a clause no species' line has (a status's, a surface's, a consumable's, an item's) is run at every depth 1–7 in a room of its own, and its scenario may name its entry `'TABLE.id'`, which takes the parameters of that table's entry. The scenarios that put an entry at `ready: true` run joined up: the room is made as a test makes it (its map, actors, items and statuses, what you know and where you stand), and from then on only real actions are played, in the real core with every rule in place and nothing stubbed; every invariant is checked after every step, and every event must pass its cause schema and read as a clean sentence. Beside them a file may keep unit-level tests that plant a hook or call a rule directly, to time or isolate it (an action of each length for the status timing table, an exhibit that only marks its ring), and tests that plant a fault to show that a check catches it; each says what it plants. Every refusal code M3 gives is made to happen by a real action at every depth 1–7 (`behaviour/refusals.test.js`).
  3. **Sensitivity.** Each module and modifier clause's scenario is rerun with `rulesOff`, and must then fail; the harness's sweep reruns every scenario of a modifier's clause so, and `test/behaviour.test.js` runs each M3 modifier's scenario files and fails unless they make such reruns. A scenario that shows what a modifier leaves as it is (marble's blows, the sides an old repair does not cover) holds with it off as well, and says so, with why, instead of being rerun.
  4. **Completeness.** Every registry id a species uses (archetype, a role with a rule of its own, modifiers, trigger, special, module flag, inflicted status) contributes a clause part (`test/behaviour.test.js` checks it for every species at every depth, and with each modifier switched off), and soak runs flag any event kind an actor causes that is neither universal nor covered by a clause of its line.

  Vague words are not allowed in a clause: "strikes hard" names its number class from §8.10. A Behaviour line therefore cannot say anything the code does not do.
- **Paragraphs carry no rules** (D10: "a paragraph that is only for reading"). A paragraph may echo, in the museum's voice, something its Behaviour line already states; it never adds a rule. The samples' Essential lines that describe systems the slice cuts (light, lamp cones) are rewritten as flavour. The paragraph lint (`test/m3-content.test.js`) checks every string in the three packs and in the shared species: a number, in digits or in words (hyphenated or not), next to a rule word (tiles or paces, turns, damage, %, pips, HP, Padding, Acc, Eva, charges, range or radius, rank or level, times, or a grade word), or a blow's damage ("strikes for 3"), fails, unless the species' own Behaviour line states the same number of the same thing, so the Brooder's "two paces from the nest" passes on its line's "2 tiles". Plain digits stay legal, as in "Round Book 3". The lint finds numbers, not every rule: a rule in words alone is caught where the test knows the rule, in each identifying label (its kind's effects, and none of another kind's) and in each item's paragraph (nothing its item does not do).

### 5.4 Modules: the event–condition–effect form

DECISIONS D2: the rules are fixed; invented rules wait behind opt-in doors. The slice still writes its modules in the small form that future invention would target, because the form is also what makes Behaviour lines and section-panel sentences derivable and checkable.

```
{ id, params, sentence,                          the panel sentence, a template over params
  rules: [ { on: <event or 'turn' or 'period'>,
             when: <predicate over the event, tiles, species, flags>,
             do: [ <effect> … ],                 a closed set: status, surface, wake, pips,
                                                 move, special, alarm, clock, tier, flag
             telegraph: <what is shown first, and for how many turns> } ],
  bespoke: <name of a hand-written function, or null> }
```

**When a rule does not fit the form,** the module uses `bespoke`, with a hand-written sentence and a scenario test. The validation log records which modules needed it, as evidence for D2's research track.

**The form as built** (M4's W0e, `js/core/rules/modules.js`). A module's words are its registry entry's: `sentence` and `params` (the panel sentence), `clause` (its part on a flagged species' line, §5.3), `covers`; a pack's values for it are its `params` (`modules: [{id, role, params}]`). Its rules register from its own file:

```
Modules.define(id, {
  rules: [ { rule,                                 its name, which its module event carries
             on: <event kind, 'turn' or 'period'>, every?, at?   a period: the boundaries where
                                                   turn − since ≡ at (mod every)
             who?: (state, e, m) → [exhibit…],     what it acts on (none: it acts once)
             when?: (state, e, m, a) → bool,       asked of each, just before it acts
             do: [ {k: <effect>, …parts} … ],      a part a value or (state, e, m, a) → value
             fields?, cause?,                      its event's at, n, turns, and more cause
             telegraph?: {turns, shown} } ],       data for the log and the words
  init?(state, pack, m, base),                     as the department is made, on or off; base the
                                                   layout attempt's stream key (none in a room for tests)
  bespoke: [ <name> … ],                           the hand-written parts, each hook among them:
  turn?, alarmDecay?, pipsDecay?, mayReturn?, threat? })
```

- **The effects,** each through the rule of record that owns it: `status` (Status.apply, for turns or `'perm'`), `surface` (Surfaces.add), `wake` (Wake.wake, with a wake cause, toward a tile; never a Centrepiece's piece, which wakes from pips and damage only, §8.2), `pips` (Wake.addPips), `move` (one step toward a tile as an exhibit steps, a closed door on the way opened as that step, its ties on the module's stream, costing it nothing), `special` (Specials.mark on its shape's tiles, always a due line at a boundary, §8.1: a mark resolved at its owner's next action is held past your next decision only when made in that owner's own action, and a rule acts in yours or at a boundary, so a module that marks gives `threat`, which warns of its lines before they go off), `alarm` (the module's alarm, `{by, since, until}`, and the alarm event), `clock` (ClockRules.advance), `tier` (§5.4's weakening: maximum and current health and damage each lose pct% of themselves, at least 1 and never below 1, and Acc), `flag` (a value in the module's state). A rule acts on exhibits, never on you. An unknown effect, part or kind, a special with no `due` or no `threat`, or a hook named `bespoke` that the module does not give, throws at `define`; an effect that cannot do what it says when it runs (an exhibit immune, one not asleep, a Centrepiece's piece to wake, no step to take, an alarm already running) throws, rather than leave its event to claim it.
- **A module's state** is its entry in `dept.modules`, `{id, role, on, since, params, …}`, made by `Modules.init(state, pack, base)` in pack order as the department is made, whether or not `rulesOff` switches it off (its init then makes its draws and discards them); `Dept.create` gives `base`, the layout attempt's stream key (`…/d<depth>/a<k>`, §3.2), under which a module's placement draws are named (`weather_cases`' `…/a<k>/weather`), and a room for tests gives none, so a module draws nothing there (MOD-WEATHER); a module whose rules no file has defined is left absent, so a department changes only as each module is built. It keeps turns, never counters.
- **Acting.** Modules act in pack order, rules in the order written, targets in the order `who` gives. A rule's module event (`module {id, rule, who?, at?, n?, turns?}`, cause `{k: id, …}`) comes before its effects' events. An event a rule makes goes through `Causes.emit` to the rules after it, but never a module event; a rule acting three deep throws. A module draws only on `module/<id>/<acc>/d<depth>` (`Modules.rng`). Once the visit is over, no module acts, nor on the event that ends it (the blow that leaves you no health, your death, your leaving). At boundary step 8 the due lines go off first (`Specials.resolveDue`), then each module's turn and period rules, then its `turn`.
- **What the core asks:** `Modules.on`, `active`, `flagged` (on, and the species flagged), `pipsDecay` and `mayReturn` (false while a module on says so), `threat(state, untilTick)` (every module's marks `{by, sp, tiles, gross, due}` falling due after now and before untilTick, each checked: anything else throws), `offFor(pack, met)` (M5's, §8.9), and `Modules.bespoke()` and `Modules.form(id)` for the validation log.

**The five slice modules:**

| Module | Department | The sentence the player is taught | Parameters (slice) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `unobserved_motion` | S.9 | "The animals move only when you cannot see them." | flagged species; 2 tiles a turn while unobserved |
| `alarm_network` | S.9 | "The waders raise the alarm when they see you; then every sleeping exhibit in the department that can hear, but the Centrepiece, wakes." (M4: the draft's "then the whole court is awake" claimed more than the call does, since the Centrepiece and a mimic sleep on) | 2 patrolling exhibits (the Waders); see below |
| `copies_weaken` | A.4 | "When an original is destroyed, its spares lose heart: each, and any body split from one, loses 33% of its health and damage and 2 Acc, and is slowed until you leave." (M4: the draft's "When an original is broken, its spares lose heart." said neither what they lose nor for how long) | groups of 3–4; see below |
| `hourly_demonstration` | M.11 | "On the chime, every station marks its line across its room, and two turns later the line strikes everything on it." | see below; every forced crossing has a window of at least `DEMO_WINDOW` (3) turns, checked by LV13 (§5.5) |
| `weather_cases` | M.11 | "Some cases hold weather. Break one, and the weather gets out." | about 20% of cases; fog (blocks sight, 10 turns) or rain (soaks a 3×3 for 10 turns; a soaked clockwork exhibit stops for 2 turns) |

**"Unobserved" is defined once:** the exhibit's tile is not in your line of sight within your sight range (`SIGHT_PLAYER`; a sight-blocking surface, a Fumigant cloud, plaster dust or M4's fog, breaks it as a wall does), nor, while you wear a lit Lamp, within its range (`LAMP_OBSERVE_RANGE`, §8.5): `Vision.observed`, which is what the board draws (§6.1) and the Lamp. You see all round (DESIGN default 7). There is no facing. An unobserved mover checks before each of its steps; it may end a step in view, and then it stops for the turn.

As built (M4, `rules/modules/unobserved_motion.js`, `rules/arch/unobserved_mover.js`; the Grazer's line, §5.3, says each part: "Moves {UNOBSERVED_STEP} tiles a turn, but only while you cannot see it (it takes no step in your sight, and stops for the turn once a step brings it into view), and strikes from beside you for {dmg}."), for an exhibit flagged with the module, while it is on, and awake:
- **The gate.** No step, and no door opened as a step, is begun while you can see it (its archetype's `mayStep`, which every walk of an exhibit asks). In your sight it does only what is not a walk: hunting, it strikes you from beside you, or loses you as any hunter does (§8.2); settling back in at home, it goes back to sleep in its time; what would set it walking home (an idle spell, a search done, being moved off its home) waits until you cannot see it, its turns still counted, so no walk is told as given up; then it waits for the rest of the turn.
- **The pace.** Out of your sight it walks as a plodder does (after you, to look, to wander, home), each step costing `TICKS_PER_TURN` ÷ `UNOBSERVED_STEP` ticks, rounded up, so no more than `UNOBSERVED_STEP` steps begin in any turn.
- **Into view.** A step that ends in your sight ends its turn: its next action comes no sooner than the next turn boundary.
- **The stride.** Each of its steps is told by a module event, rule `stride` (`who`, `n` 1), after the step. The log tells strides only of a mover in view at the end of your step, one line a mover ("The Long-Necked Grazer moved 2 tiles while you could not see it."), so what moved where you could not see stays untold until it ends in view.
- **Off** (`rulesOff`, a visit without the module, or a species not flagged with it), a Grazer walks a tile a turn, as a plodder does, in your sight too, and its line says so ("Walks a tile at a time, and strikes from beside you for …").
- **Its form** (§5.4): no rule fits the form (`rules` empty), and the validation log lists it bespoke: `UnobservedMotion.act` (the gate in your sight, the stride, the stop on coming into view), `UnobservedMotion.mayStep` and `UnobservedMotion.costPct` (the pace). The form's `move` adds a step, costing nothing, at an event or a boundary; it cannot keep an exhibit from its own steps, which is what this rule does.

**The alarm** (`alarm_network`). When a Wader sees you and no alarm is running, it telegraphs for one turn, then calls: a crash at its tile, and every sleeping exhibit in the department except the Centrepiece wakes awake, investigating your tile at the moment of the call (cause: alarm, and the Wader's uid). For `ALARM_TURNS` (30) turns pips do not decay, awake exhibits do not return, and no new alarm can be raised. Each alarm moves the next clock stage `ALARM_CLOCK_ADVANCE` (30) turns earlier, never earlier than the next turn boundary.

As built (M4, `rules/modules/alarm_network.js`, `rules/arch/patrol_guard.js`; the Wader's line, §5.3, says each part):
- **Shown.** A Wader (an exhibit flagged with the module, while it is on) hunting you that sees you, while no alarm runs and none is shown, marks its special `alarm` on its own tile: the telegraph, shown for a turn as every mark is and called off as any is (§8.1), and told by its name and the tile it marks ("… marks its own tile for its alarm, at its next action.": the special's entry gives `noun` and `marks`, which §8.1's mark sentences read). Only one is shown at a time; another Wader that sees you then only chases you.
- **The call,** at that Wader's next action: a crash at its tile (a noise, which stirs and wakes as any crash does, §8.2), then every exhibit still asleep that can hear (not pipless: never a mimic in its disguise; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable) and is no Centrepiece's piece wakes awake, wherever it is, in spawn order, sent to look at your tile as the call goes off (cause `alarm`, `by` the Wader; one in an intact case wakes and stays in it, §8.3, its cause `how: 'case'`, and the log says it woke in its case). The crash comes before the wakes, the exception to §8.2's order for one action: the other way round, the crash would send every exhibit the call had just woken within its reach to look at the Wader's tile, not at yours. Then the alarm is raised: the `alarm` event `{by, until}` (until = now + `ALARM_TURNS`), the module's state `alarm {by, since, until}`, and the clock brought nearer.
- **While it runs** (its turns since ≤ t < until): pips do not decay (`Modules.pipsDecay`), no exhibit goes back to its place for being left undisturbed or done searching (`Modules.mayReturn`; one already going back goes on, and a tethered warden still goes back when you leave its tether, its own rule, so the Wader's line names those two reasons and does not say "none awake or searching"), and no other alarm is shown or raised. At the boundary of its last turn it ends, and says so (rule `alarm_over`), and whether pips fade again (its cause's `fade`: not after the bell, when they never fade).
- **The clock** (`ClockRules.advance`, §8.4): before the bell the bell comes up to `ALARM_CLOCK_ADVANCE` turns sooner, and the night watch with it; after the bell the night watch does; never sooner than the next turn boundary; in the night watch nothing moves (decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). Its `stage` sentence says what moved, by how many turns, and these limits; the stage chip and the dial read the moved clock. A sealed hall stops the count, not this: raised while it is sealed (the cause's `sealed`), its sentence says the turns are the stopped clock's, counted once it starts again, and that the clamp is the clock's next turn.
- **Off,** a Wader only chases (§5.3).
- **Its form** (§5.4): rules `alarm_shown` (a `flag`: who showed it, when) and `call` (the `alarm` and `clock` effects, on the special's going off, telegraph `{turns: 1, shown: 'alarm'}`); bespoke, for the validation log: `pipsDecay`, `mayReturn`, `alarmDecay`, the call's crash and wakes (`AlarmNetwork.call`: the form's `wake` acts on one exhibit per module event, which would name each woken exhibit as its `who`, an exhibit whose line does not carry the module, §10.3), and the Wader's showing it (its archetype's `act`). Module events name only the Wader; the wakes are told by their own `woke` events, one line in the log.

**Weakened spares** (`copies_weaken`). Exhibits have no tier, so "losing heart" is stated directly: when an original dies, each of its spares, and any body split from them, loses `COPY_WEAKEN_PCT`% of its maximum and current health and of its damage (rounded down, at least 1), loses `COPY_WEAKEN_ACC` Acc, and gains the `slowed` status for the rest of the visit. The A.4 Centrepiece's surviving copies use the same rule. A scenario test checks it at depth 1, where A.4's Intro teaches it.

As built (MOD-COPIES, `rules/modules/copies_weaken.js`; the part on a flagged species' line, §5.3, says each piece: "When its group's original is destroyed, each spare loses {COPY_WEAKEN_PCT}% of its health and damage, never its last point (blows then {spare:DMG}), and {COPY_WEAKEN_ACC} Acc, and is slowed until you leave." A body split from a spare is the spare (§8.1: a half is the same exhibit, its label names it so), so "each spare" is each of its bodies, as the panel sentence says in full; the Trustee's line has no room to say it again, §5.3's length):
- **A copy group** is an original and its spares. Population makes them (§4.1): a spare is an exhibit whose species copies another of its pack (`copyOf`), and its original the exhibit of its group instance (`group`, one a room; the stamp's `clerks@intro`; in a pack with no gallery list, the species' own group) whose species is the one it copies, the first made. A Centrepiece names its own with `CopiesWeaken.addGroup(state, {orig, spares})`, which writes each spare's `ax.copyOf` (§4.1), at any time, before the module's state is made too (a Centrepiece is placed before `Modules.init`, §5.5's order); an original that is a spare, a spare twice or already one, a body split from another, or no exhibit is refused. A body split from another (a splitter's half) is the same exhibit, its uid `<body uid>/<k>`, so a line of bodies shares its first body's uid up to the first slash, and is a spare or an original as that body is.
- **Who takes part:** a group of species while both the spare's species and its original's are flagged with the module (§5.2's `flags`), so that every exhibit the rule acts on, and the one whose fall moves it, has a line that says so (§10.3's completeness: no module event names an exhibit, nor has a `by`, whose line does not cover it); a Centrepiece's group whatever its flags, its line being its own package's. A.4 flags the Seated Clerks and the Proposing Trustees, each original and its spares. To fit the Trustees' lines in the phone's 14 rows (§5.3's length) with the part, the splitter's, the brute's and `ally_hit`'s clauses were tightened (M4, MOD-COPIES' review; each rule as it was), and the Trustee's spare is not the sample's plaster cast (§5.5): with every rule kept, a cast's line took 16 rows.
- **`weaken`** (on `died`): an original falls when the last body of its line is destroyed (a splitting original falls with its last body; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable); one gone without dying (a dust sheet, `without`) never falls. Then each body present that copies it and takes part, in spawn order (each spare, and any body split from one), loses heart: its module event `{rule: 'weaken', who}`, cause `{k: 'copies_weaken', by: the original's first body}`; the `tier` effect (its maximum and current health and each end of its damage lose `COPY_WEAKEN_PCT`% of themselves, at least 1 and never below 1, so it is never destroyed by it; and `COPY_WEAKEN_ACC` Acc); and the `status` effect (`slowed`, `'perm'`, for the rest of the visit, cause `{k: 'copies_weaken', by}`).
- **`split`** (on `woke`, a body made by a split): a splitter's half is made with its body's numbers and no statuses (§8.1), so one split, after its original fell, from a body that lost heart is made weakened and is slowed too: its module event `{rule: 'split', who}`, then the `status` effect. It is not weakened again. The body it split from tells whether it lost heart: one that did holds `slowed` for the rest of the visit, which nothing else gives an exhibit, so a body split from the spare of an original gone without being destroyed (`without`, a dust sheet: it never fell) is not slowed. (Its first action is still the beat after it appears that `Wake.created` gives it, reckoned before it is slowed: the slowing, as any status, covers its turns from its next decision, §8.1.)
- **What is told:** both rules' events are about the spare (`who`), so the log tells a spare's weakening only while it is in view, and counts only those in view: "An original was destroyed, and 2 of its spares lost heart." and "2 spares are now slowed until you leave (each action takes 200% of its usual time): their original was destroyed." (one alone by its name). Its label shows its weakened numbers, its blow as its line states it, and "slowed, until you leave".
- **Its form** (§5.4): rules `weaken` (the `tier` and `status` effects) and `split` (the `status` effect); nothing bespoke (`Modules.bespoke`): copies fit the form.
- **Off,** an original's fall changes nothing, and no line carries the part.

**The chime** (`hourly_demonstration`). The module counts turns from department entry (its state's `since`), not the clock, so it keeps running in a sealed hall; all stations share the count. A station is a `demonstrator` the module acts on (its species flagged with it). Its period is its own room's (the room of its tile, which it never leaves): `DEMO_PERIOD_INTRO` (8) in the Intro, `DEMO_PERIOD_HALL` (8) in the Centrepiece hall, `DEMO_PERIOD` (12) elsewhere, and in a map with no rooms. Its chime is the turn boundary at which the count is `DEMO_WARNING` (2) short of a multiple of its period; there, each station still asleep and not held fixed wakes, awake, for its performance, keeping the pips it had (boundary step 4, the `period` trigger, §8.2: `Wake.perform`); then, at step 8, the chime sounds once (`module` `chime`, `turns` 2, `n` the stations whose chime it is, and the module keeps the turn, `chimed`), and each such station, in spawn order, awake and not held fixed, marks its line (the special `demo_line`; `module` `line`, which the log leaves to the mark's own sentence, "… marks 12 tiles for its line: 4 before your Padding, in 2 turns.", so it is told once), due on the multiple (§8.1's due lines: this two-turn telegraph replaces the one-action telegraph of other specials), unless another pending damage mark lies on a tile of it (§8.1's no overlaps): then its line is not marked, and `module` `blocked` says so (the module keeps the last, `blocked`). On the multiple the line goes off first (`DEMO_LINE_DMG`, gross, a curve at depth anchors 1/4/8, through each Padding, on all on it), and then each station the chime woke goes back to sleep where it stands with the pips it had (`module` `settle`, `n` those pips; `Wake.resleep`); a station awake before its chime marks its line and stays awake. **Its line** is a §8.1 beam from its tile along its direction (`route.dir`): through glass, stopping where sight does, turned back along itself by a reflector; and it stays in its room, stopping at the first tile that is not its room's (an archway, a door or an opening), so a room's countdown is true for every line that can reach you (one room, one period; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). A station never hunts, never steps (neither a rule nor the Window Pole moves it) and has no blow: whatever would wake another into a hunt wakes it awake (§8.2), and it still acts only on the chime; a noise sends it nowhere (asleep, the pip that wakes it wakes it where it stands, its cause saying so, `how: 'stands'`; awake, it only counts as a stimulus), so the log never says it set off to look; while it is awake it may mark (`Archetypes.marksAwake`), and only going to sleep ("Quiet, Please"), being fixed or destroyed calls its line off. With the module off there is no chime and no line: a station never strikes. The module's sound is a chime, never "the bell", which is the clock's. **What the log tells of it:** the chime ("The chime sounded: …"), always, since it is heard; a station's waking for its chime, its line's mark, its line going off and the line striking an exhibit, only while you see the station (or the exhibit struck), since chimes come on a schedule in every room and a line never leaves its room: one out of your sight reaches you only by its marked tiles, which the board shows wherever you see them, and a walk or a rest, which stop for what the log tells, a new mark in view and anything else in sight (§7.1), stop for those; the confirmation before a long action still names a line due before your next decision (D7.3), and a line that strikes you is told by its damage (M4's first integration review).

As built (MOD-CHIME, `rules/modules/hourly_demonstration.js`): the form's rules `chime` (on `'turn'`; a `flag`), `line` (a `'period'` rule, its period and place the station's room's; a `special`, due) and `blocked` (likewise; a `flag`); bespoke (`Modules.bespoke`): its `turn` (the settle, since no effect of the form puts an exhibit to sleep), its `threat` (below), and `Wake.perform` and `Wake.resleep` (the form's `wake` starts a waking with no pips). **Its threat** (`Modules.threat`) gives each line its chimes not yet sounded will mark, due before the tick asked (none for a chime its station will still be held fixed through, its status ending after it); a line not yet marked has no tiles, and what lies in its way can change before the chime (an exhibit that reflects steps in or out, a door opens, a cloud clears), so it is given at its reach (`HourlyDemonstration.reach(state, a, c)`, for the chime of turn c): every tile of its room along its direction up to the first that sight can never pass (its forward run: a wall or a column, not a door), and its station's tile and the run behind it, which the line takes only off a reflector in its way, while an exhibit that reflects could stand on the forward run by that chime: one within as many tiles of it (counted as steps go, in any of the 8 directions) as the steps it could take before then, at most one each fast step's cost in ticks (`COST_STEP` × `SPEED_FAST_PCT`%), and one more for a Window Pole of yours (any that reflects, while a module whose rules move exhibits is on, or while an exhibit present has, or will gain with its phases, a summons whose body reflects, §8.1: such a body comes with no step; M4 TABLEAU-CORE). An exhibit changes tile only by one-tile steps, and none that reflects is made during a visit but by a summons, which the forecast counts, so the forecast is never smaller than the line; a package that moves exhibits faster or makes a reflector otherwise must widen it. The log tells a station's waking for the chime before the chime sounds, as they come ("… woke for the chime." then "The chime sounded: …"). Its wake region (plan A8) adds that reach, for a chime as late as the turns asked allow, whenever a line of its goes off within them. `HourlyDemonstration.next(state, room)` gives a room's next chime and period (the dial's).

**Weather** (`weather_cases`; M4 plan MO1–MO3, D1.1–D1.3, D3.9). Weather sits in ordinary cases, and opening, breaking or dissolving one lets it out; `push` is cut from M4 (Q7, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable): no rule said what a push does, and a movable case would break §4.1's tile changes, so `push` stays reserved and refused as unknown, and §4.2, §8.1 and `COST_PUSH` mark it after the gate.

As built (MOD-WEATHER, `rules/modules/weather_cases.js`; `rules/surfaces.js`; fog and rain are `Registry.SURFACES`' `fog` and `rain`):
- **Which cases.** As the department is made (its `init`), every real case (none of a mimic's disguise), in the fixtures' order, takes two draws on the layout attempt's `…/a<k>/weather` (§3.2): whether it holds weather (`WEATHER_SHARE_PCT` in 100) and whether that is fog (`WEATHER_FOG_PCT` in 100) or rain. The draws are made whether or not the module is on, so a run with it off has the same rooms; with it off no case is given weather. A case in the Intro is given none (§5.1; the stamps hold no case), and the Centrepiece hall's case is given rain whatever its draws (`mp_tableau`'s rain jar, §5.5; D3.9). A room for tests, given no attempt's key, draws nothing and holds what it is built with. A case's `weather` never changes (§4.1).
- **The release.** A case that holds weather lets it out once, when it is opened (rule `release_opened`, on `case_opened`) or broken by a bash or dissolved by Solvent (rule `release_broken`, on `glass_broke`): the module event `{at}` (the case's tile; cause `by`, whoever opened or broke it), then the `surface` effect: the case's weather, `WEATHER_SIZE` (3) tiles a side, centred on the case, for `WEATHER_TURNS` (10) turns (§8.1's surfaces; the surface event's cause `weather {case}`). The log tells it once, by the weather that got out ("Fog got out of the case: it blocks sight, 3 tiles a side, for 10 turns.").
- **Fog** blocks sight across its square while it lies, both ways, so an axis does not see through it (no pip), and a beam or a line marked while it lies stops at it (§8.1, §8.2); a mark already made keeps its tiles (§4.1), so fog let out after a line is marked does not cut it.
- **Rain** blocks nothing and is not a loud floor. It stops every exhibit flagged with the module that has a tile under it, and nothing else (rule `rain_stop`, on the rain's `surface` event: M.11's gallery station, M.11.2; never the Intro's, M.11.12, flagged with the chime alone): the `status` effect puts `fixed` on it (cause `weather_cases {case}`), so it only waits, and a mark it shows is called off at once ("… line was called off: it was fixed in place."); a station held fixed neither wakes for its chime nor marks (above). It is held until the boundary `RAIN_STOP_TURNS` (2) turns after the rain's last turn: while it stands in rain and for 2 turns after (a rain laid in turn t lies through turn t + 10 and is cleared at the boundary of t + 11, so the stop ends at the boundary of t + 13). Its turns are counted by §8.1's timing rule, from its next decision (the turn of its next action, or the next turn for one asleep), so the status's turns are that end less that start, and its sentence says them ("… is now fixed in place for 12 turns: rain stops it while it stands in it, and for 2 turns after."). A rain is laid only in your action (you open, break or dissolve), so the status is settled as that action ends. An exhibit flagged with the module never steps (a station: neither a rule nor the Window Pole moves it), so it stands in rain exactly while the rain lies; a second rain over it lasts to the later end, and one ending no later adds nothing. The stop is told while you see the exhibit (the status's own log rule).
- **Its words.** The module's part on a flagged line is "Rain stops it.", a sentence of its own after the modifiers (§5.3; the rule is the module's, not clockwork's, and holds with clockwork switched off; MOD-WEATHER's review, decided in play: M.11's station's line keeps the phone strip's 14 rows with it once clockwork's clause says its rule in fewer words, §8.1, while A11's whole "Stops while it stands in rain and for 2 turns after" would take 15). How, and for how long, is told on the label of a case that holds rain (§8.3), as what a fog case does is on its own, in the log as it happens, and on the station's label while it holds (its status, and that a chime would make it mark nothing, §8.1); the module's panel sentence stays as the table gives it.
- **Its form** (§5.4): rules `release_opened`, `release_broken` (each the `surface` effect) and `rain_stop` (the `status` effect); nothing bespoke (`Modules.bespoke`): weather fits the form.
- **Off,** no case holds weather, so nothing gets out.

### 5.5 The slice departments, trimmed

**Every department is Cabinet size (5–7 rooms):**
- an Intro gallery, entered from the west; its first tiles are the vestibule, and every tile within 8 walking tiles of the entrance is the safe zone, where no hostile is placed (`design/layout.md` §7.1). From layout version 3 (M4) the Intro is `INTRO_WIDTH` (14) columns wide and holds its pack's stamp (§5.2), which curation leaves alone; it opens onward only `INTRO_DEPTH` (12) or more columns east of its west wall, whatever makes the opening (a link, a loop, a repair or the staff band), so every onward opening is at walk 12 or more from the entrance, and only across its stamp's cut;
- two to four section galleries;
- the Centrepiece hall;
- a coda with the exit doors.

The samples list more galleries than a Cabinet holds. Each pack maps its sample galleries onto the generated rooms (`Populate.mapping`, no draw): the Intro, the hall and the coda by role; then each section gallery that names a programme element takes the first section, in tour order, with that tag that none has taken (decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable: tour order alone put S.9's court contents in a band room in a third of seeds at layout version 2, and puts them there, and A.4's maze's too, in every seed at version 3, whose wider Intro brings a band room first in the tour); then each other section gallery the first section left in tour order; and a section gallery beyond the plan's sections, and the samples' study-room contents (`last`), join the last section in tour order. A room keeps the indices of the galleries it holds (`dept.rooms[].galleries`, only where a list filled it; `Populate.galleryOf` gives the first, which names it). Measured at layout version 3 (3 × 1,000 seeds), `L_tour` runs 60–83 (p5–p95; 51–78 on the same seeds at version 2, 49–80 at M1), which puts the bell at about 250–310 turns (230–300 at version 2), with the fixture stamp; each department, with its pack's own stamp (below), measures 62–80 (S.9), 64–86 (A.4) and 59–84 (M.11) on 1,000 seeds, the bell at 260–300, 260–320 and 250–310.

**The Intro grows to its stamp** (layout version 3; M4, Q1: decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). At version 2 the Intro's onward openings were 6–9 walking tiles from the entrance: in 23% of S.9 and A.4 plans (225 and 235 of 1,000) the whole Intro lay inside the safe zone, and in two thirds of all plans (2,034 of 3,000) the safe zone spilled into the next room, so no Intro could hold a teaching instance at walk 9–14. D-SPIKE measured the change on 3 × 1,000 seeds before it was built: fallback 0.0%; p95 9.4 CPU-ms (limit 25); locality 0 leaks; every onward opening at walk 13–15; 74–107 Intro floor tiles beyond the safe zone (median); the bell a median 20 turns later in M.11, 10 in A.4 and none in S.9. As built it measures the same (`node tools/layout-sweep.js 1000 --locality`, run again at its review): fallback 0.0%; p95 at most 10.3 CPU-ms; 0 leaks; every onward opening at walk 12 or more and the cut holding in 3,000 of 3,000 plans; first layout attempts rejected 0, 5.1 and 2.5% (enfilade, labyrinth, court; 0, 4.9 and 5.9% at version 2); the nearest onward opening at walk 14–15; 74–107 tiles beyond the safe zone. With it:
- **LV4** also checks that the stamp fits, that every onward opening is at walk `INTRO_DEPTH` or more, and the stamp's `cut` and `near` (§5.2), naming the opening at fault, nearest the vestibule first;
- **the identity checks** (`design/layout.md` §13.2) count the glass and narrow shares outside the stamped Intro, whose furniture is its pack's, not the programme's. Outside it the shares are version 2's (3 × 1,000 plans, p50: the enfilade's glass 64% of its blockers, as at version 2; the labyrinth's glass 92% and narrow 22%, 90% and 20% at version 2), so the rest of the department keeps its programme. But the Intro is now 35%, 25% and 24% of the public floor (enfilade, labyrinth, court; 22%, 15% and 14% at version 2), so each pack's stamp is held to its programme's bar instead (the stamps, below): counted with the fixture stamp, which no pack takes, the enfilade's glass share is 35% (under 40% in 794 of 1,000 plans) and the labyrinth's glass and narrow shares 78% and 17% (under their bars in 231 and 181). `tools/layout-sweep.js` prints the shares both ways. Pillar loops still count the stamp's free-standing fixtures, and the court needs them: with none in S.9's stamp, 10.8% of the court's first attempts fail at layout alone, so S.9's stamp keeps at least two;
- **the certified fallback** depends on the stamp, so each pack has its own seed, certified with its own stamp (`Layout.FALLBACK`, `test/m1.test.js`), and each programme one for plans made without a pack;
- **the enfilade's rows** may run to 50 − `INTRO_WIDTH` − the hall's width (44 less the old Intro's width before), which changes nothing at Cabinet size;
- **every department's plan moves,** and maps are 7 columns wider (`L_tour` above).

*If not:* the Intro keeps its 6–8 columns, and the department teaches its signature in the first section instead.

**The three stamps** (`content/<acc>-intro.js`, M4). Each teaches its signature module only (§5.1), with its exhibits at walk 12 or more (within `design/layout.md` §7.1's 9–14), so that no exhibit's waking ring (`RING_MOVING` walking tiles in sight) reaches the safe zone, whether the partition's door is shut or open, and no line does:
- **S.9, "The Lamp Corridor":** a partition with one door (the cut, column 8) closes off the vestibule; beyond it the Grazer stands on its plinth, three rows north of the entrance row, behind staggered columns, so that walking past it loses sight of it from some tiles and not others unless you route to keep it in view; two Hoppers wait by the way out, where a column hides the Grazer from them. Its plinth and three columns are four free-standing blockers. Its south stretch row is low cabinets in the vestibule (D-PACKS), so that in the court, whose band room runs beyond the Intro's south wall, no tile of the safe zone lies against that wall, and a warden's tether there (which needs no sight) reaches none. The sample's floor lamp is left out: the slice has no light.
- **A.4, "The Office Counter":** rows of writing desks (low furniture) with one-tile lanes; a counter of low furniture (column 11), seen across, with one gap (the cut); the original Seated Clerk, free-standing at the far end of a one-tile aisle on the visitors' side, and its two spares behind the counter beside the gap, one copy-group instance (`clerks@intro`). The sample's counter, climbed in 2 turns, needs a rule the slice lacks.
- **M.11, "The First Landing":** a partition with one door (the cut, column 8); the First Landing's own Semaphore Station (M.11.12, flagged with `hourly_demonstration` alone, so its line states no other module's rule; the Intro holds no weather case) just beyond it, in sight from the doorway over a low cabinet of its timetables, which keeps it 5 walking tiles from the doorway, beyond your ring and the door's noise there; its line runs south (`dir` S) down the column behind the door to the Intro's south wall, so every way onward crosses it, one step from the doorway, and none of it lies in the safe zone; a Logbook Trolley that follows you, at walk 13 (column 13), beyond even a lit Lamp's ring from the doorway (D-LV6's review); low cabinets of timetables and a bench in the first room.

Each is in keeping with its programme: the Intro's own furniture meets the bar its programme's identity check holds the rest of the department to (the enfilade's glass 40–85% of its blockers; the labyrinth's glass at least 75% and its narrow floor at least 15%), or, for the court, whose check asks 8 pillar loops of a plan, the Intro's quarter of them, 2, the Intro being about a quarter of its public floor. With the Intro and the rest each within a share's bar, the department counted whole is too, since a share of two parts lies between theirs. On 1,000 seeds of each department (`test/m4-intro.test.js`): the Intro's glass 51–55% (M.11); glass 100% and narrow 39% (A.4); 4 pillar loops (S.9); every exhibit at walk 12–13; first layout attempts 985, 938 and 1,000 of 1,000 (S.9, A.4, M.11), and no fallback. Population places a stamp's exhibits only for a pack with a gallery list, each once its archetype is ready (the archetype count, below); until then the stamps change the plans alone.

Species, items and labels come from `design/samples.md` §§2–4, with these trims.

**The gallery lists** (M4, D-PACKS): each pack's list is its sample's galleries, in its order, mapped onto a Cabinet department as above; each Intro's exhibits are its stamp's, so its gallery lists none. Measured on 700 seeds of each department with every M4 archetype set ready: no population rejection, every exhibit where its gallery says; `test/sweep/population.check.js` holds this on the shared sweep's 2,100 departments as they are made (a species that waits for its package placed nowhere), and `test/m4-packs.test.js` on 21 seeds each with every M4 archetype planted ready.
- **S.9:** the Mount Court (the court, `central`): a Grazer, the Dawn-Caller, the two Hoppers the Intro does not hold, both Waders, the Overshoes in a case (the court has none, so on its floor); the Brooders' Bay (the band room, `band`): the Brooder guarding a case (its nest, Round Book 9 in it), the Cabinet-Backed Tortoise beside a real case among the band room's cases, the Tongs and the Pin-Driver; the hall: two Grazers and the Lamp's case. The Brooder guards a case in 61% of departments (424 of 700, seeds 1–100 at each depth, as D-ITEMS measured them; 420 before a treasure's hall was curated, §5.5): the band room is S.9's only room with cases a warden can guard under the take rule at `TETHER_RANGE` 2 (the court has none, the coda's 1 in 700 qualify), and it lies along the Intro's south wall, so the Lamp Corridor's vestibule has low cabinets along that wall (its stretch row) and its tether, which needs no sight, reaches no tile of the safe zone (it did in 82 of 700 with that row plain). Elsewhere the Brooder guards its own tile, and its line stays true; it then stands where a keeper can walk round it, as the night staff sweep round it (population's walk-round rule, M4 D-RUNNER: the first floor tile of its room, drawn as a mimic's is, from which its tether reaches no tile of the safe zone, and every way into the band room lies outside its tether and its ring, at `RING_MOVING` in its sight, all of them joined through the room's tiles outside both; any floor tile, logged, when none is). Placed anywhere in the room before, it stood across the bay's way in about half of those departments (84 of seeds 1–200), where the worst-case runner could not pass without waking it: 42 of S.9's 46 failures under charge (b) at 80% on seeds 1–200 began with it. Decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable.
- **A.4:** Originals Under Glass (the maze, `central`): the Seated Clerk's original in a case and its three spares, the Proposing Trustee's original in a case and its two spares (`clerks@s2`, `trustees@s2`), and the Countersigning Clerk; the Copying Room (the band room, `band`): two swarms of Carbon Flimsies, the Copying-Room Bell, standing apart (out of call of every exhibit, below), and the Date Stamp in a case; the Registry joins the maze with the Register.
- **M.11:** the Line (s1): a Heliograph and a Signal Lamp; Weather Kept for Trials (s2, where the cases are): the section's Semaphore Station (plan DE7), the Reflecting Dish, the second Heliograph and the second Signal Lamp; the Logbook Room joins s3 with the Signal Flag. The sample's two Heliographs are one in each axis room, so fog can break an axis where the weather is, and an axis along the entrance row reaches the Intro's open doorway less often (11 of 700 departments, 23 with both in s1, every M4 archetype ready); LV6 counts an axis (below), so such an attempt reseeds (17 of 1,000 M.11 departments, once each). The Logbook Trolley and the Apron stay the Orientation's. Decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable.
- Item entries are the item placement's (§8.5, `Kit.place`, D-ITEMS): each item a list names lies in its gallery's room, `case: true` in an empty case there, or on its floor when none is left (S.9's Overshoes, in a court with no cases), and any other on its floor; an item listed directly after a warden that guards a case lies in that case, its nest, whenever it has one: S.9's Round Book 9, which the Brooder's paragraph says the Museum displays with it ("the two together"), lies in the Brooder's nest in 424 of 700 S.9 departments (seeds 1–100 at each depth), and in the other 276, where it guards its own tile, in its room (decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). The Lamp, listed in the hall, is the treasure, in the treasure case (below). A record that says where an item lies, or names one, is held to it (`test/m4-items.test.js` lists the claims: the Brooder's "displayed the two together", Round Book 9's "in the nest (see S.9.4)"; `test/sweep/items.check.js` checks each).

**General trims:**
- A modifier's effect is the vocabulary's sentence (`design/vocabulary.md` §3) minus any clause that needs a system the slice lacks: fire, light, or damage kinds. The registry stores the trimmed sentence, and the Behaviour line is generated from it.
- `textile`, `luminous` and `paper` have no slice effect and are dropped from the species that carried them.
- Item primitives outside the slice's set are replaced with one in it, of the same budget, or the item is cut. A budget counts S as 1, M as 2 and L as 3, and a flag (`reach`, `quiet`, `breaker`) as 2, as the samples price them; each of the slice's instruments counts as 2, the Pin-Driver's price as the sample's `bolt(edge, M)`, so a sample instrument worth less (the Stamp Moistener's `bolt(water, S)`, 1) is cut. A weapon keeps its sample's form (Balanced where the sample names none), so a flag never becomes the Reach form.
- **The Signature pieces** (M4, D-PACKS; plan D2.9) are trimmed the same way, and each Centrepiece names its own (`signature`), which no stand-in places as loot: S.9's Day Curator's Lamp (S.9.11) is a curio with `observing` (below), the treasure; A.4's Repealing Pen (A.4.11), the sample's `strike_kind(edge, S)`, `breaker` and `surprise_bonus(S)` (4), is Light, and a Light weapon breaks only Plate glass, which one bash breaks anyway (§8.3), so `breaker` could do nothing on it: it takes the sample's own fallback without `breaker`, `strike_kind(edge, S)` and `surprise_bonus(L)`, as Light with damage S and Acc L (4), and no Light weapon of a pack carries `breaker` (`test/m3-content.test.js`); M.11's Pocket Heliograph (M.11.10), the sample's `bolt(light, L)` and `flash(S)` (4), is a Pin-Driver with Acc M (4), the one instrument with a primitive beside its instrument. Their paragraphs say no more than they do: the Lamp's names no case or hall, the Pen's no glass, plaster or paper, the Heliograph's no flash, and none gives its bonus to whoever carries the item, since a bonus counts only while the item is worn. Decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable.
- **A paragraph says where its exhibit stands only where population keeps it** (M4; `test/m4-packs.test.js` lists the claims, and the sweep checks each): A.4.4's "kept under glass" (its gallery stands it in a case, and the maze always has one empty) and M.11.12's timetable "in the cabinet in front of it" (its stamp's). So A.4.1/a's sample paragraph ("Spare (a) of three. For the original, see the case at the end of this row.") is rewritten, since the Intro's original stands in no case and every spare read "(a)"; A.4.1's "the original sat behind glass" is "was kept from harm"; S.9.2's Wader keeps its round "corner to corner", not "pausing at the same lecterns"; and M.11.2's station "stands where it is and waits" between chimes, since no station travels a rail (plan D1.10). A gallery named as a place is such a claim too: A.4.3's bell said nobody in the Copying Room had ever slept through it, and its call reaches `HERALD_RANGE` tiles, short of most of the band room it stands in, so it says nobody standing near it has.

**S.9 (sculpture court):**
- Grazers (`unobserved_mover`), Waders (`patrol_guard`), the Dawn-Caller (`herald`), the Brooder (`tethered_warden`), the Cabinet-Backed Tortoise (`mimic_vitrine`) and Hoppers (`darter`).
- The Waders are exhibits on patrol, with no lamp cone: they see as every exhibit sees. Both walk the court, as the sample and "The Waders' Round" ("This court is alarmed") put them, each on a round of its own (CP1 review; D-PACKS had both on the band room's round): a round keeps to the court's half farther from the Intro, since the court opens onto the Intro along its entrance row and a Wader walking past that door sees the Intro's partition doorway, in the safe zone, once its door is open; so no Wader sees the safe zone in its first `SAFE_ZONE_TURNS` turns, every door open (0 of seeds 1–100). On the band room's round the two Waders saw nearly every tile of it and the coda's doorway turn after turn, and the worst-case runner, timing them, passed 26% of seeds; on the court's far half, 80%. Decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable.
- Items: the Overshoes' `muffled` becomes `quiet`; the Long Tongs' `knockback` becomes Acc; Round Book 9's `perception` becomes Eva; the Spring Pin-Driver is kept.
- The Intro gallery is redrawn for all-round vision: a corridor with staggered columns (a plinth is seen through, §8.2), so walking past the Grazer breaks sight of it unless you route to keep it in view (the stamps, above).
- The Day Curator's Lamp becomes a toggled curio, taken hooded. Lighting or hooding it costs 100 ticks. While it is lit, exhibits within `LAMP_OBSERVE_RANGE` (4) of you count as observed, even out of sight, and your ring is `LAMP_RING` (1) larger, which stands in for the sample's drawback that you are never in the dark. Any range of 3 or more means a Grazer cannot reach you while the Lamp is lit, so the larger ring is the price; the paired bots check that Lamp-lit play is not strictly better in S.9.
- The Centrepiece is a treasure, the Composite (`completing_guardian`) standing over the Lamp in a Security case (Laminated at depths 1–2, §8.3).
  - **The treasure case** (M4, D-ITEMS; `js/core/layout/treasure.js`, `LTreasure`) is the hall's wall case, the one case curation stands in a hall. Its glass is the pack's override, the Centrepiece's `treasure.case.glass` (3, Security), never stronger than the depth's mix holds (so Laminated at depths 1–2, where the mix holds no Security), which `lint-registry --report S9` lists; its lock is drawn for that glass as §8.3 draws any case's, on the hall's populate stream after its own draws; it is never on loan. The Lamp lies in it, hooded, from the department's making, whether or not the Composite stands there (until its core is ready none does, A10), so until then it can be taken as any item in a case is.
  - **The treasure rule:** the case lies at least `LAMP_CASE_MIN_WALK` (6) walking tiles from every opening of the hall (a case's walking distance, 1 + its nearest neighbour's, from each tile outside the hall one steps in from, staff doors included), or the layout attempt is rejected (`treasure`) and reseeds (at most 4 times, then the certified fallback, A10, whose case keeps the rule, below). This is the only place that rule is written, in its validator.
  - **A treasure's hall** (decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable): curation stands its wall case beside the guardian, as the design has the Composite standing over the case (`design/samples.md` §3.8, and the approved placement rule: the Lamp's case beside the Composite, at least 6 walking tiles from every opening). Among the hall's wall tiles at least `LAMP_CASE_MIN_WALK` walking tiles from every opening on the plan as it stands (measured as the treasure rule above measures a case, the footprint counted as floor, as the department has it), it takes the one nearest the footprint, at least 3 tiles from each of its tiles; should none be that far, the farthest. The shuffle that chose any hall's case breaks every tie, so no draw changes and the case may stand on any side of the guardian. Any other hall's case stands as before, anywhere along its wall 4 tiles from the footprint's first tile. Curation only aims at the treasure rule; its validator judges the department. Measured on S.9 (seeds 1–30 at each depth for the first, 1–100 for the rest):
    - placed as other halls have it, the case lay fewer than 6 walking tiles from an opening in 62% of S.9's halls (131 of 210): only 38% of S.9's departments would have come from their first layout attempt, 9.5% (20 of 210) would have fallen back, and the certified fallback's case lay 4 tiles in, so every fallback broke the rule;
    - on the wall tile farthest from the openings (D-ITEMS as built, before its review), the case lay 11–13 walking tiles in for 244 of 700 departments, longer than the `TREASURE_SEAL_TURNS` (12) turns after the case is touched leave for the rest of a Security case's bashes, a take and the walk out, so the hall would seal before a visitor walking out with the Lamp could leave it; and it lay more than 3 tiles from the footprint in 356;
    - curated beside the guardian, the validator turns down 26 plans in 736 attempts over 700 departments: 667 come from the first attempt, none falls back, the case lies 6–10 walking tiles in and 3 tiles from the footprint in 675, and the certified fallback's case lies 7 tiles in, which every validator accepts at each depth. M1's thresholds hold for S.9's plans curated so (1,000 seeds: no fallback, p95 11.4 CPU-ms, no tile of another room changed when a room is re-curated).
    Only S.9's plans change; A.4's and M.11's are as they were byte for byte (layout version 3, §3.2).
  - **The Composite** (M4 COMPOSITE-CORE and COMPOSITE-PHASES; `content/S9-cp.js`, `rules/arch/completing_guardian.js`, `rules/treasure.js`; `design/samples.md` §3.8, trimmed per plan D3.5, D3.6 and D3.14, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable) is one 2×2 body of the role `centrepiece`, added with the hooks every Centrepiece shares (§8.7):
    - **It guards the Lamp's case.** It takes the hall's case as what it guards (`guards`), and its trigger, `touch_case`, wakes it at once, hunting, when that case is opened or bashed (§8.3: a touch reaches what guards a case); Solvent's dissolving it is no touch. The first touch's turn is recorded (`dept.cp.touched` `{turn}`), from which its treasure's seal counts (below). Its line names that case as its own ("Its case is the Lamp’s, which it guards, and a view that puts it under its dust sheet closes that case too.").
    - **It never leaves its hall:** no step, its own or the Window Pole's, puts a tile of it outside. While you stand outside the hall it starts no fight with you: its limbs mark nothing and, hunting or searching, it strikes no blow; hunting, while it sees you it walks back to its place and waits there, still hunting, and once it does not, or searching, it goes back (`lost`, cause `outside`: "… goes back to its place: you are outside its hall, which it never leaves."), so the log never has it search where you are. Awake, sent to look by a noise, it looks only within its hall: a noise beyond it, which no step of it reaches, leaves it wandering its hall, as an awake exhibit does that no step gets to what it was sent to look at (§8.2), and it goes back once nothing has disturbed it for `AWAKE_IDLE_TURNS`, as any exhibit does; so its line says it goes back only of it hunting or searching for you ("…while you are outside the hall it starts no fight with you: hunting or searching for you, it goes back to its place"; COMPOSITE-CORE's review).
    - **Its limbs** are its specials (§8.1: its neck, `cp_sweep`; its legs, `cp_charge`; its tail, `cp_ring`), marked in turn. Walking grows its neck, then its legs, one for every `GUARDIAN_PER_STAGE` (2) galleries you have walked, and no other (`Guardian.WALKED`; never more than `GUARDIAN_STAGE_MAX`, 4, in all): the galleries are the rooms of the department that hold a gallery and that you have stood in, the one you start in and its own hall among them, a room holding two galleries (S.9's court, which the Round-Book Room joins) counting once and a staff room never (`Guardian.galleries`), since a player sees rooms, and nothing of the Round-Book Room the court holds; then each of its phases from the second grows its next limb (below), and its tail and its fourth limb, its crest, come only so. Placed, it has those already due, untold; then at every turn boundary (a hall check) each limb newly due grows, told by a `phase` event in the phase it is in (cause `walked {n}`, with its `stage` and the limb `gained`: "… grew its legs, for the 4 galleries you have walked: it has 2 limbs."), wherever you are, since it says nothing of where it stands. Every S.9 department holds five gallery rooms (all 420 of seeds 1–60 at each depth: the Intro, the court, the band room, the hall and the coda), so walking gives it its neck and its legs at most, and a tail for six galleries would be a promise walking never keeps: the samples' "galleries explored ÷ 2" counts seven galleries, and the Cabinet holds five rooms (plan D3.5, which adds a limb at phase 2 for it). So walking grows no other, its line says so ("first its neck, then its legs, and walking grows no other"), and its tail comes from its phases alone (COMPOSITE-CORE's review, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). A visitor who reaches its hall by the shortest way meets it with one limb in 253 of those 420 departments, and with two in the rest, where that way passes through four or five of them; one who walks every gallery room meets it with two.
    - **Its phases** (COMPOSITE-PHASES; plan D3.5's limb at phase 2, and the sample's stage at phase 3, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable): each phase it enters from the second grows its next limb, the first of its neck, legs, tail and crest that it lacks (`Guardian.gain`; while it has fewer than `GUARDIAN_STAGE_MAX`), told after that phase's own event (`phase`, cause `limb`: "… grew its tail as its phase 2 began: it has 3 limbs."). So one who walked every gallery room meets its tail at phase 2 and its crest at phase 3, and one who walked fewer its legs, then its tail. Its crest (`cp_call`, shape `crest`) is a call, as a herald's is (§8.1): marked only while you stand in its hall on one of its tiles (those within `HERALD_RANGE` (6) of it), it deals no damage, and when it goes off every sleeping exhibit on them that can hear, never a Centrepiece's piece, wakes awake, sent to look at your tile (cause `herald`, by it; one in an intact case wakes in it and stays there), which in S.9's hall are its two Grazers.
    - **The alarm** (COMPOSITE-PHASES; the sample's phase 2, trimmed): its record is flagged with `alarm_network`, whose part sits in its second phase's slot ("From phase 2: it grows its next limb, and in its hall it chases you, first showing the alarm for a turn and raising it unless one is shown or running: …"; with the module off, "… and in its hall it chases you as before"). From its second phase, hunting you in its hall and seeing you, with the module on and no alarm shown or running, it shows the alarm on its own tiles, at an action at which none of its limbs is marked, and its next action raises it, as a Wader's does (§5.4: the crash, the wakes, the alarm's turns, the clock nearer); outside its hall it shows none, starting no fight.
    - **The leave-heal** (COMPOSITE-PHASES; plan D3.5): a turn boundary that finds you outside its hall (an opening is outside it) after one that found you inside it while it hunted you is your leaving it while it hunts you, by any step or by a Re-admission Ticket: it regains `GUARDIAN_LEAVE_HEAL_PCT` (100)% of its maximum health (`healed`, cause `left`) and drops its last limb (`phase`, cause `left` naming the limb: "You left …’s hall while it hunted you, so it dropped its legs: it has 1 limb."). Its phase never goes back, and walking gives a dropped limb back only for galleries walked after. A blow or a shot from outside its hall goes unanswered, since it starts no fight there and the leave-heal answers only a leaving, but it is no safe way to kill it and then carry the Lamp out unopposed (measured with its core planted ready, seeds 1–20 at each depth; COMPOSITE-PHASES' review, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable): its place lies beside none of its hall's openings (0 of 140), and lured to one, it walks back from you as you step out into it, so no blow from an opening landed (0 in 376 tries, one from each opening); a Pin-Driver's bolt reaches it at its place from outside its hall in 20 of the 140 (from an opening each time), and with its 4 charges and one back every 30 turns it never killed it before the night watch (a visitor who could not die, shooting from turn 0, took 391–2,341 turns; the median visitor died first in 19 of the 20, and killed it in one, at depth 1, 391 turns in, in the night watch), so a shot from outside only softens it for a fight (for the expert's probes, R14).
    - **Its treasure** (COMPOSITE-PHASES; `rules/treasure.js`; plan D3.14): its hall does not seal on the hunt alone. While it hunts you in its hall, the hall seals once no one stands in an opening, from `TREASURE_SEAL_TURNS` (12) turns after its case was first opened or bashed, or while its health is `TREASURE_SEAL_HP_PCT` (34)% of its full health or less (§8.4; the seal names no piece as `by`, so its line need not cover `stage`).
