At each turn boundary, after the seal's own step, holding the Lamp (the department's, from its case; not one you came with, the carry's `you.c1` …) outside its unsealed hall, an opening included, carries it out: `dept.cp.treasure` `{turn}` records it, once, and the Certificate follows (`award('treasure')`: "…: you carried its treasure out of the hall."), unless the visit has one. A Re-admission Ticket read while you carry it puts you at the entrance and opens a sealed hall, so the boundary that ends the read counts it carried out. Killing it earns nothing (D3.14); carrying the Lamp out after its fall still does. A view that puts it under its dust sheet closes its case too (§8.7). Measured with its core planted ready (seeds 1–20 at each depth, alarm and unobserved on): standing beside the case, bashing it open, taking the Lamp and walking straight to the nearest opening, round whoever is in the way, carries it out in 92 of 140 departments, with at most 3 turns to spare, and the hall seals first in 48 (in 18 of the 21 whose case lies 10 walking tiles in); decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable.
    - **Its line** says that it guards the Lamp's case, which its dust sheet closes too, and never leaves its hall, the leave-heal, its treasure's seal, its blow, what each limb marks and deals, its form's rule (killing it opens its hall but earns nothing), how its limbs grow, as its first phase's part, which the phone's strip shows in phase 1 ("It grows a limb, each a special, for every 2 galleries you have walked, the one you start in and its own hall among them: first its neck, then its legs, and walking grows no other."), as its second's, its next limb and the alarm's part, and, as its third's, its next limb and that its limbs' specials then come more often. Its strip takes 7, 14 and 5 rows in its three phases with the alarm on (4 in phase 2 with it off; 14 at most, §5.3's length); its whole line, on its label, 57 (59 as built; the review left out what the line said twice). Its gateway label, on the lectern (plan D3.8: 96 words), is the sample's, trimmed to the Composite and the day curator's visit, and claims no rule.
    - **Its death awards nothing** (§8.7): no Signature piece drops and no Certificate comes; a sealed hall opens.
    - **Not yet placed:** its core stays at `ready: false`, so no department places it, until what the Ordinance's and the Board's wait for: the perk choice a Certificate brings has an interface (UI-PROGRESS), and a Centrepiece's strip is the phone's and its lectern's and sheet's labels exist (LABELS-M4); then the tests that assume no Centrepiece is placed are brought up to date, S.9's runner sweep, soak, `Dept.create` p95 and views are measured with it placed, and `m3MonkeyS9` moves. Until then the Lamp's case is guarded by no one and the Lamp can be taken as any item in a case is (above).
  - carrying the Lamp out of the hall alive is S.9's form of defeating its Centrepiece: it earns the Certificate and the Signature piece. The placard and the Composite's Behaviour line say so;
  - viewing earns the Certificate alone;
  - a treasure hall does not seal when the Composite starts hunting. While it hunts you there it seals `TREASURE_SEAL_TURNS` (12) turns after the case is touched, or when the Composite falls to `TREASURE_SEAL_HP_PCT` (34)% health, as in `design/samples.md` §3.8 (above). Crossing the hall's threshold with the Lamp before then, or leaving by Re-admission Ticket while carrying it, counts as carrying it out;
  - the sample's phase 2 is trimmed to "the Composite gains one stage; if `alarm_network` is on, the alarm is raised" (from then on, as a Wader raises it), and its phase 3 to "the Composite gains one stage" and the seal (above). Security lighting is dropped with light, and the sample's attachments (the Dawn-Caller, a second Brooder) with its alarms' count: the Dawn-Caller's call is the crest's.

**A.4 (case labyrinth):**
- Seated Clerks (`plodder`; the original `marble`, its spares `plaster`), the Proposing Trustee and spares (`splitter`; the original `marble`, its spares with no modifier), Carbon Flimsies (`mote_swarm`) and the Countersigning Clerk (`conservator_healer` + `clockwork`).
- The Spirit Duplicator takes the sample's own fallback: a `herald`, "Copying-Room Bell". It stands apart (its entry's `apart`; CP1 review, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable): beside the Flimsies its call woke both swarms, and through the maze's wall a copy group, and the worst-case runner passed 76.5% of seeds; apart, 94%. Moved into the maze it passed 96.5%, but it is the Copying-Room Bell, and the Quiet notice is posted in the Copying Room for it.
- The `ally_hit` groups are the clerks (the Seated Clerk and its spare) and the trustees (the Bust and its spare).
- The Seated Clerks and the Proposing Trustees, each original and spare, are flagged with `copies_weaken` (§5.4). The Trustee's spare drops the sample's `plaster` (decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable): with `copies_weaken`'s part, the spare's line (splitter, brute, plaster, `ally_hit`) took 16 rows of the phone's strip even with its clauses tightened, against §5.3's 14, so it is a full-health brute, in keeping with the sample's "some spares are better made than their originals", and its Medium is cast stone, so that it names no material modifier (the tombstone cue).
- Items: the Date Stamp, Light as in the sample, swaps its `sweep` for the `reach` flag; the Sleeve Protectors' fire resistance becomes Eva; the Register takes the sample's own fallback without L+, `perception(M)`, as Acc; the Paperweight is kept; the Stamp Moistener is cut, since it is worth 1 and each instrument 2.
- The Centrepiece is the `mp_ensemble` fight of `design/samples.md` §4.8, trimmed (M4 ENSEMBLE-CORE, `content/A4-cp.js` and `rules/arch/mp_ensemble.js`; Q8, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable): **The Duplicate Ordinance, With Its Three Certified Copies**, four one-tile pieces (`ENSEMBLE_PIECES`) on its 2×2 footprint, not the sample's five sheets sharing one health pool, which would make "break the original first" mean nothing.
  - **Its pieces.** The signed original (A.4.12, its number with no letter) stands on the footprint's top-left tile, where the Repealing Pen drops; certified copies a, b and c (A.4.12a–c) on the others. Each is an exhibit of its own with its own label: its name, number, tombstone and paragraph say which it is ("The Duplicate Ordinance, Signed Original"; "…, Certified Copy a"), and the board draws the original's sheet signed and each copy's stamped (§5.2's pieces' words). Each holds its own health, an equal whole share of the role's, the original also the rest (§8.7), which its own odds readout counts. They never step (neither a rule nor the Window Pole moves one), and each strikes from beside you with the role's blow. Copies a and b carry the burst (`cp_burst`) from the start; the original and copy c none (the sample's `summon` waits for its owner; copy c gains one only by inheritance, below).
  - **Its phases** count all its pieces' health, and their full health, together (§8.7). Phase 2's special, the burst, goes to its first piece still standing as the phase begins: the original while it stands, else the first copy left by letter, which has it already. Its phase events take a cause of its own, `ensemble`, which says whether you see the piece that gains (`seen`, as the view model judges it): its sentence names that piece only then, and otherwise says "The first of them still standing, out of your sight, has a burst", so no sentence names a piece of it out of your sight.
  - **The original first.** The copies are the original's spares, one copy group (`CopiesWeaken.addGroup`, made when its pieces are set up), and its record is flagged with `copies_weaken`, so its line carries the module's part (§5.4): with the module on, the original's fall weakens and slows each copy still standing, and their health together falls with it, which can bring a phase; a copy's fall weakens no one; with the module off, neither does the original's.
  - **Inheritance** (M4 ENSEMBLE-PHASES; the sample's rule, design/samples.md §4.8; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). While its original stands, when a copy falls, each piece still standing, in its pieces' order, takes on every special the fallen copy had (in the order it had them) that it lacks, at the end of its own list, which takes turns as every Centrepiece's specials do (§8.1). The original's own fall passes nothing on, and once it has fallen (or was left out by `without`) no fall passes anything: so dealing with the original first, as its gateway label advises, leaves the copies only the specials they hold, whether `copies_weaken` is on or off (the module's own rule, the copies' lost heart, is the spares'). Each is told by a `phase` event in the phase it is in (it enters none), cause `inherited` (`from`, the fallen piece; `seen`, whether you see the piece that takes it on, as for `ensemble`): its sentence names that piece only when you see it ("A piece still standing, out of your sight, took on the burst of …, which fell"; the fallen piece is named, as its own fall's sentence names it), and the pieces that take one special on from one fall are told in one sentence. A spent shatter (below) is not taken on; since phase 3 gives the shatter to the original while it stands, and nothing passes once it has fallen, no shatter is taken on in play.
  - **Phase 3's shatter** (`shatter_all`, the sample's phase-3 special, trimmed; M4 ENSEMBLE-PHASES): phase 3 gives it to its first piece still standing, alongside phase 3's quicker cadence. Its shape is the tile of every case of its hall still intact (shut, on its vitrine; never a mimic's, and no mimic is placed in a hall), marked in its owner's turn while it hunts, whether or not it sees you, a mark that deals no damage; at its owner's next action each case still intact on a marked tile breaks as a breaking bash breaks one (§8.3), but with no touch: it is broken, passable and loud, what stood in it is released, hunting and homeless, and its shards fall as the shard rule lays them from its own tile (on its first floor or archway neighbour in direction order), each told by `glass_broke` with the cause `shatter`, whose sentence names no piece ("The Centrepiece's shatter broke the Plate glass of a case in its hall, and shards spread"); then, every case broken, a crash from each. It is no bash: no `touch_case`. A shatter's mark and its use are told only while you see the piece (`Text.seenOnly`), since a piece marks it without seeing you. **The shatter is spent once no case of its hall is intact**: each piece holding it drops it, told by `special` (cancelled) with the cause `spent`, naming the piece only when you see it, at the first turn boundary, phase it enters, fall of a piece, or action of that piece, with none intact; so a shatter that could not be marked never holds a piece's other specials back for more than that action, in which it acts as it otherwise would. A.4's hall has one wall case, which holds nothing; the sample's four side vitrines, whose spares joined phase 3, are cut (plan D3.7), and its hall's gallery lists nothing.
  - **Its gallery hook** (`ENSEMBLE_HOOK_PCT`; the sample's originals are labelled "Companion piece to A.4.12"; M4 ENSEMBLE-PHASES, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). Each original destroyed outside its hall (an exhibit some spare copies, not a piece of its own, every body of whose line is gone, its first body destroyed, not left out by `without`, where it stood outside the hall: in a gallery, the Intro or a passage) costs the Ordinance `ENSEMBLE_HOOK_PCT[0]` (8)% of its full health (its pieces' maximums together, rounded down), for at most `ENSEMBLE_HOOK_PCT[1]` ÷ `ENSEMBLE_HOOK_PCT[0]` (3) originals, so at most `ENSEMBLE_HOOK_PCT[1]` (24)% in all. Its first piece standing loses it, never its last point, at the turn boundary after the original's fall (a check the hall runs, `Hall.addCheck`, after its seal), counted in `dept.cp.hook`: `damaged` with the cause `hook` (`of`, the original), which is no blow, so no piece wakes and no one hears it (a phase 2 it brings wakes the Clerk, as every phase 2 does), and whose sentence names no piece ("An original, a companion piece to the Centrepiece, was destroyed outside its hall: its first piece standing lost 3 health"); none is told when it takes nothing. The loss can bring a phase, cause `ensemble`. It counts with `copies_weaken` on or off (the module's rule is the spares'), and not once none of its pieces stands.
  - **Its attachment, the Senior Countersigning Clerk** (A.4.13, its record's `attachments`; M4 plan D3.13): a `conservator_healer` with `clockwork`, as A.4.5 is, with the healer's rule and line (it keeps `CONSERVATOR_KEEP` (2) tiles from you, never strikes, and restores `CONSERVATOR_HEAL_PCT` (25)% of the most-hurt ally it sees, shown for one action; its pieces among them, below). It is made with its pieces, asleep, on the hall's tile two below its footprint's top-left tile, or the free tile of the hall nearest it (Chebyshev, ties in row-major order; free: walkable for an exhibit, no closed door, no fixture, no one there, and more than `RING_STILL` + 1 walking tiles from the hall's reading tile, as no gallery exhibit stands nearer), with its own role's numbers (`support`) and its own label (`Packs.speciesFor` gives a Centrepiece's attachment as a species of its own). It is no piece: not in `dept.cp`, its health in no phase, and its fall changes nothing of the Ordinance's. It wakes as any exhibit does (its line's waking, a blow, a broken case), and, if it still sleeps as the Ordinance enters phase 2, then, hunting you (the sample's "wakes behind the lecterns", plan D3.13; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable), whatever brought the phase (a blow, a fall, its gallery hook): `woke` and `hunting` with the cause `ensemble` (`phase`), a wake cause of its own ("…woke and began hunting you: the Centrepiece is in phase 2"); its phase-2 part says so. The Clerk's interrupt (a Stamp Moistener's jet) stays cut (plan D3.7): nothing interrupts its restore but what calls off any mark (§8.1). **Measured** (M4 ENSEMBLE-PHASES' review: real A.4 departments with its core planted ready, depths 1–7, seeds 1–6, the single visit's visitor starting inside the hall and made unable to die): striking its pieces alone, original first and never the Clerk, all 42 fights ended, in 40 turns at the median and 65 at most, against 33 and 44 with the Clerk left out, its restores giving back about 3 of every 10 health struck; chased first, the Clerk fell in 10 turns at the median and every fight ended within 52. So it lengthens the fight but does not stall it; a visitor weaker than the single visit's (grade I at depth 7) cannot out-strike its restores, about 10 health every 3 turns there, and must strike it down first. (Able to die, the single visit's visitor won 2 of the 42 with or without the Clerk, and none chasing it first: the pieces' own blows decide it, a balance question for the runner and the tune, not the Clerk's.)
  - **Its line** states each piece's share, that its phases count its health together, which piece is the original, inheritance, the gallery hook, the copies rule, where phase 2's burst goes, and that from phase 3 each piece marks a special at most once every `CP_SPECIAL_EVERY[1]` of its actions, not `CP_SPECIAL_EVERY[0]` (§8.1's cadence, which every Centrepiece's third phase brings and each piece's odds readout gives as it stands), and its first piece standing has the shatter, which is spent once no case of its hall is intact, and that its Clerk, if asleep, wakes as phase 2 begins. At the strip's measure it takes 43 rows at depth 7, which its label shows and scrolls; its strip takes at most 6, 7 and 9 rows in phases 1, 2 and 3 (§5.3, `m4-lines`). Its Clerk's line is A.4.5's, 12 rows. Its gateway label, on the lectern, names the original by its number. Sentences that speak of the Centrepiece as a whole name none of its pieces for it: its Certificate by defeat says "you defeated its Centrepiece", and a view, which names its first piece still standing, says that the Centrepiece must sleep and that the sheet covers the Centrepiece (§8.7).
  - **A conservator** (A.4.5, and its own Clerk) restores a piece it sees as it restores any exhibit, never past that piece's maximum (a copy that has lost heart, only to its weakened one), and never undoes a phase, which never goes back (§8.7), nor tells one again when the piece is struck back down.
  - **Not yet placed:** its core stays at `ready: false`, so no department places it, until the perk choice a Certificate brings has an interface (UI-PROGRESS), a Centrepiece's strip is the phone's and its lectern and sheet have labels (LABELS-M4), and the hall's seal no longer names a piece you cannot see. (Its pieces hunt but stand fixed, and since M4 TABLEAU-CORE the log tells their modes as made where they stand, §8.2's `how: 'stands'`, never that one set off to look, searched where it last knew you were, or went back to its place.) Then the tests that assume no Centrepiece is placed are brought up to date, A.4's runner sweep, soak, `Dept.create` p95 and views are measured with it placed, and `m3MonkeyA4` moves. Its gallery hook, its Clerk, inheritance and phase 3's shatter are built (M4 ENSEMBLE-PHASES, `test/behaviour/mp_ensemble-phases.test.js`); placed, its Clerk too is measured with the rest.

**M.11 (enfilade, with a parallel staff corridor):**
- Heliographs (`axis_sniper` + `glass`), Semaphore Stations (`demonstrator` + `clockwork`), Signal Lamps (`kiter` + `old_repair`), Reflecting Dishes (`reflector`) and Logbook Trolleys (`plodder`).
- `glass` keeps its shards (the one shard rule, §8.3) and its reflection, which turns back shots as well as beams, as a reflector does (§8.1); it drops "blunt ×1.5" (a damage kind) and "its body does not block sight" (no body blocks sight, §8.2).
- `old_repair` doubles the damage that comes from its repaired side, after Padding and the minimum: `max(DMG_MIN_ON_HIT, raw − Padding) × OLD_REPAIR_PCT %`, and the cause says `side`. The side is a compass point in the species' `modParams` (the Signal Lamps': west). Damage comes from it when its `from` tile (§8.1) lies toward that point, or either point beside it (for west: north-west, west and south-west), seen from the body's nearest tile; so a blow, a Reach blow, a shot or a beam from that side counts.
- The Centrepiece is the `mp_tableau` fight of `design/samples.md` §2.8, trimmed (M4 TABLEAU-CORE, `content/M11-cp.js` and `rules/arch/mp_tableau.js`; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable): **The Great Board of the Central Station**, one 2×2 body, its Signature piece the Line Superintendent's Pocket Heliograph (M.11.10).
  - **It never moves** (`Archetypes.neverSteps`: no rule steps it, and the Window Pole moves only a one-tile exhibit, `too_big`). It hunts as any exhibit does, strikes from beside you with the role's blow, and every change of its mode takes place where it stands, as the log tells it (§8.2's `how: 'stands'`).
  - **Its specials take turns** (§8.1's several specials, one every `CP_SPECIAL_EVERY` of its actions), each waiting until it can be marked, so a beam that cannot be marked holds its burst back: from the start its beam (`cp_line`: while it sees you in one of its rows or columns, that line from it toward you, up to `AXIS_BEAM_RANGE` tiles, a beam) and its burst (`cp_burst`), the sample's line and 3×3 alternating; from phase 2 its summons too (`summon`, §8.1: Logbook Trolleys, M.11.5, its record's `summons`; up to `CP_SUMMON[1]` of them, onto the free tiles of its hall beside it nearest where it last knew you were, still free when it goes off), the sample's 2-turn telegraph trimmed to the mark every special shows until its owner's next action, and while it is marked it takes `CP_SUMMON_DMG_PCT` (200)% of the damage dealt to it, the sample's punish window, which its odds readout and its Attack button count and say; from phase 3 its specials come every `CP_SPECIAL_EVERY[1]` actions (the sample's "every 2nd action").
  - **A Logbook Trolley it calls is no piece of it** (`dept.cp.uids` is the Board alone): its blows, and its standing beside you, neither restart the sealed hall's quiet count nor keep the hall sealed (§8.4), and the Board's fall is its own.
  - **Its line** says all of this; at the strip's measure it takes 30 rows, which its label shows and scrolls, and its strip at most 11 in any phase (§5.3, `m4-lines`). Its gateway label, on the lectern, is the sample's.
  - **Not yet placed:** its core stays at `ready: false`, so no department places it, for what the Ordinance's waits for (above): the perk choice's interface (UI-PROGRESS), a Centrepiece's strip as the phone's and its lectern's and sheet's labels (LABELS-M4); then the tests that assume no Centrepiece is placed are brought up to date, M.11's runner sweep, soak, `Dept.create` p95 and views are measured with it placed, and `m3MonkeyM11` moves.
  - **Its attachments** (M4 TABLEAU-PHASES, the record's `attachments`, set out by its core as it is placed, `rules/arch/mp_tableau.js`; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable): two exhibits of their own, no pieces of it (`dept.cp.uids` is its body alone: their blows neither restart the sealed hall's quiet count nor keep the hall sealed, §8.4, and the Board's fall is its own), asleep, each with its role's numbers, their uids the hall's next after the Board's (§3.2), each placed once its archetype, modifiers and trigger are past `ready: false`. Nothing is drawn, and they take the same tiles under any `rulesOff`; a department made without the Board (`options.without`) has neither.
    - **The Board's Repeater Arm** (M.11.11a; the sample's `demonstrator` with `clockwork`): a station like M.11.2 (its archetype, modifier, trigger and flags, so M.11.2's line word for word), so it performs its line on every chime, every `DEMO_PERIOD_HALL` turns counted from entry, the hall sealed or not (§5.4), and rain stops it. It stands beside the hall's case, within its weather's square (`WEATHER_SIZE`), on the free tile of plain floor nearest the Board (row-major on a tie): the hall's one rain case (§5.4's init gives the hall's case rain; plan D3.9, the sample's two rain jars as one case) stops it when opened, broken or dissolved. Its station's rule puts it there whatever the reading tile's still ring (a station never hunts: your ring there at most wakes it, and it marks its line at each chime awake or asleep), but, since it never leaves its tile, never on the reading tile itself, nor on one that, with the Board's footprint, would cut a tile off from the entrance or leave a case no tile beside it to reach (a mimic's disguise's rule in population, below); the next nearest that keeps it is taken (TABLEAU-PHASES' review: the nearest was the reading tile in 339 of 2,100 halls). A hall with no case (27 of 2,100 M.11 departments at layout version 3, seeds 1–300 at depths 1–7), or none with such a tile, puts it beside the Board, the first such tile in row-major order; then no rain falls on it, and nothing in the hall says any will (its line's "Rain stops it." holds of rain anywhere; once the core is placed, the hall's case check, below, rejects such a layout attempt, which reseeds). Its line runs N, E, S or W: of those whose line, out and back along itself (a reflector turns it back), never crosses the Board, the longest no other exhibit stands on (the station's rule, above), so it never strikes the Board; one of the two axes always misses it.
    - **The Board Dish** (M.11.11b; the sample's `reflector`), with the Board's own rule, `tab_screen`, a modifier its line states: the Board's second phase wakes it at once, hunting you (its wake cause `tab_phase`, by the Board), or, woken sooner and awake, searching or going back, turns it to hunting you (TABLEAU-PHASES' review); hunting while the Board stands, it strikes you if it can, else walks toward the free tile of the hall beside the Board nearest where it last knew you were (by squared distance, then row-major), going round the others as an exhibit walking anywhere but to you does (§8.1), and waits there. So it stands between you and the Board: your bolts at the Board come back into you, and the Board's beam, marked while the Dish stands in it, turns back into the Board (§8.1), the Dish's doing. Out of its hunt, or once the Board has fallen, it is a reflector like any other, as it is with its rule switched off (`rulesOff`). Until its waking it stands apart: of the hall's free tiles of plain floor never within `RING_STILL` + 1 walking tiles of the reading tile, those out of the Board's rows and columns and of the Repeater Arm's line first, then the nearest the Board at `max(RING_MOVING, NOISE_SOFT)` + 2 or more walking tiles from its footprint (beyond your ring and the soft noise of your blows beside it), else the farthest. It wakes as any exhibit does too, so going near it wakes it sooner (walking straight in from an opening to the Board and fighting it wakes it before the second phase in 252 of 720 entries, M.11 seeds 1–60 at depths 2 and 5, every opening; its line says so). Its paragraph says a signal goes back the way it came, never that it shields the Board. The chime's forecast needs no widening for it: it changes tile only by its own steps, which `HourlyDemonstration.reach` counts for every reflector (§5.4).
    - **Cut:** the sample's side-door Heliographs of phase 3 (plan D3.7); nothing in the slice says they come. The sample's pushed third rain jar goes with `push` (§4.2).
- Items: the Hand Lamp's bolt of light becomes the Pin-Driver's ranged attack; the Smoked Apron's armour and light resistance become Padding; the Signal Flag keeps its `reach` flag, on a Balanced weapon, since its sample names no form; the Spare Lamp Hood is cut, since it needs light.

**Archetype count:**
- 14 non-Centrepiece archetypes, plus `night_watchman`, plus three Centrepiece cores (`completing_guardian`, `mp_ensemble`, `mp_tableau`).
- M3 builds eleven: `plodder`, `darter`, `kiter`, `axis_sniper`, `splitter`, `mote_swarm`, `herald`, `tethered_warden`, `reflector`, `mimic_vitrine`, and `night_watchman` for the clock.
- M4 builds the rest with the modules.
- **A pack with no gallery list places only species whose archetype M3 builds, and no Centrepiece,** since the others' Behaviour lines could not yet be true (§5.3). This stand-in placement (`Populate.exhibits`) puts one of each such species in the sections and studies, outside the safe zone (`SAFE_ZONE` walking tiles); a `mote_swarm` comes as `SWARM_BODIES[0]` bodies, on the nearest free floor tiles of its room.
- **A pack with a gallery list** (M4) places a species only once its archetype, each of its modifiers and its trigger are past `ready: false` (`Populate.ready`; the log says which entries wait), and its Centrepiece only once its core is. With the pack's own stamp (§5.2, its marks in `plan.intro`), each mark whose letter the legend names an exhibit puts it on that tile, and the Intro gallery's own contents are not placed again; under the fixture stamp, which a pack that gives none takes and which names no exhibit, the Intro gallery is placed as any other. Every other entry is placed in its gallery's room, `count` times, on that room's `exhibits` stream: on a tile its option asks for (an empty case; a tile beside a real case; a tile within the tether of a case it could guard), or, with none left, the room's floor (logged); always beyond the safe zone, off the Centrepiece's footprint, more than `RING_STILL + 1` walking tiles from the hall's reading tile, on no one, and 3 or more tiles from every exhibit where the room allows, else 2, else 1. A mimic's tile keeps these rules as the case its disguise makes it (a case's walking distance is 1 + its nearest neighbour's, which round a doorway's corner can be shorter than the floor tile's), and its disguise, with those of the mimics placed before it, cuts no tile off from the entrance and leaves every case a tile beside it (LV1 and LV10 again); a stamp's mimic that would not is not placed. No mimic stands in the hall. A swarm comes as `SWARM_BODIES[0]` bodies, on the nearest free tiles to the first. A copy group is one instance a room (`group` is `<group>@<roomKey>`; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). Then, with no draw: a station's line runs N, E, S or W along the longest straight run of its room that sight passes and no other exhibit stands on (the first on a tie), and, when every run meets an exhibit, the layout attempt fails (M4 FU3: a Signal Lamp struck awake so shot you at the entrance in 2 of M.11's seeds 1–140), unless its entry gives `dir` (a sleeper on its line would be struck at the first chime and wake hunting you, §8.2, from a room you may never have seen, and a reflector on it would send the line back into its station at every chime: M4's first integration review; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable); a Wader's round (CP1 review; decided without play, revisit once M4 is playable) keeps to the half of its room farther from the Intro's onward openings (the room cut across its longer side; the whole room in a plan with no stamped Intro), so that no Wader walks past the Intro's mouth, and is its own: a room's k Waders cut that half into k strips across its longer side, a line of tiles kept between two, the j-th in spawn order taking the j-th; its posts are the free tiles nearest its strip's corners, NW, NE, SE, SW, each once, and it starts the visit on the first; a post its walk would stall on for good (its way there blocked only by exhibits that stand still, or another Wader head on: `Patrol.timeline`'s `stuck`, §8.2), or the post it started on if it never left it, is left out, logged, and the round fixed again without it, a strip left with fewer than two posts giving way, once, to the whole half's round; a herald whose entry gives `apart` stands where its call (`HERALD_RANGE`) reaches no exhibit placed before it, or, with no such tile, elsewhere in its room (logged); a warden with `guards: 'case'` guards the nearest empty case of its room from which no tile you could take is one step from outside its tether, and its own tile if none is; a Wader whose room gives fewer than two posts keeps no round (logged). A plan the list cannot fill fails the attempt as `population`, with the first exhibit it could not place and its tiles; the rest are still placed and fixed, and every exhibit not placed is logged with why (an entry stops at its first, the line counting what is missing), so a certified fallback taken despite the rejection holds every exhibit that fits and says which do not. The log also says when a gallery's `where` names an element no section left has here, and when a stamp places the Intro's exhibits instead of its gallery.

**The visitor of a single visit** (M4; Q4, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). Until M5 builds the run, a department visit is made for one visitor, `Dept.create(seed, acc, depth, {carry})`: by default the median visitor at that depth (`Progress.median`), the visitor `MEDIAN_MAX_HP` and the arenas assume: grade d (at most VII) with that grade's health, a Balanced weapon and a Standard garment of the lower tier on offer at d (`TIER_LOW_BY_DEPTH`), level 0, no perks and no items. At depth 1 that is a run's start, so a first visit is as it was. `carry: 'start'` gives the visitor of a run's start at any depth (M3's: grade I, tier 1), and `carry: {grade, perks: [{id, rank}], tier, items: [{ref, slot}]}` another, any part left out being the median's: perks go to the run, and each item, one of the department's own, is worn in its slot or carried in the tote (`slot: 'tote'`), as `you.c1`, `you.c2` … at the carry's tier. The carry is not kept in the state. A Certificate earned in the visit raises its visitor's grade, and the perk taken from its offer is held, for the rest of that visit only; "Another visit" starts from the visitor the visit was made for (§8.7, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable).

**Making a department** (M4): each layout attempt whose plan passes layout's checks is populated, in this order: the cases, the exhibits, `without`, each archetype's init, the Centrepiece, your starting kit and the carry's items, the items, the seals, the consumables, the panels, the modules and your stats; then, unless population failed, every validator registered with `Populate.addValidator(id, fn(state, plan, log) → null | {reason, tiles})` runs, in the order registered, and the first rejection fails the attempt, which reseeds up to 4 times and then takes the certified fallback, populated the same way (`Layout.generate`'s `accept`). They are registered in load order: LV6, the treasure case, LV11 (the worst-case runner, below), then LV7, LV13 and the hall's case (below); LV11 alone may change what it judges, moving at most `RUNNER_MOVES` exhibits, each logged. LV6, LV11, LV7, LV13 and the hall's case judge the department as it is made (`Populate.asMade`: every module on, nothing left out), so neither a switch (`rulesOff`) nor `without` changes which attempt is taken. A validator may add lines to the attempt's layout log (`log`). `Dept.generate(seed, acc, depth, options)` gives the department with how it was made, which the state does not keep: the attempt taken, whether it was the fallback, each failed attempt's failures, the fallback's own rejection, and the log. `tools/dept-p95.js` measures what a department costs to make (§10.2).

**The safe zone** (LV6; `js/core/layout/lv6.js`, the first validator registered; M4, D-LV6; `design/layout.md` §7.1). The safe zone is every tile you could stand on within `SAFE_ZONE` (8) walking tiles of the entrance, and LV6 rejects a department in which anything an exhibit does over the visit's first `SAFE_ZONE_TURNS` (20) turns reaches you on any tile of it. It asks of every exhibit, the Intro's, a Centrepiece's pieces and one in a case included:
- where it stands: no tile of it within `SAFE_ZONE` walking tiles of the entrance (a case or plinth one beyond its nearest neighbour you could step onto);
- its wake region (`Archetypes.wakeRegion` over those turns): a sleeper's ring at `RING_MOVING` in sight, a station's line, an awake exhibit's sight;
- a patrol on its round (S.9's Waders): what it sees from where it stands at each of its actions over those turns, as `Patrol.timeline` tells it exactly (the timeline the worst-case runner reads), and its wake region only where the timeline cannot be told;
- what the wake region leaves out: a sleeper's `axis` (its row or column within `AXIS_RANGE`, in its sight) and its `tether` (within `TETHER_RANGE` of what it guards, which needs no sight, so it reaches through a wall);
- the noise of opening or closing a door beside the zone (a soft noise at the door, `Noise.reach`): no exhibit within `NOISE_SOFT` of it that the noise stirs: a sleeper, since `PIPS_TO_WAKE` turns of it would wake it, or an awake exhibit, which comes to look at the door, unless it never steps (`Wake.stands`: a station, the Great Board) or only waits in its case (§8.2);
- your ring at its largest there: `RING_LOUD_FLOOR` wider standing on a loud floor, from that tile only (your ring is measured from where you stand), and `LAMP_RING` wider again with the Lamp lit where the department's pack holds a curio with `observing` (in M4, S.9's Lamp, which a visitor may wear lit from the start, plan A2) or you carry one.

Each is asked with the doors as the department is made (shut) and again with every ordinary door open, since you open the Intro's partition door to go on, and an exhibit opens the doors on its way; staff doors stay shut. A surface is not counted, since it only ever hides you. Its rejection says, in a player's words, which exhibit (named as the log names it), how it would wake or find you, and where: the zone's tile it reaches, the door it hears, or, for one standing in the zone, its tile nearest the entrance; for example "standing at 8,7, in the safe zone, once the doors are open, you would wake the Heliograph, Landing Pattern: you are in its row or column within 8 tiles, in its sight". It marks the exhibit's tiles and the zone's tiles it reaches. Over seeds 1–1,000 of each department (seed s at depth ((s − 1) mod 7) + 1) it rejects an attempt of no S.9 or A.4 department and of 17 M.11 departments (1.7%), each a Heliograph whose axis holds the Intro's open doorway, and each reseeds once; none falls back. Each of the 17 is true in play: standing in the doorway with its door open, the Heliograph wakes on the third turn. The first attempt is taken in 93.4% of S.9, 93.8% of A.4 and 98.3% of M.11 departments, the rest failing layout's own checks (LV8, LV14), the treasure case (S.9) or population; with LV11 a validator too (below), in 92.6%, 91.7% and 97.2%. In play (D-LV6's review), standing on every tile of the zone for its first 20 turns, moving, with the doors as made and all open (and in S.9 with the Lamp lit), and opening and closing each door beside it three times, nothing gains a pip, wakes, hunts or strikes you on seeds 1–70 of each department. Counting the doors open, an axis, a door's noise and the Lamp's ring, and taking an exhibit awake from the start whose rules give it no wake region to find you, are decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable. M.11's Logbook Trolley stands at walk 13, where not even a lit Lamp's ring from the zone reaches it (the stamp's column 13, D-LV6's review): in column 12 that ring reached it from the open doorway in all 1,000 M.11 departments, so a Lamp carried into M.11 (M5's run) would have sent every attempt to the fallback; now it rejects none. Not counted, as it falls outside the first 20 turns: a sleeper a station's line strikes at its first chime wakes hunting you from wherever it is (population's line rule lets a line meet an exhibit only where every run of the station's room does); in 2 of M.11 seeds 1–140 a Signal Lamp so woken in s2 reaches the entrance and shoots you about turn 35. LV6 judges the department as it is made, every module on and nothing left out (`Populate.asMade`, as LV11 does; D-RUNNER's review): judged with an exhibit left out, as it was before, it took M.11's first attempt in 16 of 1,000 departments when the exhibit left out was the Heliograph it rejects there, which moved every other exhibit against §3.6.

**The worst-case runner** (LV11; `js/core/layout/runner.js`, a validator since M4's D-RUNNER, after LV6 and the treasure case; plan D2.3, Q2's default: charge (b) at 80%, at least 90% of seeds at the first attempt, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). Its runner is the median visitor less one Certificate: a grade less, with that grade's health and its kit's stats at that grade (`design/layout.md` §9.2's "minus one Restoration level on the weapon" has nothing to take from the median's level 0; the grade short of the depth's kit, at depths 3, 5 and 7, costs Acc and Padding instead). Its route starts where you stand as the visit begins, one tile a turn, a closed door taking a turn more to open, never through the hall, a staff door or an exhibit that stands still; it wakes what the slice's rules wake (the ring and every `wakeRegion`, an axis and a tether, and what a waking wakes in turn: the alarm, a herald's call, `ally_hit`), each charged as Q2 says. **A tethered warden fights only within its tether** (D-RUNNER's review; decided without play, revisit once M4 is playable): it starts no fight while you stand outside the disc of `TETHER_RANGE` round what it guards (§8.2), so it is charged from the first turn, once it is awake, that the route stands within that disc, and not at all when the route keeps outside it, however it woke; played, a route that wakes the Brooder with its ring and keeps outside its tether leaves with full health (`test/m4-runner.test.js`). **A patrol is timed** (CP1 review; decided without play, revisit once M4 is playable): timing a Wader is S.9's whole skill, so where its walk can be told (`Patrol.timeline`) the runner may wait for a gap, and a Wader sees it only from where the Wader stands at each of its actions; once the runner has a fight it keeps to no timetable, and from then on a timed patrol finds it wherever it could see it on its round. Routes that wait, played in the game, wake nothing and reach an exit (`test/m4-runner.test.js`). **A station's line** is charged once, at its net damage, when the route stands on a tile it could take as it goes off, and a route may wait it out.

**LV11's verdict and its moves** (D-RUNNER; decided without play, revisit once M4 is playable). An attempt passes when its cheapest route under charge (b) costs at most `RUNNER_PASS_PCT` (80%) of the runner's health; the search stops at the first route that passes, which is the full report's verdict (a failure has had every route tried), and the timed search stops after 600,000 steps tried (it never came near that on 600 departments; a route it would miss is a pass missed, never a cost understated). One that fails is repaired, as `design/layout.md` §9.2 says, by at most `RUNNER_MOVES` (2) moves, each of another exhibit, never a removal. The exhibit moved is the costliest the cheapest route wakes by itself (its ring, its axis or its tether; not a waking's chain or a patrol's sight), counting its charge (b) fought alone and that of what its waking wakes in turn, among those a move may take: asleep, on the floor, one tile in size, and not a station, a swarm's body, a warden whose nest is a case, a Centrepiece's piece, or one in the hall or the Intro, whose places their own rules fix. It goes to the nearest tile of its own room (walking from where it stood; row-major on a tie) where population could have put it (floor beyond the safe zone, off the Centrepiece's footprint and the reading tile's ring, no actor or item on it, no exhibit beside it, never where a patrol walks or a station's line could run, and out of call of every exhibit for a herald its gallery keeps apart), where none of its ring, its axis and its tether (a warden guarding its own tile takes that along) holds a tile of that route, and where LV6 still passes; then the runner is asked again. Once it passes, each move is logged in the attempt's layout log as a sentence that names the exhibit and what woke it, for example "moved the Ground-Nesting Brooder, With Its Nest, from 19,20 to 12,20, in the Brooders’ Bay: the worst-case runner's way came within 2 tiles of what it guards, which wakes it at once; from 12,20 it passes clear of it", or "moved the Seated Clerk, Certified Spare, from 22,6 to 16,2, in Originals Under Glass: the worst-case runner's way passed within 3 walking tiles of it, in its sight, for 3 turns, which wakes it; from 16,2 it passes clear of it"; a tethered warden that woke otherwise is moved only where the way then came within its tether, and the sentence says so ("…, which wakes it, and came within 2 tiles of what it guards, where it strikes; …"). If it still fails after the last move, every move is undone and the attempt is rejected, and reseeds; the rejection says what the cheapest route costs and what starts its costliest fight, in a player's words ("the worst-case runner's cheapest route costs 114 of its 45 HP (at most 36, 80%): the Reflecting Dish, on a Cart wakes as it passes at turn 24, and 3 more join the fight", M.11 seed 154; a tethered warden woken before the route reaches its tether, "… wakes as it passes at turn 11, and strikes once the runner comes within 2 tiles of what it guards, at turn 12"), then the moves tried and what the route still costs ("; with the … moved from x,y to x,y, then …, it still costs n"), or that the runner "finds no way to an exit but through the hall, a staff door or an exhibit that stands still". A move stays in its exhibit's room, so where the route crosses that whole room within the exhibit's ring there is no tile to move it to, and the attempt reseeds (M.11 seed 119's Signal Lamp). LV11 judges the department as it is made with every module on and nothing left out (`Populate.asMade`), and makes the same moves whatever is switched off or left out, so a switch or an exhibit left out never changes which attempt is taken or where anything stands (paired runs share rooms). Its moves draw nothing.

**Its gate** (§10.3; `node tools/runner-sweep.js --check`, and check-all's shared sweep on seeds 1–100). Over seeds 1–1,000 of each department (seed s at depth ((s − 1) mod 7) + 1), every final department passes LV11 run again; the first attempt LV11 judged passes in 989 S.9, 979 A.4 and 989 M.11 departments (975, 948 and 967 with no move); LV11 reseeds 11, 22 and 11 attempts; none falls back; 14, 37 and 32 moves are made in 14, 32 and 24 departments; no exhibit is removed; and the oracle agrees on seeds 1–100. Counting every check, 926, 917 and 972 departments take layout attempt 0 (measured at D-RUNNER's landing, its review's tethered warden included: charged as before, a warden woken outside its tether charged its whole fight, which moved four S.9 departments of these for a fight they never have, and LV11 now reseeds one, S.9 seed 820, whose cheapest route the Waders find, that a move of the Brooder repaired by chance). Before D-RUNNER, on the same seeds, charge (b) at 80% passed the first attempt it would have judged in 82.3% of S.9's, 94.8% of A.4's and 96.7% of M.11's departments ((a) 82.3, 97.4 and 97.3; (c) 82.3, 95.6 and 95.9), and one A.4 department had no way to an exit at all. With LV11 stood aside, the walk-round rule alone brings S.9's to 97.5%; with the moves alone, no walk-round rule, S.9's first attempt passed 98.2%, but only 82.3% with no move (161 departments moved). When a Centrepiece is placed (each core is `ready: false` so far), the runner must be measured again: its route keeps out of the hall, so a piece wakes only from what reaches through the hall's openings, and the seal and the phases are not modelled; a move never takes a piece.

**LV7, LV13 and the hall's case** (`js/core/layout/lv-m4.js`, registered after LV11; M4 D-VALIDATORS, plan D2.11, D1.12 and D3.9; decided without play, revisit once M4 is playable). Each judges the department as made and draws nothing; a rejection is a failed layout attempt, which reseeds, and its sentence says what fails and where. `node tools/layout-sweep.js <seeds> --validators` sweeps the three departments (`--cores` plants every Centrepiece core ready, as the tests do), and check-all's shared sweep holds every department to them, with an oracle for each.
- **LV7, no unrewarded long dead end** (`design/layout.md` §9.1). On the room graph (each room one node; each tile of an opening between rooms, the entrance and each exit a node of its own; joined where you could step from one to the other; staff doors left out), a dead end is what one node, its junction, cuts off from the entrance and from every exit, one inside another included (a reward down one branch does not reward the walk down another). It sees dead ends between rooms only, not a pocket inside a room. One reaching `DEAD_END_REWARD` (12) walking tiles or more beyond its junction must hold a reward: an item on its floor or in one of its cases (a Restoration kit is a Highlight), or the Centrepiece hall, the set piece's room, counted so whether or not its core is placed yet. M4 has no lure, and layout.md's filling of empty dead-end corridors, a layout repair, is not built. **It is vacuous at Cabinet size:** over seeds 1–200 of each department at depths 1–7, the only dead end, nested ones included, is the Centrepiece hall, in S.9 (up to 15 walking tiles deep) and A.4 (up to 13); M.11 has none. So LV7 has never rejected a department; planted faults and the sweep's oracle hold it.
- **LV13 for the chime** (MO12). A station's line at its reach (`HourlyDemonstration.reach`, the widest it can be at any chime) that cuts the entrance from every exit must leave a window: its period less the most turns running that the best way through stands on it must be at least `DEMO_WINDOW` (3). A way goes as LV11's route goes (over tiles you could step onto, never a staff door, a tile of the Centrepiece hall, or a tile an exhibit asleep or in a case stands on), a turn a tile, and the rejection says so ("every way …, but through the Centrepiece hall, a staff door or an exhibit that stands still, …"); stepping off the line and back starts the count again, since the line goes off on one turn of each period. The lines of the stations of one room, which strike on the same turn, are judged together: a way that steps from one onto another stands on both in one run. **It is pessimistic, on purpose:** it takes each line at its widest (its back run counts whenever any reflector exists, and a line not marked because it would overlap one already lying counts all the same), so it may reject a department a player could cross; that is cheap, since it has never rejected one. Over the same sweep the First Landing's line is the only one that cuts the way (in every M.11 department: one turn on it, a window of 7 in its period of 8), and LV13 has never rejected a department.
- **The hall's case** (D3.9: M.11's hall has one rain case). Once a Centrepiece is placed in a department whose weather is held in cases (`weather_cases`), its hall must hold a real case, and each station among its attachments (the Great Board's Repeater Arm) must stand within that case's rain (`WEATHER_SIZE` square, centred on it), so rain can stop it. The cores are at ready false, so nothing is judged yet. With the Board's core planted ready, 17 of 1,400 M.11 halls (seeds 1–200 at depths 1–7) held no real case; each now reseeds (first attempt 96.9% → 95.6%, no fallback).

---

## 6. The board views: top-down and 3D, and how to revert

Julian's answer: try the 3D view, built so that going back to top-down is easy. The design makes the 3D view a second renderer of the same view model, never a second game, and gives it its own milestone (M2b, §11) so that it can never hold up the core.

### 6.1 The view model

`ViewModel.derive(state, ui) → vm` is pure and lives in `js/core`, so it is tested headless. It holds:
- the map size and its tiles: each tile in view as it is now, each remembered tile as you last saw it (`dept.seen`, written for the tiles in view at the end of every step), and each tile you have never seen as `' '` (masked), so a board rebuilt from the view model shows only what you have seen: a door an exhibit opened out of your sight shows shut until you see it again (the log only says a door opened nearby, as its noise). A hall's shutters (M4), whose closing and opening the log tells you, are remembered as they close and open on every opening you remember, so the board never shows a remembered opening open while its shutter is down; as they open, each remembered opening goes back to what you last saw there before they closed (a door an exhibit opened out of your sight shows shut until you see it), and one you saw only as a shutter is unseen again; an opening you have never seen stays unseen. Reading a Floor Plan remembers every tile as it is then, and an arena's `seen: all` every tile as built;
- per tile: unseen, remembered or visible, and inside your ring;
- the actors on visible tiles (and you), each with uid, position, size, sprite id, mode, pips, whether it is hostile, the special it is marking, its statuses and whether it stands in an intact case; never one that shows as something else (a mimic in its disguise, which is listed as the case it looks like);
- **marks,** one a tile of each kind: telegraphed tiles with their exact damage to you, and a herald's call, on visible tiles; noise rings: the noises made since your last action that took time (all of a long action's turns, such as a garment's three), on the tiles an exhibit could stand on (`Grid.hold`) that each reached as hearing measures it, so walls and fixtures are not marked, and on tiles you see or remember, each measured on the map as it stood when the noise was made (a door opened later in the turn does not widen it), the loudest class where two noises reach a tile; an action that takes no time (a refusal, such as a bump into a wall, or a free read) leaves them as they were, as it leaves your ring (§8.2); the tile you aim at and a thrown Solution's square round it, its own kind's once you know the kind and, while you do not, the largest any Solution has (5×5, the area §8.6's throw refusal measures), so the square says nothing of the kind; in the Orientation, what its step asks you to use (§7.2: an exhibit you see, or a tile in view); the path preview; the selection;
- what stands or lies on visible tiles: cases (a mimic's disguise exactly as a real case, with no accession number), staff doors and their grades, items (a consumable by its seal's shape), identifying labels and surfaces;
- your health, grade, statuses, kit, tote and charges; the clock; once a Floor Plan is read, the rooms that still hold a Highlight;
- the camera target: you, leaning at most two tiles towards a telegraph that covers you;
- one-turn animation cues (the tile an actor moved from, only where that tile is in view; hit flash; glass that broke in view; a special gone off), from the same events as the noise rings: those of your last action that took time.

**It holds no field that only one renderer reads.** Wall and fixture heights, billboard sizes, silhouettes and camera placement are computed inside each renderer from `vm.tiles` and `vm.actors`: these are drawing choices, not game decisions. A test lists every vm field and the renderers that read it. Neither renderer reads `state`, computes visibility or decides anything.

`coreHash` (§3.6) covers `js/core` and `content/` except `viewmodel.js`. Because the bots read the view model (§10.2), sim output also records `vmHash`, a hash of `viewmodel.js`.

### 6.2 The renderer interface

```
Views.register(id, label, factory)   factory(host) → renderer
Views.list()                         → [{ id, label }]; the settings line is built from it
Views.active()                       → { id, note }
renderer.ready                       Promise: resolves when it can draw; rejects on a failed load, which falls back
renderer.draw(vm)                    draw one frame; called only when the vm, the zoom, the size or a cue changes
renderer.pick(clientX, clientY)      → { x, y } or null (below)
renderer.tileRect(x, y)              → { centre, rect, tapRadius } in CSS pixels
renderer.zooms, renderer.zoom        the view's zoom table, and the index in use
renderer.setZoom(index)
renderer.setInset(px)                how much of the board's foot the phone's label sheet covers; the camera centres in the rest
renderer.inspect()                   → [{ kind: 'tile' | 'actor' | 'mark', id, x, y, rect }] drawn in the last frame
renderer.idBuffer(opts)              3D only: → { w, h, ids, legend } for the occlusion check; { floorOnly: true } draws the
                                     floor alone, { scene: true } the floor and what stands on it, and the default adds every
                                     actor and mark, each legend entry recording its pixels alone and those shown over the scene
renderer.dispose()
```

**On the board.** An actor counts as on the board when at least a quarter of its square shows each way in the part no interface covers; a hostile on the board keeps its hostile bar inside the part that shows, and any other hostile in sight gets an edge arrow, slid along the edge clear of every actor and other arrow.

**Canvases.** Every canvas a view draws is a whole number of device pixels, and its box on the page exactly that size divided by the pixel ratio, so the page never resamples it: a board of fractional height would otherwise blur the pixel art and shift each mark by up to a device pixel. The board's canvases also keep their box a whole number of CSS pixels wherever the ratio allows (one of the four whole sizes just below the board's), since the page resamples a canvas whose box is not: a box 524.5 px tall at a ratio of 2, 1049 device pixels, drew every sprite half a device pixel off (found by check-3d at D-ITEMS' landing).

**The display list.** Both views build their overlays with `Marks.list()` (`js/view/marks.js`): surfaces, the ring, calls, noise rings and a Solution's square, the moved-from cue, the path preview, telegraph fills, case glyphs (the lock with a bar per grade, cracks, what a case holds or the empty frame of a loan), staff-door grades, labels and items (each of these glyphs of what stands or lies on a tile sits on a dark backing, black at 45% or more and the lock on black, a crack over a wider dark line, so that no wall, floor or glass, however pale, hides it: over its backing on any department's walls, floors and glass each glyph's colour has WCAG contrast 3:1 or more; a lock's bars are each at least 2 pixels wide with a pixel between, at every zoom of both views), the Orientation's frame round what its step asks you to use (a round-cornered frame just outside its square, beneath the actors so it covers none of them and no glyph row, and still, whatever the motion setting), actors back to front, each actor's marks (its bar; its glyph row above it, or beside its head where another actor or another's row is above, so it never covers one, and, only where both sides are taken too, shrunk to fit along the top of its own square; its hit cue, the cue of its special gone off and its selection), the aim, glass that has just broken, a selected tile, edge arrows for hostiles off the board, and the telegraph numbers last. Every colour that carries meaning is a reserved one, and every mark's meaning is in its shape too. Each item carries its own paint function, so a view paints the list, `inspect()` reports it, and the 3D id buffer repaints each item alone, so the checks measure exactly what was drawn.

**TopDownView** (`topdown.js`, Canvas 2D) is the reference:
- 16×16 art drawn at 32 px a tile on a phone (2×, 12 tiles across 390 px), with a second zoom step at 48 px (3×, about 8 across);
- three-quarter wall faces;
- a hall's shutter (`Z`, M4) as a ribbed steel shutter on a wall's top, never as floor, so a shut opening never looks open; the 3D view draws it as a steel block as tall as a wall, ribbed across its top and its south face. Both views draw it in the same two colours, a light steel and a near-black rib, the two at 10:1 to each other and, against every department's walls, wall tops and floors, one of them at 3:1 or more (`test/behaviour/hall.test.js`); `test/views.test.js` fails a view that lists it among the tiles it draws as plain floor;
- `pick` is the tile under the pointer.

**Board3DView** (`board3d.js`, three.js):
- **Camera:** orthographic, 60° above the floor, from the south, so north stays up as in top-down. Scale s is CSS pixels per tile edge, the same everywhere on screen; a tile shows as an s × 0.87s rectangle. Zoom table: s = 40 and s = 52, the second giving a 44-px disc inside each tile.
- **Units and heights.** One tile edge is 1 unit. Walls, door frames, staff doors and columns 1.2; partitions and shelving 1.0; vitrine base 0.25 with glass up to 0.85; plinth 0.5; bench 0.3; lectern 0.7. From due south at 60°, a block of height h hides h ÷ tan 60° of the tile behind it. The probe's 1.5 hid 1–2% of the floor from the south-east, but from due south it would hide 87% of every room's back row and fail the occlusion check; 1.2 hides 69%.
- **Actors and overlays are never hidden.** Sprites (16×16, nearest-neighbour, standing on the south edge of the actor's footprint: n·s wide and s·(n·sin 60° + 1 − sin 60°) tall, so an s × s square for one tile, and a 2×2 Centrepiece reaches no further into the row north of it than a one-tile figure does) are drawn on a 2D layer above the WebGL scene, back to front, and so are the overlays: telegraphs and their numbers, the hostile mark, pips and state glyphs, the selection, the ring and the path preview. Both views draw these with the same code (`js/view/marks.js`), so they look the same. Walls can hide floor, never a visible exhibit or a mark. There is no cutaway and no x-ray in the slice; the occlusion check (§6.3) says whether one is needed.
- **Lighting:** faces are shaded by direction (tops in the palette's top colour, south faces in its wall colour, the sides darker), a fixed light baked into the vertex colours, with no shadows, so that no lighting effect implies a line-of-sight rule.
- **`pick`** returns the anchor tile of the nearest actor with an opaque sprite pixel under the point; otherwise the floor tile where the ray meets the floor plane, ignoring walls; otherwise null.
- **Budget.** Device pixel ratio capped at 2, antialiasing off, and it renders only on change.
- **Context loss.** On `webglcontextlost` the board shows the top-down view at once; on `webglcontextrestored` the 3D view rebuilds from the last vm.

### 6.3 Switching, fallback, and the revert test

- **The switch.** Settings has one line, "Board: Flat / 3D", built from `Views.list()` and hidden when only one view is registered. Switching rebuilds the renderer at once; the game state is untouched.
- **The fallback.** If `board3d.js` is absent, three.js fails to load, or WebGL is unavailable, the registry falls back to top-down and the settings line says why in one sentence.
- **Input is the same in both views.** Walking confirmation is its own setting, "Walk: one tap / tap twice", independent of the board, default one tap. Renderers have no input rules.
- **Reverting is one script,** `tools/remove-3d.sh`. It deletes `js/view/board3d.js`, `vendor/three/`, `tools/vendor-three.sh` and `tools/browser/check-3d.mjs`, the two tags in `index.html` (the `board3d.js` script and the inert three.js placeholder, §6.4) and the `vendor/three` entries in the asset ledger. Nothing else needs editing: `build-single.py` has one generic rule for any `<script type="text/x-…" data-src>` placeholder, the settings line disappears with the second view, and tests that cover several views loop over `Views.list()`.
- **The revert test.** `check-all.sh` copies the repository to a temporary directory, runs `tools/remove-3d.sh` there, then builds and runs the Node tests and the browser smoke and screen checks there. They must all pass. In the full check-all they are jobs of its pool (§10.2): the copy made and built (`tools/revert-test.sh --prepare`), then every Node test file a plain `node test/run.js` runs, each a job of its own, and the smoke and screen checks, all run in full; `tools/revert-test.sh` alone runs the whole test one step after another.
- **The guard.** A test scans `index.html`, `js/`, `css/`, `tools/`, `test/` and `art/` for `board3d`, `Board3D`, `THREE`, `x-three`, `vendor/three` and a `'3d'` id, and fails on any file not in the allowlist committed beside the script.
- **The 3D checks** (Playwright, Chromium with software WebGL, which works here through SwiftShader) run on fixed scenes in all three slice programmes, at both zoom steps, at phone and laptop size, and each first asserts that `Views.active()` is the 3D view, so a silent fallback cannot pass them.
  - **Occlusion.** From `idBuffer()`: fail if more than 2% of the visible tiles without a mark (the faint rings of a noise, a call and a Solution's square do not count, since the floor shows through them) show less than 25% of their floor pixels (a tile's own case, plinth or bench counts as showing it), or if an entrance or exit whose tile lies wholly in the clear part of the board shows none of its mat. Marks and actors are drawn on top: each mark shows at least 90% of its pixels as itself, as another mark (one drawn after it, or a telegraph's number) or, for the marks §6.2 draws beneath the actors (the path preview, a telegraph's fill, calls, noise rings, a Solution's square, and the glyphs of what stands or lies on a tile), as an actor drawn over it, and never as the scene; every actor wholly on the board shows at least 75% of its pixels as itself (a nearer actor drawn over a farther one is depth, within that allowance); the 2D layer is the topmost element over each of them; a screenshot of the page shows at least 95% of the 2D layer's fully opaque actor and mark pixels in the clear part of the board in the colour painted there (so a 2D layer hidden by opacity, a filter or a blend mode, set off the board, or resampled by the page fails); and the overlay canvas matches a reference painted from the same display list, item by item in order, at 98% of their pixels (at a device pixel ratio of 1 and of 2). One scene stands you directly north of an exhibit you have just struck, a 2×2 Centrepiece where one can be reached, and is measured again with telegraphs on your tile, on the exhibit's tiles and on a free tile beside you. Two more are staged round you and measured at both zooms: an opened case, a broken one, shards and an open staff door, with a cracked case, a noise ring and a herald's call; and two awake exhibits in a column, the farther in a case and the nearer marking a special.
  - **Tap tolerance.** For every visible tile on screen, the radius of the largest disc round its centre in which every sampled `pick` returns that tile, or an actor standing on it (as `research/isometric.md` §1 measured). Fail if the 10th percentile is below 12 px at the default zoom or below 22 px at the 44-px step. The same check runs in top-down.
  - **Consistency.** Every actor and mark in the vm appears in `inspect()` with a rect inside the board, in both views.
  - **Context loss.** `WEBGL_lose_context` forces a loss and a restore; the board keeps drawing.
  - **Cost.** Frame time and load time are recorded, not gated, because this environment has no GPU. Julian's phone and Mac numbers go in the validation log, and the phone check includes leaving the page in the background for a minute mid-department.
- **Default.** Top-down stays the default until the slice gate, where half the players start in each view and the gate decides (§11, M7).

### 6.4 Vendoring three.js

- **The build.** `tools/vendor-three.sh` pins three@0.180.0 (r180, the version the probe measured) and esbuild@0.25.10, bundles an entry that re-exports only the names `board3d.js` uses, minifies it into an IIFE that defines `window.THREE`, and writes `vendor/three/three.iife.js`, `LICENSE` (MIT) and `VERSION` (the versions, the output's SHA-256 and its size). The output is committed; the build does not touch the network.
- **In development,** `index.html` holds an inert placeholder, `<script type="text/x-three" data-src="vendor/three/three.iife.js"></script>`, and `board3d.js` loads that file with a new script element when 3D is first chosen; `renderer.ready` waits for it.
- **In `dist/accession.html`,** it is inlined as an inert block, run on first use by copying its text into a new script element. It is not compiled until 3D is switched on, but every load downloads it; the validation log records the dist size with and without it.

---

## 7. Input and interface

### 7.1 One input model

**Every input works alone:** the keyboard only; a mouse or trackpad only (one button); a touchscreen only. Each reaches every verb and every screen, and none is an extra that another needs. A tap and a left click are one input, set out first; the keys follow.

**Taps:**
- **A tile:** walk there. The walk stops when a hostile comes into view, a pip appears on a visible exhibit, a telegraph appears, or an event names you. As built (`js/main.js`): it stops after a step whose events the log tells you of and that include a refusal, a door opened, a hunt begun, a hit or a miss, a death, a mark, a waking, an alert, a clock stage, an arrival, a case opened, cracked or broken, an item taken or used, a seal identified, or a status of yours beginning or ending; when an exhibit in view changes its mode or pips, or one comes into view that you have not seen this visit or that is not as you last saw it (its mode, pips or tile), never for one going out of view or one you have seen coming back into view as it was; when a new mark appears; on the first loud tile it brings you onto; and where the next tile is taken. A step that needs a confirmation (below) ends the walk: the sheet asks, and its answer takes that one step or none.
- **An exhibit or object,** adjacent or not: examine it and select it. The strip and panel show it, with its action as a button ("Attack: 75%, 3–5 damage"; "Open: Grade III"; "Break the glass: loud"). Tapping a selected adjacent exhibit again does its first action, which for a hostile is Attack. A case, an item on the floor, an identifying label and a staff door are selected the same way, and tapping what is selected does its label's first action (Open, or on a case your grade cannot open Break the glass, which asks first; Take; Read; Open or Close a staff door); with none, the next thing on that tile is selected (a case under the exhibit standing in it), or, where you can stand, you walk there. Every label button acts through the same confirmations as a tap.
- **Yourself:** Wait, Rest, Sit on a bench, and Leave on an exit.
- **Tapping yourself** shows your label with those buttons, and tapping yourself again waits; an item lying on your tile is selected before you. Rest repeats under the walk's stop rules until you are at full health, or after one rest while no health can come back (the night watch); a rest that repeated leaves one line in the log when it ends, however it ends: the turns it took and the health that came back (`Text.rest`).
- **Walking confirmation** is a setting, "Walk: one tap / tap twice", the same in both views; with "tap twice", a tile tap previews the path and a second tap on the same tile goes.

**Confirmations** come before:
- stepping onto shards (from a tile without them; a broken case's tile holds its glass, so it counts as shards);
- breaking glass (each blow, saying whether it cracks or breaks it);
- stepping into a Centrepiece's hall from outside it, while its Centrepiece stands and the hall is not sealed (`Hall.entering`): it says what the seal does and how the shutters open, and that the hall will seal behind you as this turn ends whenever one of its pieces hunts you and no one stands in another of its openings (M4, R11; a treasure's hall says its guardian's label says when);
- leaving by an exit (a minimal confirmation: the visit ends; leaving the Orientation's practice room starts one, and its sheet says so, §8.9);
- going to the practice room from a visit under way, which ends the visit (§8.9);
- any action that would leave you on a tile marked for damage by a special that resolves before your next decision (`Specials.threat`, §8.1), saying whose special it is and what it would deal you. A due line not yet marked, such as one a chime still to come will mark, is named as its owner will mark it, never as a marked tile, and as one that can reach that tile, since its tiles are fixed only when it is marked ("Stay where a line can strike?"), so a long action warned of a chime names the chime's line and its damage (M4, D7.3); each other special that would strike that tile before your next decision follows, in the order they go off, with what it would deal and whether it is marked yet.

A confirmation is a sheet over the board, saying what the action would do in `Text.confirm`'s words (`main.js` decides when to ask, never what to say), with a button of at least 44 px that does it and one that cancels; Enter does it, and Escape or a tap beside the sheet cancels. An action `step()` would refuse is never asked about: its refusal says why.

**Everything works with one button:**
- no right-click, long-press, hover-only information (no tooltip, no hover style that tells anything) or scroll wheel;
- zoom has buttons; a label longer than its panel scrolls by the panel's own scrollbar, which a mouse drags (§7.2);
- a test drives the whole slice with left clicks only, and another with a finger's taps only on a touch screen (§10).

**Touch only.** Every verb is a tap on what is on screen, every control at least 44 px (§7.2). A touch that begins on the board ends there, so the click the browser makes of a tap never answers a sheet the tap has just opened. A press on the board is a tap only where it lifts within 16 px of where it went down: a swipe or a drag across the board (a finger's or a mouse's) is no tap, so it never walks you or strikes at the tile where it happens to lift, while a swipe on anything that scrolls (the Bag, the label sheet, an overlay, the laptop's log) scrolls it and presses nothing.

**Keyboard only.** Every control is a button: Tab reaches it, Enter or Space presses it, and the keys below do the rest without it. A button that has a key shows it (after its words, or in its corner where the button is small), except on a touch screen until a key is pressed; "How the museum works" lists them all.
- **The focus ring:** a focused control has a ring of two bands, 3 px of yellow outside 2 px of near-black, so one band stands out at 3:1 or more on the dark panels and the other on label paper; the board draws the same ring inside its edge. A box that scrolls by the keys (the laptop's log and the label panel, each while it holds more than it shows) has it too: Tab reaches it, and while it has the focus the arrows, Page Up and Page Down, Home and End scroll it and never step.
- **Held keys and Caps Lock:** a held key acts in the layer it went down in: once another opens (the confirmation its step asked for, the end of a visit), its repeats are dropped, so a held step key cannot move the focus off the confirmation's yes, and a held Enter does one thing. Caps Lock changes no key.
- **A button's key** stays clear of its words at every text size: a key in a corner grows with the text no further than 10 px.
- **Layers:** while one is open (the confirmation, the Window Pole's sheet, Settings, "How the museum works", the end of a visit, the Bag or the floor plan), everything else is inert: no Tab, key, click or tap reaches what lies behind it, and keys do nothing to the game. It takes the focus when it opens (the confirmation its button that does it, the Bag its first item or button, Settings its first chosen value, the end of a visit "Another visit") and gives it back when it closes, to where it was, or else to the board. On a phone the open label covers the strip, Zoom and the bar, which are inert while it does, so Tab never reaches a control the sheet hides.
- **Keys that act on the board** (a step, Wait, `x`, `r`, a verb, a quickslot, zoom) move the focus to the board, unless it is on the label, so Enter then acts on the board, not on a button Tab had reached.

**The keys** (none of the verbs' letters is a vi-key, and no key does two things in one place):

| Keys | What they do |
|---|---|
| Arrows, `h j k l y u b n`, or the numpad (with NumLock on or off) | Step; into an exhibit, attack it (as bumping does). While aiming at a tile, move the aimed tile instead |
| `.` or numpad 5 | Wait |
| `z` | Rest, repeated as Rest is |
| `s` | Sit on a bench |
| `>` | Leave by the exit you stand on (it asks first) |
| `x`, Shift+`X` | Select the next, or the previous, thing in sight, nearest first (king moves; an exhibit from its nearest tile; on a tie exhibits, cases, items on the floor, identifying labels, staff doors, then exits, each kind in row order), and yourself last; its label opens. An exit is selected as a walk there: its path is shown, and Enter walks. While aiming, the next or previous tile the aim can be used at, those with an exhibit first |
| Enter | The selected thing's first action, as a second tap (Attack; Open, or Break the glass; Take; Read; Wait), and with none, what a second tap on its tile (an exhibit's own) does: walk onto it where you can stand (an item on the floor, an opened case, a staff door), else the next thing there, else select it again with its label shown (a far shut case or a far exhibit, which a second tap does not walk to either); never for an item you carry. A previewed walk goes. A confirmation's yes. While aiming, a second tap on the aimed tile, or with no tile aimed yet, the first tile the aim can be used at. On the recap or the end of a visit, "Another visit" (once closed, Enter shows it again) |
| `a o c g t e d q r` | The selected label's buttons, each showing its key: `a` Attack (an item's Use at a tile… or Use on an item…), `o` Open (a case, a staff door), `c` Close (a staff door), `g` Break the glass, `t` Take (Take off, Throw…), `e` Equip, `d` Drop, `q` Drink or Use, `r` Read |
| `r` | With no Read button on the selected label: select the nearest label (as before) |
| Tab, Shift+Tab | Move the focus; Enter or Space presses the button it is on |
| Arrows, Home, End, in a layer | Move the focus through its buttons (the Bag's items, a kit's items, the Card's seals, Settings' choices, a sheet's buttons); in one with no buttons but its Close ("How the museum works", the floor plan) they scroll it |
| Backspace | Back, in the Bag |
| `i`, `m` | The Bag, the floor plan; pressed again, closed |
| `1`–`6` | The quickslots; on the Window Pole's sheet, its choices in order |
| `+`, `-` | The next or previous zoom step (the Zoom button steps forward) |
| `?`, `,` | "How the museum works", Settings |
| Escape | Cancel the confirmation; close what lies on top (the Window Pole's sheet, keeping the aim; "How the museum works" or Settings over the Bag or the end of a visit); else close every overlay and the Bag, stop aiming, and let go of the selection |

**How a keyboard player reaches each verb and screen:**
- **Moving and fighting:** the step keys; a step into an exhibit attacks it; or `x` to the exhibit and Enter (or `a`). A long walk: `x` to an exit, or to a far item on the floor, an opened case or a staff door, and Enter, as a second tap walks there; to a far shut case or exhibit, as with taps, the steps (or a tap on a tile beside it).
- **Selecting anything in sight:** `x` and Shift+`X` (exhibits, cases, items on the floor, identifying labels, staff doors, exits, yourself); an item in your tote: `i`, the arrows, Enter.
- **A case:** `x` to it; `o` opens it, `g` breaks its glass (each blow asks: Enter, or Escape), `t` takes what it holds; Enter does the first of them; on an opened, empty one Enter walks you in.
- **An item on the floor:** `x`, then `t` (or Enter). **An identifying label:** `x` to it, then `r` (or Enter) reads it. (`r` alone selects the nearest label in the strip's order, §8.8, so an exhibit or a case within reading distance comes before an identifying label.)
- **A staff door:** `x`, then `o` opens it (as a step into it does), `c` closes it.
- **What you carry:** `i`, the arrows to an item, Enter; then its keys: `e` Equip, `t` Take off, `d` Drop, `q` Drink or Use, `r` Read (a Catalogue Card, a Floor Plan), `t` Throw…, `a` Use at a tile… or Use on an item…; Backspace goes back.
- **Aiming at a tile** (a throw, the fork, the Pin-Driver, the Window Pole), from Throw… or the instrument's quickslot (`1`): the step keys move the aimed tile a step, each a first tap there (its marks and words, or the refusal), `x` jumps to the next tile it can be used at, Enter does it there (a second tap, through the confirmations), Escape stops aiming. On the Window Pole's sheet, `1`–`3` (or the arrows and Enter) choose push, pull or take; Escape closes it and the aim stays.
- **A Restoration kit:** its Use on an item… (`a`); the arrows to an item, Enter shows what a level would give it, Enter again uses the kit on it.
- **A Catalogue Card's choice:** the Bag opens on it; the arrows to a seal, Enter.
