- **The floor plan:** `m`. **The quickslots:** `1`–`6`.
- **Wait, Rest, Sit, Leave:** `.`, `z`, `s`, `>`; your label (`x` to yourself) shows them with their keys.
- **Every confirmation:** Enter does it, Escape cancels.
- **The recap and the end of a visit:** Enter presses "Another visit"; Escape closes it, and Enter shows it again.
- **Settings:** `,`, then the arrows and Enter: the board (Flat or 3D), zoom, walking, text size, label font, motion. **"How the museum works":** `?`; it lists every key of the table above, and a check holds the two to the same keys. **Zoom:** `+` and `-`.

### 7.2 Screens

**Portrait phone (390×844 CSS pixels):**
- **Top bar:**
  - health;
  - the stage chip ("Open: bell in N turns", "After the bell: night watch in N turns", "Night watch: next bell in N turns", and while a Centrepiece hall is sealed "Sealed: the clock is stopped", with ", the chime still sounds" while `hourly_demonstration` is on, in `Text.stage`'s words; the dial's sentence then says the clock is stopped, and that what comes next comes once it has counted that many more turns, the boundary that opens the shutters counting the first) and your statuses, each its pictogram (`Registry.STATUSES[id].icon`) with the turns it still covers, on a second row on a phone;
  - the clock dial, with the bell and night-watch segments and module countdowns beside it, its hand the clock's count and its label `Text.clock`'s sentence;
  - the grade badge;
  - the alarm bell, when a department owns one.
- **The board.** Nothing of the interface lies over it, so no button can cover a tile or a mark.
- **A two-row strip:** a short title with the state and pips, then pictograms. In runs 1–3 the Behaviour line follows (DESIGN default 24). The newest log entry (all the sentences one action produced) takes its place until the next action. Zoom is a button at the strip's right end.
- **Bottom bar:** Wait · Bag · Q1 · Q2 · Q3 · Map.
- **The label panel** opens as a bottom sheet. While it is open the camera centres you and the selected exhibit in the part of the board it leaves clear (if both cannot fit, it keeps you in view and leans towards the exhibit). Beside the exhibit, if you and it already fit in that part round the camera the board last used, the board stays put, so on a laptop a second click on the same point strikes it; on a phone, opening the sheet can move the board when they do not fit above it. Closing the label keeps the selection, so tapping the exhibit again still acts. An open label follows every action while what it names is in view (`ViewModel.shows`: an exhibit the view model lists; a case, staff door or identifying label on a tile in view; an item you carry or one in view); once it is gone or out of sight the label closes and the selection is dropped, since a label shows only what sight gives (§8.8), never an exhibit's health, mode or statuses behind a wall.
- **A label longer than its panel** (M4 FU1; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable), as a Centrepiece's whole line is at the largest text (its strip shows its phase alone and sends you here: "Its label has the rest.", §5.3), scrolls with each input alone, and is read to the end of its Behaviour line so: a swipe on it (it presses nothing); the keys, as for the laptop's log: Tab reaches the panel itself (`#sheet`, a region named "The label", with the focus ring), and there the arrows, Page Up and Page Down, Home, End and Space scroll it and never step, and written again after an action it keeps the focus and its place; a mouse with no wheel, by the panel's own scrollbar (`overflow-y: auto`; 15 px wide in Chromium on a desktop, at phone and laptop size), its thumb dragged. `check-longlabel` reads a planted Centrepiece line of 23 strip rows (the Great Board's as the plan describes it) to its end with each input alone, at 390×844 and 1280×800, nothing acting on the game. Until the strip shows a Centrepiece's short strip (LABELS-M4), its whole line in the laptop's strip, above the panel, would leave the panel 34 px, so the check reads it from a step past reading distance.

**Laptop:** the board on the left, with Wait · Bag · Map and Zoom under it; the strip with the last six log entries (newest kept in view), the label panel and six quickslots in a right column.

**The Bag** (`js/ui/bag.js`), opened by the bar's Bag or `i`:
- **What it shows:** what you wear, slot by slot, then your tote. A tap on an item shows its label (§8.8) with its buttons, each acting through the confirmations (§7.1). A Catalogue Card's choice, one button for each seal you do not know, opens the Bag by itself and stays at its top until it is answered; closed, it opens again when anything else is tried, since nothing else can be done first.
- **Quickslots** hold your instrument, then your consumables in the order of your tote: three on a phone, six on a laptop. An instrument's quickslot aims it (pressed again, it stops aiming); a consumable's shows it in the Bag with its buttons; an empty one opens the Bag.
- **Aiming at a tile** (a throw, an instrument): on a phone the strip's place, on a laptop the bar under the board, says what you aim and what a tap does, at a height set by the text size alone (enough for its longest words, in either input's words), so the board never moves between taps. Its words are for the input last used: a tap's ("Tap a tile within 5 tiles, then tap it again to throw"; a click is a tap), or the keys' ("Move the mark within 5 tiles with the arrows or x, then press Enter to throw"; "Press Enter to throw it here"); a Restoration kit's list says the same of its items. The first tap on a tile marks it (with a thrown Solution's square, §6.1) and says what a second tap would do there, or the refusal `step()` would give; Enter does what a second tap would. With the keys, a step key moves the marked tile one step (from your tile before any) as a first tap there, `x` and Shift+`X` step through the tiles it can be used at, and Enter, with no tile marked yet, marks the first of them (§7.1). The second tap does it, through the confirmations, or, where it cannot be done, is refused with that sentence, and the aim stays for another tile, as it does when a confirmation is cancelled. The aim ends once time passes, by its action or another (a Wait), so the strip it stands in for shows what happened; Cancel or Escape ends it too. Where the Window Pole can do more than one thing, the second tap opens a sheet with a button for each, in the order push, pull, take (keys `1`–`3`); while it asks, keys do nothing to the game, and a tap beside it, its Cancel or Escape closes it, the aim kept.
- **A Restoration kit** is aimed in the Bag: the items it can be used on are listed in `Kit.useLegal`'s order (what you wear in slot order, then your tote); a first tap on one shows what a level would give it, and a second uses the kit on it.
- **The floor plan** (the bar's Map or `m`): the department as you have seen and remember it, with you, the exits and the entrance; once a Floor Plan has been read in this department, all of it, with the rooms whose Highlights have not been taken outlined and numbered. A door, a staff door and a case each have a shape there as well as a tint (a band across the tile; a band with a dot; a frame, filled while the case is shut), and so does a hall's shutter (M4: a light tile striped across, never a wall's, which the key names while one is shut).

**The Orientation's coach** (`js/ui/coach.js`, with the steps in `js/ui/teacher.js`; §8.9). While the Orientation runs, a line of its own at the foot of the top bar, on a phone and a laptop alike, so it never lies over the board and the label sheet never covers it: the step count ("2 of 7"), the step's one sentence, and Skip (44 px), which starts the visit. The sentence stays there until its step is done, so it never scrolls away. Its words are those of the input used last: taps for a touch or a pen, clicks for a mouse (`pointerType`), and §7.1's keys once a key other than a modifier is pressed; it is redrawn when the input changes. Before any input, a screen whose main pointer is coarse (`(pointer: coarse)`: a phone, a tablet) gets taps, and any other (a laptop, whose player may click or press keys) gets the first step's sentence for both ("Click a tile to walk there, or press an arrow key to take a step."), following the screen if it changes until an input is used; only the first step can show before any input. A screen reader hears each new sentence through the label panel's live region, the page's one, and the sentence again in a new input's words. A sentence says only what is so when it shows, in any order of play: the exhibit's label step calls it the exhibit, since it may have woken already, and the case's step names Open only while the case is shut (once it is opened or broken, Take alone). The view model frames what the step asks you to use (§6.1, §6.2), and a new sentence flashes once, except with reduced motion. The teacher only reads: it is given the view model and the events of each step, keeps what it has seen in a memory of its own, and never touches the state. A step whose goal is met, now or earlier, or can no longer be met (a fallen exhibit's label), is passed at once, so the steps hold in any order, and the coach blocks no verb. On a phone the board keeps 40% of the window's height with the coach showing at every text size.

**Other screens:**
- the Entrance Hall;
- door placards;
- the Stair Hall;
- the perk choice;
- the death label and its recap (in M3 the recap, `Text.recap`, is an overlay with "Another visit", which starts the same department at the same depth with the next seed until M5's run; leaving shows the end of the visit's sentence with the same button; in the Orientation the button is "Start the visit", beside "Practice again");
- Your Collection;
- the Catalogue;
- Settings: the board (Flat or 3D), zoom (one choice for each of the board's zoom steps), walking, text size, label font and motion;
- "How the museum works" (the nine sentences with their icons, then the keys, one tap from any screen; Practice room, beside its Close, plays the Orientation again).

**Accessibility:**
- **Colour.** At most six meaning-carrying colours, and shape always carries the meaning too.
- **Text.** Size steps; a readable-font option.
- **Motion.** A reduced-motion setting.
- **Sound.** Captions for every informative sound.
- **Off-screen threats.** Edge markers.
- **Telegraphs.** Auto-pan when a telegraph targets you.
- **Targets.** No interactive UI target (buttons, strip, panel, quickslots) smaller than 44×44 CSS pixels. Board tiles are exempt at the default zoom (32 px; D12 leaves comfortable tapping to zoom), but each view's zoom table includes a step with a disc of at least 44 px inside each tile (48 px in top-down, s = 52 in 3D), and the screen test checks that it exists.

---

## 8. Rules of record

These are the rules the slice implements. Each system opens with its sentence from [`DECISIONS.md`](DECISIONS.md).

### 8.1 Moving and fighting

*"Tap to move; bump into something to attack it. Ordinary blows roll dice, and the odds are shown before you commit. Dangerous moves are marked on the floor with their exact damage, and never miss. Your health recovers slowly."*

**Time:**
- **Ticks.** A turn is 100 ticks. Every awake actor has an integer `next`. The scheduler takes the smallest; ties go to you, then to actors in spawn order (§3.3). Sleeping exhibits are not scheduled.
- **Turn boundaries.** Turn k's world effects resolve at tick 100k, before any actor whose `next` is 100k acts, in this order:
  1. surfaces (a Fumigant cloud takes a pip from each sleeping exhibit inside; fog and rain in M4);
  2. status timers (statuses that have run their turns end);
  3. regeneration;
  4. wake checks (your ring, `axis`, `tether`), from your tile at tick 100k and the ring radius of your last action that cost time, then the chime's wakings (`period`, M4, §5.4); then the statuses' per-turn effects (a cough's noise, restoring's healing), so that a noise they make comes after the wake checks (§8.2, one action's wake order);
  5. pip decay;
  6. the Centrepiece hall's seal (M4), then the clock stage;
  7. alarm decay;
  8. module turn rules, which first set off the due lines whose boundary this is (`Specials.resolveDue`, M4; Specials, below).

  Each step is one named function, called in this order by `Step.boundary` and nowhere else: `Surfaces.tick`; `Status.tick`, then `Kit.recharge`; `Health.regenerate`; `Wake.boundary`, then `Status.effects`; `Wake.decay`; `Hall.boundary`, then `ClockRules.boundary`; `Modules.alarmDecay`; `Modules.turn`. Noise, damage and the other event triggers act at once, when their event happens.
- **Every action of yours that costs time costs at least a turn** (100 ticks; a Heavy blow costs 150), so at least one turn boundary runs between any two of your decisions, after your action has moved you and set your ring, unless a perk made it free or cheaper. M4's two perks for that are the only exceptions: once `apply` has given an action's cost, `step()` asks the perks you hold (`Perks.adjust`, for an action that costs time), and accepts 0 ticks only for an action that costs no time (`Actions.free`) or a step that Close the Distance made free (a move that changed your tile), at least `TICKS_PER_TURN − PERK_DUCK_AND_COVER` (50) only for an action Duck and Cover made cheaper, and at least a turn otherwise; anything else stops it with an error. After a free or cheaper action your next decision can come before the next boundary, so the ring a boundary applies counts every action since the one before (§8.2, Your ring). Statuses still count turns (below), so a status of N turns covers N turns however many actions you fit into them.
- **An exhibit's action is charged after it acts:** its `next` becomes next + the action's cost, or the tick its action set in `notBefore`, if that is later (a special's mark holds its resolve until after your next decision; Specials, below). Nothing an exhibit does in its own turn writes its own `next`. Time only moves forward: every action of an exhibit costs time, and `step()` stops with an error on one that costs none, or on an actor scheduled before the present tick (one made during the visit without its beat).
- **Waking costs a beat.** An exhibit that wakes is scheduled to first act at now + its action cost. So is an actor made during the visit (the Night Watchman, a splitter's halves), which therefore never acts in the instant it appears.
- **An exhibit's turn,** in this order (`AI.act`): if it is fixed, its pending special is called off and it waits, and if it is staggered (M4) it waits, a mark it shows waiting with it; otherwise it looks for you (it sees you as §8.2 says, and if it was not already hunting it now hunts you); a pending special resolves (but a due line waits for its boundary, and its owner waits: Specials, below); its archetype acts, if it has something to do; otherwise it acts as its mode says (§8.2). Every turn it takes, a forced wait included, counts as one of its actions (`acts`).
- **Clockwork** (M4; the `clockwork` modifier, `rules/mods/clockwork.js`; plan D3.12, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). A clockwork exhibit shows its next move as an arrow and makes it if it still can, otherwise it waits; it cannot be dazed (`Archetypes.immune`: `Status.apply` leaves `dazed` off it).
  - **Its next move** (`a.plan`, shown by `Archetypes.plan`, which the board draws as its arrow (plan VM-2D), and in words on its label, which never speak of the arrow: "Its next move: a step north-east."; a mark names whose tile it is on only for an exhibit you see, else its tiles, "1 tile"; a station's: "If the chime sounded now, it would mark its line: 9 tiles to the east.", or, while it is held fixed, when a chime makes it mark nothing (§5.4), "If the chime sounded now, it would mark nothing: it is fixed in place.") is the move it would make if it acted now, worked out on a copy of the state that is thrown away, right after each of its actions and when it wakes: a step onto a tile beside it (a closed door there opened, as any exhibit's step into one opens it), a blow at you, its special marked on given tiles, its pending mark going off, or a wait. Asleep it shows nothing; one made awake (a room for tests) shows its first move after its first action, a wait.
  - **At its action** (its turn's archetype step): if what it would do now is that very move, it acts as its archetype does, so a change of mode that comes with the move (giving up a search, arriving home) comes too; otherwise, if it still can make that move (the tile still beside it, free and one it may step onto; you still beside it for its blow; its archetype's own test for a mark, the healer's below), it makes it alone; otherwise it waits. Then it shows its next move. A fixed clockwork exhibit only waits, as any does, and keeps the move it showed.
  - **Which exhibits.** A modifier acts after its archetype (§5.3), so clockwork makes the moves of an archetype that acts by its mode alone (the plodder), or that hands it its action while it is on (`Clockwork.add`: the healer). A station (`demonstrator` + `clockwork`) acts by its own rule, a wait, and its arrow is its line: the tiles its line would take if it were marked now (`HourlyDemonstration.line`; none with the chime off, when it never strikes). Any other archetype with clockwork is refused when the exhibit is made, so no line claims the rule for an exhibit that would not keep it.
  - Its clause reads "Makes the move its arrow shows if it still can, else waits; cannot be dazed.": the same rule as "shows its next move as an arrow and makes it if it still can, otherwise it waits", in fewer words, so that M.11's gallery station's line keeps the phone strip's 14 rows with `weather_cases`' part on it (MOD-WEATHER's review, decided in play). Rain on clockwork is `weather_cases`' rule (§5.4), and acts only on an exhibit flagged with that module, whether or not clockwork is switched off; its part on a flagged line is a sentence of its own, "Rain stops it.", and the label of a case that holds rain (§8.3), the log as it happens and the station's label while it holds say how and for how long.
- **Speed.** Fast actors pay half, slow actors double, for their steps; attacks and every other action cost the usual, unless the archetype says its speed covers every action (the mimic is slow at everything). The `slowed` status doubles every action again. An actor's speed is its archetype's, or a modifier's (marble is slow) unless that modifier is switched off; a fast archetype with a slow modifier moves at the usual speed.
- **Surfaces.** A surface laid for N turns (a Fumigant cloud, plaster dust; in M4 fog and rain, let out of a case, §5.4) acts at the next N turn boundaries and is cleared at the boundary after them; while it lies, a sight-blocking one blocks line of sight across its square (§8.2). At each of those boundaries a Fumigant cloud takes 1 pip from every sleeping exhibit with a tile under it, at most one a turn however many clouds cover it, and nothing from one with none; plaster dust and fog only block sight; rain blocks nothing, and as it is laid stops the exhibits flagged with `weather_cases` that stand in it (§5.4).
- **Statuses.** A status of N turns covers the N turns that start at its bearer's next decision. Once the action that put it on is over (or the boundary, for one put on as a boundary runs: M4's `second_wind`, brought by a line due there; `Step.boundary` settles it as it ends), it lasts until the boundary of turn `idiv(next, 100) + N`, where `next` is the bearer's next decision (for a sleeping exhibit, the next turn + N); its per-turn effect acts at the boundaries of those N turns, and it ends at the boundary of the last. A decision at tick t falls in turn `idiv(t, 100)`, because that turn's boundary runs before anyone who acts at its tick. A status can be settled when its bearer's next decision falls in a turn whose boundary has already run (mid-turn: after an action Duck and Cover made cheaper, §8.7, or after a Heavy blow), so the first of its N turns has no boundary left for its per-turn effect; two distinct rules keep what such a status gives (M4; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). Coughing and restoring, which only your own drink puts on (a Fumigant or a Consolidant, so this happens only when Duck and Cover made the drink cheaper), count their N turns from the turn after it, so every cough and share comes at the very tick the drink at its full cost would give it: the discount moves only your decisions, never the drink's course (PG-PERKS-TIMING). `second_wind`, which the world's turn brings, keeps its turns and pays that first turn's share at the next boundary instead (below; PG-PERKS-B). Every other status keeps its start. So Fixative gives exactly 4 forced waits whatever the action before it (more only when Duck and Cover makes one of them cheaper, M4: a status counts turns, not actions), and a grip of 2 turns put on during a 200-tick open still holds you at your next 2 decisions. Put on again while held, a status lasts to the later end. What each does: `fixed`, its bearer can only wait (you may wait, rest or answer a choice already waiting), and blows against it have +20 to hit; `rooted`, you cannot step off your tile, though a bump that leaves you where you are (into a door, an actor or a case) is still yours; `coughing`, a soft noise at its bearer's tile at each of its turns, the bearer's own noise; `restoring`, its share of healing at each of its turns, 25% of maximum health over 5 turns in shares within 1 of one another, every 5 of them adding up to the total, never past maximum health (put on again while held, it heals at the same rate for longer); `slowed`, every action takes 200% of its time; `dazed`, its bearer's blows have −20 to hit; and M4's perks' statuses (§8.7; `reg/perk-status.js`, `Status.define`): `staggered` (Vanishing Act's and Single File's; with `skipsAction`), its bearer loses its next action: for `STAGGER_TURNS` (1) turn from its next decision it only waits, neither looking for you nor letting a mark it shows go off at its action (the mark waits with it, and the threat, below, counts the wait; a line due at a boundary, §5.4's chime, goes off there as ever), and since no exhibit's wait takes less than a turn (`COST_WAIT`, which no speed halves), that turn holds exactly one of its decisions, so it loses that one action and acts at the one after, whatever its speed; and `second_wind` (Second Wind's), its share of the health Second Wind heals at each of its turns, in shares within 1 of one another adding up to its total, never past maximum health; its first turn's share, when Second Wind comes after that turn's boundary has healed (your next decision mid-turn, or a line due at that boundary), comes at the next boundary with the next turn's, so no share is lost; it is healing, not regeneration, so neither the night watch nor a sealed hall stops it.

**Action costs (ticks):**

| Action | Ticks |
|---|---|
| Step | 100 |
| Wait or rest | 100 |
| Attack | 100 (Heavy weapon or brute 150) |
| Open or close a door | 100 |
| Open a case | 200 |
| Bash glass | 100 (Heavy 150, counting as two bashes) |
| Take or drop | 100 |
| Use or throw | 100 |
| Equip, or take off (unequip) | 100 (garment 300) |
| Push a jar | 100 (reserved: `push` is cut from M4, after the gate, Q7) |
| Sit | 100, then rest at double rate |
| View | 100 per viewing turn |
| Read, examine | 0 |

**Hit and damage:**
- **Hit chance:** `clamp(75 + 5 × (Acc_attacker − Eva_defender) + mods, 10, 95)`. Roll d100 on the attacker's combat stream; a roll at or below the chance hits.
- **Modifiers:** +20 against a fixed target; −20 for a dazed attacker.
- **Damage:** a uniform roll in [min, max], minus the defender's Padding, at least 1 on a hit. Its sentence in the log shows the sum (the roll against the chance, the damage less Padding) and anything the defender's archetype or modifiers changed: through an `old_repair` exhibit's repaired side the damage is doubled, and a body that falls to any one blow loses all its health.
- **No critical hits.**
- **Shots** (a kiter's, the Pin-Driver's) are rolled as blows are. A shot's cause names it a shot (or, fired by an instrument, the instrument) with its range, and its damage comes from the last tile the bolt crossed before its target; an instrument's bolt is made with its own damage range and your Acc without the weapon's.
  - **A bolt** is aimed at a tile in line of sight within its range (a disc, as "within r" is, §8.2: `ROLE_ARTILLERY_RANGE` for an exhibit, the Pin-Driver's for you) and hits the first body on the line toward it. It passes only what can be seen through and is not glass, so nothing is shot into, out of or through a case. A shot is refused before it costs anything if its bolt would meet glass first (`behind_glass`, so a case, an exhibit in one and a mimic's disguise look the same), nothing at all (`no_target`), or if its tile is outside its range (`out_of_reach`) or out of sight (`blocked`).
  - **A reflector** (the `reflector` archetype, or a body with the `glass` modifier while it is on) is not hit by a bolt: it sends it back along the tiles it came by, into the first body on them, which is its shooter, so nothing behind the reflector is hit. The bolt sent back is rolled as a shot at its shooter, with the numbers it was fired with (the shooter's Acc and damage, or the instrument's), against the shooter's own Eva and Padding, on its own stream, never a surprise; it comes back at most once, and its way back does not count toward its range. Its cause is the reflector's (`reflected`), naming who fired it (`shooter`), and its damage comes from the tile next to the shooter on its way back. The log calls what was sent back a shot, or for a special's line the noun its registry entry gives (a station's line, M4), else a beam.
  - **The kiter** keeps `KITER_HOLD` (3 to 5) steps from you, counted as it would walk to you, while it hunts and sees you. Its posts are the tiles 3 to 5 steps from you with a clear shot (you within its range, in line of sight, and the first body on its bolt's line); it walks to a post only by a way that never comes nearer than 3 steps to you, and its nearest post is the nearest by that way, ties in row-major order. Beside you, or nearer than 3 steps, it backs away; farther than 5, it walks toward its nearest post. Then, if it did not move and has a clear shot, it shoots; otherwise, 3 steps or more from you, it walks toward its nearest post, or, farther than 5 with no post to walk to, steps toward you; failing all of these it waits. So it never comes nearer than 3 steps of its own accord, and cornered beside you it shoots from there. With no way to walk to you it shoots whenever it has a clear shot, and otherwise acts as a plodder does, as it does out of sight or in an intact case.
- **Reach.** A Reach weapon also strikes a target exactly `REACH_RANGE` (2) tiles away in one of the 8 directions, over the tile between, which must be one you could walk onto and see through (not a wall, a closed door or a case, and not under a Fumigant cloud, plaster dust or M4's fog, which block sight) with no actor on it (refused `blocked`); a diagonal Reach blow keeps the corner rule on both of its halves (`corner`). So Reach strikes only what you can see. A target beside you is struck as usual. A Reach blow is a blow like any other: it comes from your tile, costs your weapon's blow, and never reaches into a case.
- **Surprise.** An attack hits automatically for maximum damage (×1.5, rounded down, with a Light weapon's melee blow) on an exhibit that is asleep or stirring, or awake and has not seen you since it last woke; any blow of yours can surprise, a Reach blow or a shot included. The target then hunts you. Searching, returning and reinstalling exhibits are never surprised, but by the surprise attack Duck and Cover leaves waiting at its second rank, which catches any exhibit but the Night Watchman whatever it is doing (M4, §8.7). Against a Centrepiece a surprise blow hits automatically but rolls damage, and it starts the encounter.

**Specials** (telegraphed): one action marks the tiles, each showing the exact damage you would take. The exhibit's next action resolves the special, with no rolls, and costs an attack plus `SPECIAL_RECOVERY` (100), so the exhibit's action after that comes 200 ticks after the resolve: marked at tick 400, resolved at 500, its next action at 700. Every special number in §8.10 is gross: a marked tile shows, and the special deals, `max(DMG_MIN_ON_HIT, gross − your Padding)`, recomputed from your Padding at every view-model update, so what resolves is what the floor showed the moment before. Specials ignore Eva.
- **Who marks.** Only a hunting exhibit marks a special, or an awake one whose archetype marks while awake (`Archetypes.marksAwake`: M4's stations), and only one at a time. An exhibit in an intact case may mark only a beam (which passes glass) or a call (which is sound), never a ring of blows. Nor does a ring strike into an intact case: only a beam passes glass, so a ring never marks a case's tile, and an exhibit in a case, or a mimic in its disguise, is never hit by one.
- **No unseen special.** After a mark, the exhibit's next action comes no earlier than your next decision, and ties go to you, so you always see a mark before it resolves. If you start a 300-tick equip at 400 and an exhibit marks at 400, it resolves at 700, just after your decision at 700; a fast exhibit that marks at 400 while your next decision is at 500 resolves at 500, not at 450.
- **Due lines** (M4). A special may be marked due at a turn boundary (`Specials.mark(…, {due})`, the tick of a boundary after now): it goes off at that boundary, in step 8 before the module turn rules (`Modules.turn` calls `Specials.resolveDue` first), not at its owner's action, and costs its owner nothing; until then its owner only waits (should its boundary pass with nothing to set it off, its owner's next action does). It is shown as any mark is, with its exact damage (`Specials.marks` also gives the tick it goes off), and its sentence says in how many turns. Since it may go off before your next decision, during a long action of yours, the threat warns of it (below), and of a line a module will mark and make due before then (`Modules.threat`: the chime's lines before the chime sounds), before any action that lets it go off: no line goes off on you unwarned, and none marked before your decision unshown (settled, D7.3; the invariant `combat_special_shown` holds both). A line that defeats you ends the visit at its boundary, as an exhibit's action that defeats you ends the world's turn: no line after it goes off.
- **Several specials** (M4). An exhibit with a list of specials (`ax.specials`, in the order it gained them: a Centrepiece, or one of its pieces) marks them in turn, one every `CP_SPECIAL_EVERY` of its actions (the first value in phases 1 and 2, the second from phase 3): its first at that many actions since it woke, then each no sooner than that many after the last was marked (`Specials.markNext`). The one due is the one after the one it marked last in its list, back to the first after the last, and the first if it no longer has the one it marked last. One that cannot be marked when it is due (no tiles, or the overlap rule) is tried again at its next action, which it spends as it otherwise would.
- **Shapes** (M4). Besides the ring, the call and the line, each rule registers the shapes it uses (`Specials.addShape(id, {tiles, from?, via?, glass?, after?})`), and only one that says so (`glass`, a beam) passes glass (`Specials.passesGlass`, which the invariant `combat_glass` reads); `after` is what its owner does once a damage mark of the shape has gone off (after every hit and its noise, before the special is told), while its owner stands and the visit goes on (M4: the Composite's charge). **The burst** (`cp_burst`'s shape) is the 3×3 square on your tile, aimed at you, so marked only while its owner sees you: your tile and those round it that an exhibit could stand on, never an intact case's (it is blows) nor its owner's own. **The Composite's limbs** (M4 COMPOSITE-CORE; `rules/arch/completing_guardian.js`, each a Centrepiece's gross, plan D3.5, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable) are marked only while you stand in its hall on one of the tiles the limb would mark: **its neck** (`cp_sweep`, shape `sweep`) the tiles round its footprint on your side of it (west of it when you stand west of it; both sides past a corner); **its legs** (`cp_charge`, shape `charge`) the lane on the side of its footprint you stand beside, as wide as it, of up to `CHARGE_RANGE` rows of tiles of its hall it could step onto (so never a case's, the Lamp's least of all, nor past a wall, a closed door or its hall's edge), and when the mark goes off, after striking everything on the lane, it charges down the lane row by row, its mark's ticks paying for the run, while the next row is marked, clear and stood on by no one, so it stops short of anyone the blow left standing; **its tail** (`cp_ring`, shape `tail`) all the tiles round it, as a ring; **its crest** (`cp_call`, shape `crest`; M4 COMPOSITE-PHASES) a call's tiles, those an exhibit could stand on within `HERALD_RANGE` of it, dealing no damage: when it goes off, the Composite's call wakes every sleeping exhibit on them that can hear, never a Centrepiece's piece, as a herald's does ("… marks the tiles its call reaches for its crest"). A mark and its going off are told by the shape's name (a ring of blows, a beam, a call) and the tiles it covers, or, where the special's entry gives them, by its own name (`noun`) and what it marks (`marks`): "The Lantern-Crop Wader marks its own tile for its alarm" (§5.4); any other is "its special".
- **A Centrepiece's specials** (a registry curve written `cp:KEY`) are gross `pct(MEDIAN_MAX_HP at the depth, KEY's curve at the depth) + MEDIAN_PADDING's curve at the depth` (`Specials.cpGross`), the same share of the median visitor's health through their Padding at every depth: with `ROLE_CENTREPIECE_SPECIAL_PCT`, 6, 12 and 18 at depths 1, 4 and 7.
- **Whether a mark covered you** (M4). A damage mark notes whether your tile was among its tiles when it was made (`Specials.atYou`; the mark keeps `offYou` only when it was not, so the part is absent until used, §4.1); a call never covers you. After any special goes off, the perks you hold hear of it (`Perks.onResolve`, with the mark, `atYou` and whether it struck you).
- **No overlaps.** No two pending damage marks share a tile: an exhibit whose special would mark a tile already marked for damage does not mark, and acts otherwise. A herald's call neither blocks a damage mark nor is blocked by one, and is drawn beneath them.
- **Each actor is hit once,** whoever it is, however many of its tiles are marked and however often a beam crosses its tile, each through its own Padding; the marks show only your number. A beam goes straight through glass, stops at what blocks sight (a tile that cannot be seen through, or one a Fumigant cloud, plaster dust or M4's fog covers), and turns back along its line where it meets a reflector, the way back counting toward its range, so nothing behind the reflector is hit and the shooter's own tile is on the way back: a sniper can be hit by its own beam. On the tiles a beam reaches only on its way back (its owner's tile and those behind it) the damage is the reflector's, as a bolt sent back is: its cause is `reflected`, by the reflector, naming the beam's owner as `shooter`, with the special's numbers (`sp`, `gross`, `pad`, `net`, the tile), so the log says the dish sent the beam back. A reflector on a beam's tile is struck by it, as everything on its tiles is, as it sends it back. The damage of each marked tile comes from the tile next to it that the special came from (the owner's, for a ring; the one the beam crossed before, for a beam), which is the side a blow comes from for `old_repair`.
  A beam's tiles, its way back included, are fixed when it is marked, so what comes onto them or leaves them while the mark is shown changes nothing, and what the floor showed is what resolves: a cloud or dust laid on them does not cut it; a reflector that steps or is moved onto them does not turn it, but is struck like anything else on its tiles, and what stands behind it is struck too; and one that leaves them after turning it still turns it where it stood. So a reflector shields what is behind it only from a beam marked while it is in the beam's way, as its Behaviour line says.
- **A damage special is an attack,** so its resolve makes a soft noise at its owner's tile, after its damage. A special that hits several actors applies every hit, with the wakes each causes (`ally_hit` included), before any noise, the loud noise of an exhibit it destroys included. A call makes no noise: it wakes directly.
- **The restore** (M4; the `conservator_healer`'s special, `rules/arch/conservator_healer.js`; plan D3.13, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable) is a mark that deals no damage. The healer marks the tile of the ally it sees (within `SIGHT_EXHIBIT` of a tile of it, in line of sight) that has lost the most health, the first in spawn order on a tie: any other exhibit, not gone, in no intact case (only a beam passes glass), not showing as something else (a mimic in its disguise), and missing health its restore would give back (at least 1 of its share, so not a swarm body of a few health). The mark shows that tile until the healer's next action, which comes after your next decision, as every mark's does; then every exhibit standing on a marked tile, other than the healer and in no intact case, regains `CONSERVATOR_HEAL_PCT`% of its own maximum health, rounded down, never past its maximum (`healed`, cause `restore`). So it heals what the board showed: an ally that stepped off the tile regains nothing. It marks while it hunts or is awake (`marksAwake`), and only what calls off any mark calls it off: the healer's interrupt is cut (plan D3.7, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). Its readout line says it heals an exhibit within its reach, its tile marked first; a special that deals no damage and is no call or heal is said only to deal no damage, its Behaviour line saying what it does.
- **The Ordinance's shatter** (`shatter_all`, M4 ENSEMBLE-PHASES; `rules/arch/mp_ensemble.js`; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable) is a mark that deals no damage, on the tile of every case of its owner's hall still intact, marked while it hunts, whether or not it sees you; at its owner's next action each case still intact on a marked tile breaks, with no touch, then a crash from each (§5.5, §8.3). Its readout line says only that it deals no damage, its Behaviour line saying what it does. It is spent, and leaves its owner's list, once no case of its hall is intact (§5.5), so it never holds the owner's other specials back for more than one action.
- **The Great Board's beam** (`cp_line`, M4 TABLEAU-CORE; `rules/arch/mp_tableau.js`): while its owner sees you and you stand in one of its rows or columns (a row or a column of its footprint), outside it, the line from its footprint's tile there toward you, up to `AXIS_BEAM_RANGE` tiles: a beam, which passes glass, stops before what blocks sight and turns back along itself at a reflector, its owner's own tiles and those behind it on the way back, so a dish in its way sends it into the Board. Its gross is a Centrepiece's (`cp:`), and the log calls it its beam.
- **The summons** (`summon`, M4 TABLEAU-CORE; the Great Board's from phase 2; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable) is a mark that deals no damage (effect `summon`). With `CP_SUMMON[0]` (1) or more free tiles of its owner's room (its hall) beside it (a tile an exhibit can step onto, not a closed door, with no one on it; never an opening of the hall, which is in no room), it marks up to `CP_SUMMON[1]` (2) of them, those nearest where it last knew you were (its last-known tile; Chebyshev, ties in row-major order). At its next action a body of the species its record names (`summons`: M.11's Logbook Trolley) comes onto each marked tile still free, in row-major order, so a body is kept away by standing on its mark: an exhibit of its own, `<owner uid>/<k>` (k its owner's count of bodies made, `kids`, §3.2), with that species' numbers at the depth, hunting you, its story the creation cause `summoned` `{how: 'summon', from, parent}` (§3.4), first acting a beat after it comes (§8.1); one that `without` names is made and at once gone, with no event. While the summons is marked its owner takes `CP_SUMMON_DMG_PCT` (200)% of all the damage dealt to it (`Archetypes.adjustDamage`), which your figures count (the odds readout above). Its readout line says what it does, the damage and its cadence. The log calls it its summons, and tells two or more bodies one summons calls in one sentence ("The Great Board of the Central Station called 2 bodies onto the tiles it marked, and they began hunting you."), one alone by its own creation's ("… came from the Great Board of the Central Station and began hunting you."). A body it calls is no piece of its Centrepiece (§8.4). Since such a body comes with no step, the chime's forecast (§5.4, `HourlyDemonstration.reach`) counts any reflector as able to stand in a line's way while an exhibit present has, or will gain with its phases, a summons whose body reflects (`Tableau.callsReflector`; the Logbook Trolley does not), so a forecast never under-warns.
- **The healer** (`conservator_healer`, M4) never strikes: it has no blow, and its label shows none. Hunting you, it keeps `CONSERVATOR_KEEP` (2) steps from you, counted as it would walk to you (as the kiter's `KITER_HOLD` is): nearer, it steps away (a step that takes it further from you, ties on its own stream), or, cornered, does what follows. Then it marks its restore if it can; otherwise, hunting, it waits where it is while it sees you and for `LOSE_TRACK_TURNS` after, so it never comes nearer of its own accord, and then searches as any exhibit does. In any other mode it acts as its mode says, and in an intact case it only waits. A.4's Countersigning Clerk has clockwork too, so these are the moves it shows and makes.
- **The axis_sniper** marks its `line` while it hunts and sees you, when it shares your row or column within `AXIS_BEAM_RANGE` and its beam's way out would reach you before anything that blocks sight and before any reflector (it never fires into one, which would send the beam back at it). Otherwise it walks toward the nearest tile from which it would (by its own walking distance, ties in row-major order), and waits if there is none, or if another pending damage mark holds one of its line's tiles. It has no blow and shoots no bolts.
- **Cancelling.** A pending special is called off when its owner is fixed (at once, in the action that fixes it, so the mark is neither shown nor held against other marks), stops hunting (it lost you, was quieted, or you left by the Ticket), goes back to sleep, or is destroyed: a change of mode calls one off only when it leaves its owner in no mode to mark (`Specials.marking`), so a station sent awake to look elsewhere keeps its line, and its sentence says why truly ("it went to sleep", for one put to sleep). Nothing else calls one off: a tethered warden's ring shown while you stood within its tether still goes off if you step outside it (§8.2).
- **The threat** (`Specials.threat(state, action)`) is the mark an action of yours would leave you on, when its special resolves before your next decision: the tile is where the action leaves you, your next decision comes when the action's ticks are done, as the perks you hold make them (`Perks.adjust`, asked as `step()` asks it, with the events the action made: a step made free leaves no time for anything to go off), and the special resolves first if its owner's next action comes earlier (ties go to you; a staggered owner's mark waits with it, M4, so for one its first action that no such status takes from it), or, for a due line, if its boundary comes at or before your next decision (a boundary runs before anyone who acts at its tick), whether the line is marked already or a module will mark it by then (`Modules.threat`, asked on the state as it was before the action, at every tile the line could take when it is marked: §5.4's reach for the chime's); the answer then also gives its special and the tick it goes off. Its number is what the mark shows, which is what resolves. When several would strike that tile before your next decision, the answer is the first to go off and lists the others in the order they go off (`more`). The interface asks before such an action (§7.1). A mark the action calls off (its owner fixed, quieted, or losing you to the Ticket) is no threat, and nor is any mark when the action ends the visit. Nothing you could not know before the action is looked at, so the answer gives nothing away: a mark whose owner the action destroys still counts, since whether a blow destroys is not known before it is made, and so does a line a module would mark for that owner, since a module's lines are forecast from the state before the action, so nothing its roll changes (an owner, or a reflector, it destroys) changes them; and an action with a consumable whose kind you have not identified is judged as if it left you where you are and called nothing off.
- **The readout's line** for a special (`Specials.readout`) gives what it would deal you now through your Padding (none for a call), its gross number, its cadence in actions and its reach; an exhibit has a line for each of its specials (`Specials.list`), and for one whose specials take turns each comes at most one action in the cadence times the number of its specials.

**Weapons** (t is the tier):

| Form | Damage | Cost | Acc | Grade needed | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Light | [t, 3t+1] | 100 | +2 | one less | Surprise ×1.5; cannot break Laminated or Security glass |
| Balanced | [t+1, 3t+3] | 100 | 0 | base | none |
| Heavy | [t+2, 4t+4] | 150 | −1 | one more | One bash counts as two |
| Reach | [t, 3t+1] | 100 | 0 | base | Hits 2 tiles away in a straight line over an empty tile |

**Garments:**

| Form | Padding | Eva |
|---|---|---|
| Light | tier − 1 | +1 |
| Standard | tier | 0 |
| Heavy | tier + 1 | −1 |

**Grade requirements and tiers:**
- **Base grade needed for tier t:** 2t − 1 (T1 I, T2 III, T3 V, T4 VII).
- **Each grade short:**
  - a weapon gives −2 Acc;
  - a garment gives −1 Padding.
- **A need above VII,** the highest grade (§8.7), comes only from a T4 Heavy weapon or garment (VII + 1): such an item is always worn at least 1 grade short (−2 Acc for a weapon, −1 Padding for a garment), and its label says so in words, never as a grade VIII.
- **Tier follows depth,** 60/40 between the tiers on offer (`TIER_LOW_BY_DEPTH`): the lower one and the next, never above T4, the highest, so where the lower is T4 (depth 7) only T4 is offered.

**Your starting stats** (at a run's start; a single visit's visitor is §5.5's, whose grade and tier follow the depth):
- HP 20 (+5 per grade above I);
- Acc 3;
- Eva 2;
- Padding from the garment;
- a T1 Balanced weapon and a T1 Standard garment.

**Exhibits' stats** come from the role curves in §8.10, by depth (1–7, the Exit is 7), interpolated linearly between the anchors and rounded down.

**Bodies that dart, split, swarm or crumble** (their Behaviour lines say the same):
- **A darter's step back** is part of its blow: hunting and beside you, it strikes, then at once steps `DARTER_STEP_BACK` tile to an empty tile beside it that is farther from you by walking distance, never into a closed door, ties broken on its own stream; the blow and the step back together cost one blow. With no such tile it is cornered and stays.
- **A splitter splits** after any hit it survives with 2 or more health, if a tile beside it is free (the first in direction order that it could step to, not a closed door, with nobody on it): the new half takes `SPLIT_HP_PCT`% of its health, rounded down and at least 1, and it keeps the rest. Both are then one generation on, and a body of generation `SPLIT_MAX` does not split. The half copies the body's species, numbers, maximum health and speed, hunts as the body does, starts with no statuses, and has the tile it appeared on as its home; its story is `summoned`, naming how it was made (`split`), the body it came from, who hit it, and a copy of the body's own waking.
- **A swarm's bodies** each lose all their health to any hit, of whatever kind.
- **A plaster cast** has `PLASTER_HP_PCT`% of its role's health and maximum health (rounded down, at least 1; a half split from a cast keeps the cast's maximum). Destroyed, it leaves dust on its tile, `PLASTER_DUST_SIZE` tiles a side, for `PLASTER_DUST_TURNS` turns.

**The odds readout** (on examine, live), all integer through `int.js`. Let d(r) = max(1, r − Padding) be the net damage of a roll r, and n the number of rolls in the range:
- Its chance to hit you, in percent.
- "Typically" = 100 × Σ d(r) ÷ (n × your current HP), rounded half up, as a percentage of your current health.
- "At worst" = ceilDiv(your HP, max d(r)) hits.
- An archetype whose attacks are shots (the kiter, which never strikes: cornered beside you it shoots, by its rules above): its first line says it shoots, within `ROLE_ARTILLERY_RANGE`, with its Acc and damage range used as a blow's would be, so the chance, "typically" and "at worst" are its shots'; no line tells of a blow.
- Your chance to hit it, and hits needed = ceilDiv(its HP × n, Σ d(r)) with your rolls against its Padding; a body that loses all its health to any hit (a swarm's) needs 1. A splitter that can still split (below generation `SPLIT_MAX`, one tile in size) counts the hits on it and on every half it splits off, and its line says "to destroy it and every half it splits off": each hit deals the mean net hit, Σ d(r) ÷ n, with health counted in n-ths of a point so that the count stays exact in integers; after a hit a body survives with 2 or more health below generation `SPLIT_MAX`, `SPLIT_HP_PCT`% of what it has left (rounded down, at least 1 health) goes to a new half, both a generation on, and each half is counted the same way; each body's last hit is spent on it whole. It assumes room beside it for every split. When no split would come before it falls, the count is the plain one and the line says "to destroy it". Your figures (the hits needed, the surprise figure and the Attack button's damage) are those of a blow from your tile as its archetype and modifiers take it (`Archetypes.adjustDamage`), so through an `old_repair` exhibit's repaired side each d(r) is doubled, and so it is while the Great Board's summons is marked (M4, §8.1's summons), when a line after this one says why ("Its summons is marked: until it goes off, it takes 200% of the damage dealt to it, and your figures count it.", from `Archetypes.readout`'s `taken`; the Attack button's damage counts it too).
- With Reach (a Reach weapon, or any weapon with the reach primitive, a Light one included, §8.5), a line saying that your weapon also strikes it with Reach from `REACH_RANGE` away in a straight line, over a clear tile.
- Its surprise figure, while a blow now would surprise it (Duck and Cover's waiting surprise attack included, M4: `Combat.surprised` is asked with the state, §8.7); with Reach, when a surprise Reach blow from `REACH_RANGE` away would deal otherwise (only a Light weapon's blow beside it deals ×1.5), both figures.
- One line per special, with its exact net damage and cadence.
- An exhibit with no blow (its archetype says so through `Archetypes.readout`: the axis_sniper, which "never strikes"; M4's stations, which have "no blow", and the healer, which "never strikes") shows none of the first three lines, nor its Acc and damage among its pictograms, since it never uses them: what it threatens you with is told by its special's line. Your chance to hit it, the hits needed and the surprise line stay. An exhibit in an intact case, which never strikes out (§8.3), keeps its lines, which say what its blows would do once it is out, but not the surprise line: no blow of yours reaches it now (`behind_glass`), and getting it out wakes it (breaking the case wakes a sleeper inside hunting; opening it beside the exhibit fills its pips), so no blow could catch it as that line would say.

**Health:**
- **Regeneration:** 1 HP every `max(4, floor(400 / maxHP))` turns (the interval). It is kept as a store of units (`regen`), so that halving and doubling the rate stay exact on an odd interval: at boundary step 3, below full health, the store gains `REGEN_BELL_MULT` (2) units a turn, and when it reaches `REGEN_BELL_MULT` times the interval it gives you 1 HP and gives up that many units. At full health the store is empty, so nothing builds up to spend later. So at 20 maximum health you regain 1 every 20 turns; at 30, every 13, and on a bench every 7 and 6 turns in turn.
- **Benches** double it while you sit on one (4 units a turn); standing on one does not. `sit` is legal on a bench when you are not seated already (refused `no_bench` or `seated`) and costs `COST_SIT`; waiting and resting keep you seated, and any other action that costs time gets you up. `rest` is a wait under its own name, which the interface repeats.
- **After the bell** the interval is multiplied by `REGEN_BELL_MULT`, so it doubles (1 unit a turn; 2 on a bench). Regeneration runs before the clock's step, so on the bell's own boundary it is still the open rate.
- **In the night watch,** and while a Centrepiece hall is sealed, regeneration stops (no units).
- **Healing** that is not regeneration (Consolidant, restoring, a warden back home, M4's healer's restore and Second Wind) is a `healed` event with its cause, and never takes anyone past their maximum; when nothing is regained, nothing is said.
- **Death** opens the death label and its recap: what killed you, why it was awake, and when and how it woke, read from the killer's own story (§3.4). `Text.recap(state, nameOf)` gives the killer (the killing cause's `by`, so a bolt or beam a reflector sent back is the reflector's), the turn and cause of its most recent waking, the turn its hunt began, and the lines: who defeated you and on which turn; the blow that did it, as the log told it; then its story, oldest first, in its causes' own sentences: for a body split from another, how that body woke and then how it split; how it woke; and when it began hunting you, if that came later. The story is the killer's own, never the cause records, so it is the same however much has happened since.

### 8.2 Waking

*"Every exhibit is asleep, stirring or awake. Being close or making noise fills its three pips; the ring around you shows what counts as close. A species may have one extra trigger, shown as one icon."*

**States:**

| Mode | Shown as | Acts | Leaves it when |
|---|---|---|---|
| dormant | asleep (Zz), or stirring with 1–2 pips | no | 3 pips → woken (below); damage → hunting |
| awake | ! | investigates its last stimulus, or wanders its room | sees you → hunting; 20 turns without stimulus → returning |
| hunting | !! | pursues and attacks | out of sight for 8 turns (none with Vanishing Act, §8.7) → searches your last-known tile for 3 turns (sees you → hunting) → returning |
| returning | ↩ | walks home, then reinstalls for 3 turns | sees you → hunting; reinstalled → dormant with 1 pip |

- **An exhibit with no home** (its case was broken or dissolved) never returns. It stays awake and wanders. Nor does an exhibit that never sleeps (`awake`) go back: when a search of either ends, it wanders instead (a patrol goes back to its round, below); and one going back or settling in that loses its home wanders from where it is.
- **What each mode does** (`AI.byMode`, every exhibit's behaviour when its archetype has nothing of its own to do, and all of the plodder's). Each timer counts turns from the exhibit's `stim`, which is its last stimulus while awake, the last turn it saw you (or began the hunt) while hunting, and its arrival while searching or reinstalling:
  - **awake:** it walks to what it was sent to look at (onto the tile, or beside it if no exhibit can stand there or another actor stands there), then wanders: it walks to a tile of its home room (the room it stands in, if it has no home) that it can reach, drawn on its own stream, then to another; when other actors leave it no way to a place, it gives that place up and picks another;
  - **hunting:** it strikes you if it can; otherwise it walks toward you while it sees you, else to your last-known tile, and waits there; 8 turns after it last saw you it searches (`AI.loseTrackTurns`; with Vanishing Act, at its first action out of sight of you, §8.7);
  - **searching:** it walks on to your last-known tile, and its 3 turns start when it arrives, or once no step gets it closer;
  - **returning:** it walks home (a 2×2 body until its anchor stands on its home tile), and reinstalls on arriving; when other actors leave it no step home (one stands on its home, or in every way to it), it wanders its room instead, and goes back again after 20 turns undisturbed;
  - **reinstalling:** it waits at home, and 3 turns after arriving it is asleep with 1 pip (a pipless exhibit with none); moved off its home meanwhile, it goes back to it.

  An exhibit in an intact case changes mode by the same timers, but only ever waits. An exhibit that never steps (`Wake.stands`, above; M4 TABLEAU-CORE) changes mode by the same timers too, every change where it stands, and the cause of each change by those timers, by the Re-admission Ticket, by a sealed hall's shutters opening and on settling in says so (`how: 'stands'`): out of sight of you it keeps watch where it stands (searching), then settles where it stands (returning, and at once reinstalling, since it is home), and sleeps; idle, it settles where it stands. So the log never says that it set off to look, searched where it last knew you were, went back or is back in its place: "… lost you: it has not seen you for 8 turns, and keeps watch where it stands.", "… stopped keeping watch for you, and is settling where it stands.", "… settled back to sleep." Its settling in, in the step it settles, is told by that step's sentence alone.
- **A patrol** (a `patrol_guard` on its round, M4: S.9's Wader, whose trigger is `awake`) walks its round instead of wandering, and never returns to sleep. Its round is `route {posts, i}` (population's, with no draw, §5.5): each action it takes the first step, in the compass order N, NE, E, … NW, of a shortest walking path to post i (actors never counting) that no other actor stands on, and once it stands on post i it sets off for the next, round and round, drawing on no stream; a closed door on the way is opened as an action; with every such step blocked it waits, a post another actor stands on is reached from beside it, and one it cannot reach is passed over (a Wader with no round keeps to its home). Two patrols head on (each with every such step taken, one by the other, as in a way one tile wide) would wait for good: the one whose action it is steps aside, onto the first tile in compass order that no actor stands on, is no closed door and is none of the other's shortest steps (with none, it waits), and the other walks on; its line says "stepping aside head on" (M4 FU3; decided without play, revisit once M4 is playable). Sent to look at something, it goes there, then resumes its round from where it stands. Seeing you, it hunts you; having lost you and searched, it goes back to its round (`alerted`, cause `round`, to look at its next post). Its wake region (`Archetypes.wakeRegion`, M4) holds every tile it would see you on before it finds you, whatever stands in its way, since LV6's safe zone reads it over `SAFE_ZONE_TURNS`: the tiles you could stand on (not its own tile now) within `SIGHT_EXHIBIT` of, and in line of sight from, a tile it may stand on at one of its actions up to the given turns from now (through the doors opened by then; a surface, which only ever hides you, not counted). Walking its round, those are the tiles it walks to as above, each action at its cost, with the sleepers in its way where they lie, and, wherever another patrol walking its round may stand in its way by then, any other shortest step, and, where each of its shortest steps may hold another patrol, a step aside. Where that cannot be told (another exhibit awake on no round; a door a patrol may open within the earshot of another exhibit, whose noise would send it to look or stir it), and while it searches or is sent to look, they are every tile it has the time to walk to. None while it hunts you. Its timeline (`Patrol.timeline(state, turns)`, CP1 review) tells where each patrol walking its round stands at each of its actions over the next turns, you standing where none sees you: its round taken exactly, every patrol at once in the scheduler's order, the sleepers and the encased where they lie, a door opened and left open; null where that cannot be told (another exhibit awake on no round, or a door opened within another exhibit's earshot). It takes a step aside as the round does. A patrol that waits 8 of its actions in a row with its way blocked only by exhibits that stand still is `stuck` (nothing will move out of its way), as is the later in spawn order of two head on that wait on each other 8 actions each (neither can step aside). One state's timeline is worked out once and shared by every reader of it (the worst-case runner, and LV6 when D-LV6 builds it); it draws on no stream.
- **A Grazer** (`unobserved_mover`, M4: S.9's) asleep has the default wake region, its ring. Awake but not hunting you, its wake region (`Archetypes.wakeRegion`) holds every tile it could see you on before it finds you over the given turns, never fewer, since LV6's safe zone and the runner read it: the tiles you could stand on (not its own tile now) within `SIGHT_EXHIBIT` of, and in line of sight from, any tile it could stand on at one of its actions by then, that is within the steps it has the time for at its quickest (each step at what a step costs it now: `UNOBSERVED_STEP` a turn while `unobserved_motion` acts on it, a tile a turn otherwise; no status slowing it), its home plinth among them (a step beyond a tile beside it), every door and shutter open (each may be by then), whatever it does on the way; asleep again at home within the time, its ring lies inside that sight. In an intact case it only waits: its own tile. None while it hunts you, nor while it is gone. It draws on no stream.
- **Mode on waking.** Hunting, from the ring, `axis`, `tether`, `touch_case`, `ally_hit`, being seen, damage and the night-watch bell (its last-known tile is yours). Awake, investigating the source, from noise, a herald's call (it goes to look at your tile at the moment of the call; one in an intact case wakes in it, its cause `how: 'case'`, and the log says so, never that it set off to look) and the alarm. The trigger that adds the third pip decides; if two complete it at once, hunting wins.
- **A Centrepiece wakes from pips and damage only** (M4): a herald's call, the night-watch bell (§8.4) and the alarm (§5.4) pass over its pieces, which sleep on.
- **One action's wake order.** Within one action, every other wake effect is applied before that action's noise. So a blow wakes the victim's group (`ally_hit`) before its soft noise; a special that hits several actors applies every hit and its wakes before any noise, even the noise of an exhibit it destroys; opening a case, and every bash, touches it (`touch_case`) before its noise; the breaking bash releases the sleeper inside before its crash; and a mimic's grip comes before the noise of the open or bash that touched it. A sleeper at 2 pips touched by any of these therefore wakes hunting, not awake. The one exception is an alarm's call (M4, §5.4): its crash comes first, then its wakes, so that those it wakes go to look at your tile, not at the crash. At a turn boundary, likewise, noise comes after the ring, `axis` and `tether` (§8.1, step 4), so a sleeper at 2 pips inside your ring wakes hunting while you cough.
- **Waking and changing mode.** Waking is leaving dormant, and only waking resets what belongs to one waking: whether it has seen you (`sawYou`), its count of actions, and its archetype's per-waking state (a herald's one call). It costs a beat, starts the exhibit's story (`woke`, and `hunted` if it hunts), and is told by a `woke` event, then a `hunting` event if it hunts. Every other change of mode resets nothing, so a returning exhibit that has seen you and is sent to look by a noise is not surprised by your blow. Each change has its event: into hunting, `hunting`; from searching, returning or reinstalling to awake, or a new place to look while awake, `alerted`; returning to reinstalling and reinstalling to dormant, `returned`; any other change (losing you, going back, settling), `lost`. A hunt begins with your tile as its last-known tile, whether the exhibit is woken into it, turns to it, or is made hunting.
- **Pipless exhibits.** A mimic is pipless while it sleeps, in its disguise or, after it has shown itself, asleep again: it gains no pips, hears nothing, and only damage, its case being opened or bashed, or its case being dissolved wake it. So your ring, noise, a herald's call, an alarm's call (M4), the night-watch bell, "Quiet, Please" and Fumigant never touch a sleeping mimic, and its Behaviour line ("Wakes only when it is opened, bashed, dissolved or struck") stays true whenever it sleeps. Awake, it hears noise and can be quieted as every exhibit can.
- **A herald's call.** A herald calls once each time it wakes, the first time it then sees you: the call is its special (§8.1), marking every tile an exhibit could stand on within `HERALD_RANGE` of it, and when it goes off every sleeping exhibit standing on a marked tile that can hear (so not a pipless one, below) and is not a Centrepiece's piece (M4, above) wakes awake, to look at the tile you stand on then. A call called off is still that waking's one call.
- **A tethered warden** never steps outside the disc of `TETHER_RANGE` round the tile it guards (its own, when it is made), so a noise draws it only as far as the edge. If you are outside that disc at its action while it hunts or searches for you, it starts no fight with you: no blow and no new ring. A ring it has already shown still goes off at that action, as every shown special does (§8.1, Cancelling), so what the floor showed is what you take. While it sees you (and so hunts you), it walks back to its place (its home, or with none what it guards) and waits there, still hunting, with no change of mode, so a warden you watch from outside its tether stands its ground quietly. Once it does not see you, it goes back (`lost`, cause `tether`), heals fully when it reaches home (`healed`, cause `home`), and settles; an exhibit with no home never goes back, so it stays awake. It marks its ring (§8.1) only with you on it, within its tether: at its `ROLE_WARDEN_SPECIAL_EVERY`th action since it woke or the first one after that, then no sooner than `ROLE_WARDEN_SPECIAL_EVERY` actions after each mark.

**Your ring** is drawn round you at all times:
- **Radius:** 3 if your last action that cost time was anything but wait, rest, view or sit; otherwise 2. Actions that cost no time (reading, examining) never change it; a step a perk made free is a step. +2 while you stand on a loud floor (shards, water); +`LAMP_RING` (1) while you wear a lit Lamp (§5.5). Perks (Soft Soles, §8.7) and items (Quiet, §8.5) can shrink it, never below `RING_MIN` (1). That is each action's ring. A turn boundary applies the largest ring among your actions since the previous boundary, a boundary that falls inside an action counting that action; with no free or cheaper action since the previous boundary (M4's perks, §8.1), that is the last action's ring, as in M3.
- **What it does:** a sleeping exhibit within that walking distance, with line of sight to you, gains 1 pip a turn.
- **What is shown:** a tile is drawn as inside the ring exactly when a sleeping exhibit standing on it would gain a pip at a boundary with the map as it is now; one predicate (`Vision.inRing`) decides both. Only tiles an exhibit can stand on are drawn (`Grid.hold`), and a 2×2 exhibit gains its pip if any of its tiles is inside. A test checks that equivalence over whole layouts.
- **Which turn.** The ring drawn is the ring the next boundary would apply if it ran now: from your current tile, on the map as it is now, with the largest ring among your actions since the last boundary (`Vision.ringRadius`; `you.ringMax` holds it while a free or cheaper action has run since then, and the boundary clears it), which is your last action's ring when every action since was a full one. With full actions only, a boundary runs after each action that costs time and before your next decision, so the ring drawn is the ring that boundary applied, from the same tile with the same radius, unless a tile or a surface changed after it. That can happen only when your decision falls mid-turn (after a 150-tick Heavy blow or bash), since exhibits then act between the boundary and your decision: a door one opens then is open in the ring drawn, though the boundary found it shut. After a step a perk made free or an action it made cheaper, your next decision can come before any boundary has run. Until one runs (while `you.ringMax` is set), the next boundary applies at least the ring drawn whatever you do next, and exactly it if you wait, since that ring is already owed (the floor under you staying as it is). Once a boundary has run inside such an action, nothing is owed: the ring drawn is that action's own, as after a full action, and a wait then applies its own still ring, which may be smaller. The ring drawn cannot show the ring of an action you have not yet chosen.

**Noise:**
- **Classes:**
  - soft, heard within 3 walking tiles: an attack, hit or miss (heard from the attacker's tile, and made last in the blow, after everything else the blow does); opening a case or a door, or closing a door (heard from the door); a thrown item landing;
  - loud, within 6: bashing glass; an exhibit destroyed;
  - crash, within 10: glass breaking; an alarm.
- **Doors.** A closed door counts as 3 extra tiles.
- **Hearing.** An exhibit never reacts to a noise it made itself.
  - A sleeping exhibit that hears a noise gains 1 pip; it gains at most 1 pip from noise per turn. If that pip is its third, it wakes awake and goes to look at the noise's tile.
  - An awake exhibit that hears one goes to investigate the noise's tile, and so does a searching, returning or reinstalling one (it is alerted); a noise from the tile it is already going to look at only counts as a stimulus.
  - An exhibit that never steps (`Wake.stands`, M4: one that never hunts, a station, §5.4, which never steps either; and one whose archetype never steps though it hunts, `Archetypes.neverSteps`: the Great Board and the Ordinance's pieces, §5.5) is sent nowhere by a noise. Its third pip wakes it awake where it stands (its cause has `how: 'stands'`); awake, a noise only counts as a stimulus; searching, returning or reinstalling, a noise makes it awake where it stands (`alerted`, `how: 'stands'`, its own tile what it looks at).
  - An exhibit in an intact case (§8.3) is sent to look as any other is, but it stays in its case: its cause has `how: 'case'`, and the log says it woke in its case, or heard the noise but stays in its case, never that it set off to look (M4's first integration review).
  - A hunting exhibit moves your last-known tile to the tile of a noise you made (a Tuning Fork's included); another exhibit's noise says nothing about where you are. Noise does not reset its turns out of sight.
- **What is shown:** the tiles an exhibit could stand on that a noise reached (as hearing measures it; walls and fixtures are not marked) flash as a ring until your next action that takes time, with a caption ("Crash: glass breaking, heard 10 tiles away"); a long action shows every noise of its turns, and an action that takes no time (a refusal, a free read) leaves the rings as they were (§6.1).

**Pips:**
- 3 to wake, for every exhibit;
- 1 pip decays per 10 turns without stimulus, before the bell, counted from its last pip gained (or the turn it went to sleep) and from its last decay: a sleeper left at 2 pips on turn t has 1 on turn t + 10 and none on turn t + 20;
- none decay after the bell;
- each trigger adds at most once per turn.

**Special triggers,** at most one per species, shown as one icon:

| Trigger | Fires when | Pips |
|---|---|---|
| `axis(r)` | you stand in its row or column within r of one of its tiles, in line of sight from that tile | 1 a turn |
| `period` | its module's chime (§5.4): at the boundary of its chime, while it sleeps and is not held fixed | wakes awake for the demonstration, keeping the pips it had (`ax.perform`), then sleeps again with them on the multiple (M4) |
| `tether(r)` | you come within r of the tile it guards (a tethered warden guards its own); no sight is needed | 3 |
| `touch_case` | its case, the case it is, or the case it guards (M4: the Composite, the Lamp's), is opened or bashed (touched, §8.3) | 3 |
| `ally_hit` | a member of its group within 5 of it takes any damage (only sleepers whose own trigger is `ally_hit` answer) | 3 |
| `awake` | it is never dormant, and never goes back to sleep: it is never returning or reinstalling | none |

The alarm is not a species trigger: `alarm_network` wakes the department itself (§5.4).

At a turn boundary the wake checks (your ring, `axis`, `tether`) are all made from your tile at that tick before any is applied; then each sleeper, in spawn order, takes its ring pip, its `axis` pip and its `tether` pips, in that order, so the one that adds its third pip is what it woke for; then each station still asleep whose chime it is wakes for it (`period`, M4), so one your ring woke at that boundary was awake before its chime.

**Damage always wakes, straight to hunting,** but for an exhibit that never hunts (M4's stations: `Archetypes.huntsOnSight` false): wherever another would start to hunt (your ring, a blow, a bell, a broken case), `Wake.wake` and `Wake.setMode` wake it, or keep it, awake, with nothing to look at, so a station is never hunting.

**Sight:**
- You see every tile in line of sight within 12. Since every tile is lit, light plays no part.
- An exhibit that is awake, hunting, searching, returning or reinstalling sees you within 8 (`within r`, below) of any of its tiles, with line of sight from that tile. Seeing you turns any of these modes into a hunt, unless its archetype never hunts on sight (`Archetypes.huntsOnSight`: M4's stations, as their lines say), and a hunter that sees you knows your tile. A sleeping exhibit sees nothing.

**Geometry, defined once in `grid.js` and used by every rule:**
- **Line of sight:** a Bresenham line traced from whichever endpoint comes first in row-major order, blocked by any sight-blocking tile between the ends, or by a tile between them that a sight-blocking surface covers (a Fumigant cloud or plaster dust: the `size` × `size` square centred on its tile; fog in M4). Glass passes sight; closed doors block it; actors do not. It is symmetric by construction, and a test checks `los(a, b) === los(b, a)` over whole layouts.
- **Within r** (sight, the Lamp, `axis`, `tether`, `ally_hit`, a herald's call, a shot's range): `dx² + dy² ≤ r² + r`, the layout prototype's disc.
- **Walking distance** (the ring, noise, "walking tiles"): 8-connected steps, where a diagonal step is allowed only if both orthogonal neighbours could be stepped onto; actors never block it, and staff doors count as doors. To something on a tile you cannot step onto (an exhibit in a case or on a plinth, a Centrepiece's footprint, a lectern), it is 1 + the walking distance to the nearest neighbouring tile you could step onto (`Grid.reachDist`). A closed door, ordinary or staff, adds `NOISE_DOOR` (3) for noise (`Noise.reach`). A hall's shutter (`Z`, M4) is no tile to walk, but a noise passes it as it passes a closed door, `NOISE_DOOR` extra included (§4.1). There is no other limit on distance.
- **Moving:** `move(dir)` follows the same diagonal rule. A pursuing actor (an exhibit hunting you, or walking on to your last-known tile to search it) takes the first step of a shortest walking path, breaking ties on its own `ai/` stream. An exhibit walking anywhere else (to look, to wander, home) goes round the other actors where they stand now: it takes the first step of a shortest walking path that crosses no tile another actor stands on, ties likewise. For an exhibit, staff doors, open or closed, are walls, also for the diagonal rule, and it never steps out of an intact case (`Grid.canStep(m, x, y, d, 'exhibit')`).
- **Where an exhibit can stand** (`Grid.hold`): every walkable tile, a vitrine and a plinth. An exhibit made standing on a plinth (M4: S.9's Intro stamp stands its Grazer on one, §5.5) steps off it as from any tile, its walking distance there one more than that of the nearest tile it could step off onto, and steps back onto it only as its home, going home, by the same diagonal rule; no exhibit steps onto any other plinth, or walks across one (`AI`; `modes_exhibit_steps`).
- **Doors.** Closed doors block sight and movement. An exhibit opens a closed door by stepping into it, as you do (`COST_DOOR`, a soft noise). You close an open door (`'` or `s`) beside you that you could step into, with `close(x, y)`, for `COST_DOOR` and a soft noise at the door; closing one with an actor in its doorway is refused (`doorway_occupied`), as is a tile that is not an open door beside you (`no_door`) or one past a corner (`corner`). Exhibits treat staff doors as walls, so a staff corridor is a refuge you pay a grade for, and its section panel says so.

### 8.3 Cases

*"You can see through glass but not walk through it. Open a case if your grade allows, or break it, loudly."*

**Glass grades:**

| Grade | Bashes to break | Lock (grade needed to open) |
|---|---|---|
| Plate | 1 | I–II |
| Laminated | 2 | II–IV |
| Security | 3 | IV–VII |

**Placement.**
- Glass by depth follows `GLASS_MIX_D1` … `GLASS_MIX_D6` (§8.10): Security joins the mix from depth 3.
- A case's lock is drawn uniformly from the overlap of its glass range and I to (depth + 1). If they do not overlap, the lock is the glass range's minimum.
- Every vitrine inside a room is a case. A vitrine in a wall's place (a glazed screen, which belongs to no room) is fixed glazing: it is seen through and refused like a case when bumped, and it is never opened or bashed.
- A case's accession number is the department's number, its room's number and its place among the room's cases in row-major order, counted from 1 (`S.9.103.2`), with no draw; an on-loan case keeps its number.
- A staff door's grade is drawn uniformly from I to (depth + 1), never above VII, on the department's staff stream, the doors in row-major order.
- Each case draws its glass, then its lock, then whether it is on loan. A mimic's case is drawn the same way, on the mimic's own stream (§3.2), so it differs from a real case only in having no accession number.
