- A Highlight is never behind a lock above the grade you enter the department with, nor in a case on loan, a mimic's case or one that already holds something (`Cases.highlightOk`).

**Opening** costs 200 ticks, makes a soft noise and fires `touch_case` (with Light Fingers, M4, your opening costs 100 ticks and makes no noise, and fires `touch_case` all the same, §8.7). An opened case is passable, with no shards (its tile becomes `u`, as a dissolved one's does); an exhibit in it may leave, keeps its home there, and can return to it.

**`open(x, y)` and `bash(x, y)`** take an intact case beside you, never diagonally past a corner (as a blow). Anything else is refused before it costs anything: no intact case there (`no_case`), not beside you (`out_of_reach`), past a corner (`corner`), a lock above your grade, and the grade above it that Light Fingers at its second rank adds (`case_locked`, to open; §8.7), or glass your weapon cannot break (`glass_too_strong`, to bash).

**A touch.** Opening a case, and every bash, touches it (Solvent never does, and a bump is refused). Every exhibit standing in it, at home in it, or guarding it (its `guards` the case's tile: M4's Composite, which guards the Lamp's case) whose trigger is `touch_case` gains 3 pips, so it wakes at once, hunting; the mimic whose case it is wakes at once, hunting, without pips. In one open or bash the touch comes first; then the case changes (it opens, its glass cracks, or it breaks, releasing what stood in it and laying its shards); then the case's event; and last its noise, from the case tile (§8.2, one action's wake order).

**Bashing:**
- each bash costs 100 ticks and makes a loud noise;
- the breaking bash makes a crash;
- a Heavy weapon counts two bashes a swing;
- a Light weapon cannot break Laminated or Security glass;
- `breaker` adds one bash to each swing (`BREAKER_BASHES`), but never lets a Light weapon break Laminated or Security glass, and no swing counts more bashes than break the glass.

**A broken case:**
- becomes passable, with shards, and shards spread to one floor tile on your side: the tile you bashed from, by the shard rule below;
- its contents can be taken from an adjacent tile;
- a sleeping exhibit inside wakes hunting and has no home; one already awake inside turns to the hunt, homeless too; and an exhibit whose home it was, standing elsewhere, loses that home.

**A case that holds weather** (M4, `weather_cases`, §5.4) lets it out when it is opened, broken or dissolved, once. While it is shut its label says so: "A case of fog" (or rain) where it would be "An empty case" (an item, an exhibit inside or a loan keep their titles), and first among its lines, which its strip shows too, what it lets out, how large and for how long, and what that does ("It holds fog: opened, broken or dissolved, it lets it out, 3 tiles a side, for 10 turns, and the fog blocks sight."; for rain, which of the department's exhibits it stops, by name: "While the Semaphore Station, Two-Armed stands in rain, and for 2 turns after, it only waits, and any mark it has made is called off."). Opened or broken, it says nothing of weather. A mimic's disguise never holds weather.

**A mimic's case** is opened and bashed as a real case is, with the same refusals, but nothing opens or cracks: the touch reveals the mimic (its tile becomes floor) and wakes it, before the noise. Solvent reveals it and wakes it hunting, silently and without its grip. A case whose mimic is gone shows itself floor when it is touched or dissolved.

**An exhibit in an intact case** gains pips as usual. If it wakes, it stays inside, shown awake or hunting, and acts only by specials and ranged attacks that pass glass: it can never step out of the case, and nothing strikes into or out of an intact case. Its mode still changes by the modes' timers (§8.2), but it only waits in its case, so the log never has it set off to look, search where it last knew you were or go back to a place it never left: woken by a noise, a herald's call or the alarm it "woke in its case"; sent by a noise it "stays in its case"; and losing you (after 8 turns, or at once with Vanishing Act, §8.7), giving up the search and settling down after 20 quiet turns say that it stays in its case (each such cause has `how: 'case'`, from `Wake.hear`, the herald's call, the alarm's call and `Wake.setMode`; M4's first integration review; `vanished` M4's PG-PERKS-A). A bump into a case, and a blow at anything standing in one, are refused alike (`behind_glass`, "Glass is in the way."), so a mimic in its disguise is refused exactly as a case is. Solvent leaves it homeless and wakes it hunting, silently. The night-watch bell never picks an encased exhibit.

**A mimic** is made, asleep, as a case on its own floor tile; a mimic made asleep anywhere else (not one tile, not on a floor tile, or on a fixture) is a content error, and the room or department is not built. Opened or bashed, it shows itself (its case `revealed`, its tile floor again) and grips whoever touched it: `rooted` for `MIMIC_GRIP_TURNS` turns, which covers your next 2 decisions; then the touch wakes it, hunting (`touch_case`), all before the noise of the open or bash. Its first action, after its beat, is a rolled blow from beside you, and it is slow at everything. Struck while it is still a case (only a beam reaches it), it shows itself and wakes without the grip; dissolved by Solvent, it shows itself and hunts silently, without the grip.

**Staff doors** (the M.11 staff corridor, and any staff layer a programme draws) open like case locks: each carries a grade, drawn as locks are, and opens for 100 ticks with a soft noise at that grade or above (an open staff door is `s`, which can be seen through). Below it, it is a wall (`staff_grade`), whatever perks you hold (Light Fingers opens case locks above your grade, never a staff door, §8.7), and a path for a tap never goes through it. Nothing required lies behind one (`design/layout.md` LV5). A department records every staff door's grade; a room built for a test or an arena records one only when it is given one, and a bump opens a staff door with no recorded grade at grade I. A path for a tap goes through a closed staff door only where its grade is recorded and within yours: never through one with no recorded grade, and never through any on a board that records no grades, as a policy's board rebuilt from the view model does unless it carries the staff doors' grades and yours (`Path.to`).

**Other rules:**
- **Shards** only ever land on floor or an archway (`Cases.shardTile`): on the tile they fall to (a glass exhibit's own tile; for a broken case, the tile on your side, or, for a case the Ordinance's shatter breaks, its own tile, which is no longer floor, §5.5), if it is one; otherwise on that tile's first floor or archway neighbour in direction order (N, NE, E, … NW); otherwise nowhere. So shards never cover a door, an exit, the entrance, a bench or a case.
- Solvent, thrown, dissolves every case in a 3×3 silently: no noise, no shards, no `touch_case`. Its tile becomes `u`, what stood in it is released as from a broken case, and each case dissolved is told by one `glass_broke` with no shards.
- About one case in six is "on loan elsewhere": a label with no object.
- Vitrines never stand on gate cells (`design/layout.md` LV10).

### 8.4 The clock

*"At the bell your recovery slows; later the night watch begins its rounds, waking whatever is nearest to you."*

**Open hours** are set by the department's tour length `L_tour` (`design/layout.md` §13.1):
- **The bell:** `B = round10(2.5 × L_tour + 100)` turns after entry; about 250–320 at Cabinet size, from `L_tour` measured at layout version 3 with each pack's own Intro stamp (§5.5; 220–300 at M1).
- **The night watch:** at B + G, where `G = max(60, round10(0.15 × B))`.
- **Calibration** happens at the slice gate.
- **The count.** The clock counts the turns since entry (`dept.clock.count`), one at each turn boundary, at step 6 (§8.1). The bell, the night watch and each night-watch bell come at the boundary where the count reaches them, after that boundary's regeneration (step 3) and pip decay (step 5), so they take effect from the next boundary; each is told by a `stage` event. A clock made part-way through (an arena's or a test's) takes its stage, and the night-watch bells already rung, from its count, with no events.

**The bell:**
- pips stop decaying;
- the regeneration interval doubles;
- an announcement and a section-panel banner. Section panels come with M4's content, so in M3 the announcement is a plain banner in the strip, with the stage on the dial.

**The night watch:**
- regeneration stops;
- a Night Watchman (`night_watchman`, a shared species with the warden curve's numbers at the department's depth) enters at the department entrance, or, if someone stands there, on the nearest free tile by an exhibit's walking distance (ties in row-major order; free: a tile an exhibit can step onto and stand on, not a closed door, with no one on it), and its announcement then says it came in near the entrance. It is created hunting, its story reading "began its rounds, hunting you from the start" (cause `night_watch`, §3.4), so it is never caught by surprise, and it first acts one action after it enters (§8.1). It walks to your actual position over the whole map, never to a tile where it only last knew you were, and it never searches or goes back. Staff doors are walls to it, as to every exhibit (§8.2), so when no walk reaches you it goes to the tile it can reach that is nearest you and waits there. There is never more than one. If defeated, the next enters `WATCHMAN_RETURN` (20) clock counts later, as `<acc>.<depth>.watch.<k>` with k counting up (§3.2);
- the Night Watchman is an exhibit, not a member of staff: DESIGN.md and `design/core-systems.md` §5.3 describe night staff who cannot be harmed, this section wins by precedence, and its label never calls it staff;
- every 30 turns after the night watch begins (`NIGHT_WAKE_EVERY`; the first at B + G + 30, since the Watchman alone marks B + G), a night-watch bell rings, and the 3 sleeping exhibits nearest to you by walking distance (`NIGHT_WAKE_COUNT`; from your tile, as for the ring, §8.2; ties in spawn order) wake hunting you, with your tile as their last-known tile. It wakes only sleepers that a walk from you reaches, that are not in an intact case, that are not pipless (a sleeping mimic, in its disguise or not, §8.2) and that are not a Centrepiece's pieces (M4). The walk is the ring's: staff doors count as doors whatever their grade, so the bell can wake a sleeper behind a staff door you cannot open, which then cannot come through it (§8.2). The bell is not a noise: it wakes them directly.

**The alarm brings the clock closer** (M4, §5.4; `ClockRules.advance(state, n, cause, ev)`): the next stage comes n turns sooner, never sooner than the next turn boundary. Before the bell the bell moves, and the night watch with it; after the bell the night watch moves; in the night watch nothing is left to move. Each is told by a `stage` event, `bell_sooner` or `watch_sooner` with the turns it moved, or `nothing_sooner`, with the alarm's cause and its sentence, which states these limits. A sealed hall stops the count, not this.

**A Centrepiece hall** (M4; Q6, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable) seals with shutters (`Z`, §4.1) over every one of its openings, staff doors included, at a turn boundary (step 6, before the clock: `Hall.boundary`), and only while you stand inside it, no one stands in any of its openings, and its Centrepiece calls for the seal: a fight's while one of its pieces hunts you from inside the hall (any tile of it there; one hunting you from outside calls for nothing, so the seal shuts you in with it and never alone, and a piece outside as it closes is shut out); a treasure's by its own rule (§5.5; `Hall.defineSeal(form, fn)`), never on the hunt alone: while one of its pieces hunts you from inside the hall, from `TREASURE_SEAL_TURNS` (12) turns after its case was first opened or bashed (why `touched`) or while its health is `TREASURE_SEAL_HP_PCT` (34)% of its full health or less (why `hurt`), naming no piece as `by`, and the sealed sentence says which ("Shutters closed over the hall’s openings, since the Centrepiece is hunting you and its treasure’s case was first opened or bashed 12 turns or more ago: …"; M4 COMPOSITE-PHASES). While someone stands in an opening the seal waits, says so once (`stage` `waiting`), and is tried again at each boundary; the stage's cause names what calls for the seal as `by` (the hunting piece, as a sealing names it, so the stage is that exhibit's doing and its line covers it) and who stands in the opening as `in` only if you see it then, so the sentence never names an exhibit out of your sight ("someone out of your sight stands in an opening"), and an exhibit that merely stands in the way causes no stage. While it is sealed, the count toward the bell and the night watch, the night-watch bells and the Night Watchman's coming stop (`dept.clock.pausedSince` is the turn it sealed), and so does regeneration. Every other turn-boundary step runs as usual: surfaces, statuses, pip decay, alarm decay, module turn rules and chimes. No one steps onto a shutter, so a Night Watchman outside stays outside; a step into one, or a take of an item lying under one, is refused (`sealed`); a noise passes it as it passes a closed door. The shutters open (`stage` `unsealed`, saying why):
- **quiet:** at the boundary that ends the last of `SEAL_LIFT_TURNS` (50) turns in a row in which none of the Centrepiece's pieces hurt you, at any range, or stood beside you at the turn's end. A hurt restarts the count from the boundary that ends the turn it falls in (`Combat.hurt` tells the hall, `Hall.onHurt`). A body a Centrepiece's summons calls (the Great Board's Logbook Trolleys, §8.1) is no piece of it: its blows, and its standing beside you, neither restart the count nor keep the hall sealed (M4 TABLEAU-CORE; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable), so hiding from the Centrepiece still opens the shutters in time, the bodies' blows the price;
- **ticket:** when you read a Re-admission Ticket, which returns you to the entrance: each of the Centrepiece's pieces still standing regains `SEAL_HEAL_PCT` (25)% of its maximum health and, unless asleep, goes back to its place (`Hall.onTicket`);
- **defeat:** when the Centrepiece is defeated, at once when its rules say so (`Hall.onDefeat`), and in any case at the next boundary once none of its pieces stands (`treasure` for a treasure's guardian, `sheet` for one under its dust sheet). It never seals again;
- **alone:** at the next boundary once none of its pieces still standing is in the hall (an ensemble's last piece inside fell while others stand outside), so that you are never kept sealed in alone with the clock stopped.

Once they have opened, the hall does not seal again until you have stood outside it at a turn's end, so a quiet lift, or one that left you alone, lets you walk out, and its sentence says so. While the count is stopped, what the clock brings comes once it has counted that many more turns: a defeated Night Watchman's successor too, as its Behaviour line says ("when the clock has counted 20 more turns"). The board draws a shutter as a ribbed steel shutter in both views (§6.2), and your memory of each opening you have seen follows its shutter, and as it opens goes back to what you last saw there (§6.1). A step into the hall asks first, saying when it would seal behind you (§7.1).

**Leaving.** The exit doors work at any time: `leave(i)`, made standing on exit door i, costs no time and ends the visit (`over` with k `left`, and a `left` event that names the door and the clock's stage); it is refused on any other tile (`not_on_exit`) and for a door that does not exist (`no_exit`). Leaving forfeits unearned Highlights and the Certificate. There is no time bonus. In M3 leaving only ends the visit; the doors' placards and the run's next department are M5's (§8.9).

### 8.5 Your kit

*"A weapon, a garment, two curios and an instrument. Restoration kits improve them."*

**Slots and carrying:**
- weapon, garment, two curios, an instrument;
- a tote of 16 slots, with one slot per consumable kind (stacking) and one per unequipped item;
- no weight.

**Restoration kits:**
- **Two per department,** as Highlights: never in a dead end beyond a lock above your grade, never secret.
- **Each adds a level to one item:**

  | Slot | +1 level gives |
  |---|---|
  | Weapon | +1 min and max damage, and +1 Acc at every even level |
  | Garment | +1 Padding |
  | Curio | +1 rank |
  | Instrument | +1 charge |

**Item primitives the slice implements** (closed):
- damage, Acc, Eva and Padding bonuses;
- `reach`;
- `quiet` (your ring −1 when you move or strike);
- `breaker` (+1 bash);
- `observing` (the Lamp's rule);
- `charges` (for instruments);
- one effect per instrument from: Tuning Fork (a soft noise at a tile within 8; 3 charges, recharge 40 turns), Pin-Driver (a ranged attack, damage [t, 2t+2]; 4 charges, recharge 30), Window Pole (push or pull 1 tile, or take from an open case at range 2; 4 charges, recharge 40).

**Each department places:**
- 2 pieces of equipment;
- 1 curio or instrument;
- 5 consumables;
- the Centrepiece's Signature piece.

From M4 a pack's gallery list may name some of these, and more: the counts are then filled from the rest (below), so S.9, whose list names its two pieces of equipment, two curios and instruments and its Lamp, places all of them.

**Using your kit** (`js/core/rules/kit.js`):
- **Taking and dropping.** You take an item from your tile or one beside you (a diagonal one not past a corner, as a blow), lying on the floor or in an opened or broken case; walking over an item never takes it. It goes into your tote, a consumable into the stack of its kind and seal if you have one (the stack keeps the department it was first taken in, `from`), and a full tote refuses. You drop an item from your tote onto your tile. Each costs `COST_TAKE`.
- **Equipping.** Only from the tote. A weapon, garment or instrument takes the place of the one you wear, which goes into the tote where the new one was, so a full tote never stops it; a curio takes the first empty curio slot, and is refused when both are full. Taking an item off puts it in the tote, costs what putting it on does, and is refused when the tote is full; the weapon slot is never empty, so a weapon is changed by equipping another. Like every action, item actions take twice as long while you are slowed.
- **Bonuses.** Every primitive on what you wear (weapon, garment, curios and instrument) counts: a damage, Acc, Eva or Padding bonus adds its size's value (`BONUS_*`: S, M, L), the damage bonus to both ends of your damage; a curio's rank (its Restoration levels) adds 1 to each of its bonuses; a garment's own Padding is never below 0, however far short of it you are, and Padding bonuses add to it, so taking a garment off never raises your Padding; `reach` makes your blows Reach blows; `quiet` and `breaker` count once however many items have them. Your stats change once your action's ticks are done (§8.1).
- **Quiet.** Your ring is `QUIET_RING` smaller while your last action that cost time was a move or an attack and you wear a quiet item. Any move counts, a bump into an exhibit or a door included, so a blow counts however it is struck.
- **The Lamp** (an `observing` curio, §5.5): `use(uid)`, bare, lights it or hoods it where you wear it in a curio slot, for `COST_USE` (doubled while you are slowed), with a `used` event (`how` light or hood); it is refused `not_equipped` anywhere else and `bad_use` aimed. Taking it off hoods it, so it is lit only while worn. While it is lit, every exhibit within `LAMP_OBSERVE_RANGE` of you counts as observed (`Kit.observing`, `Vision.observed`), and your ring is `LAMP_RING` larger (§8.2).
- **Instruments** are used only from your instrument slot, at a tile within their range (a disc, as "within r" always is) in your line of sight, for `COST_USE` and one charge. An instrument that is not full gets one charge back every recharge turns from its first use, wherever it is; Restoration's charge comes filled. The Tuning Fork makes a soft noise of yours at any tile an exhibit could stand on. The Pin-Driver fires a bolt (§8.1, Shots) at an exhibit you can see with a clear line, since bolts stop at glass, and is refused as a shot is (`out_of_reach`, `blocked`, `behind_glass`, `no_target`), so an exhibit in a case, one behind glazing, an empty case and a mimic's disguise are all refused alike: damage `PIN_DRIVER_DMG` at its tier, rolled with your Acc without the weapon's (`PLAYER_ACC` and the Acc bonuses of your garment, curios and instrument). The Window Pole, with `how` push or pull, moves a one-tile exhibit not in a case, and with no special marked (it would leave or enter tiles already shown), one tile straight away from you or toward you, only where it could step itself and onto no actor or closed door, without waking it; with `how` take, it takes the item from an opened or broken case into your tote. A use's `used` event comes before what it does: the noise, the bolt, the move or the take. A use is refused before it costs anything, with nothing changed (`Kit.aimCode`): what is not a known instrument with `no_item` or `no_use`, and a `how` or `on` it does not take with `bad_use`; every instrument with `not_equipped` (not in your instrument slot), `no_charges` (none left), `no_aim` (no tile of the board) or `own_tile` (your own); the fork and the pole with `out_of_range` (outside the disc of its range) or `no_line` (out of your sight); the fork with `not_a_place` (no tile an exhibit could stand on); the pole with `no_exhibit`, `too_big`, `in_case`, `marked` or `cannot_move` for a push or pull, and `nothing_to_take` or `tote_full` for a take; the Pin-Driver with a shot's codes above.
- **Restoration.** `use(kit, on)`: a kit in your tote, on a weapon, garment, curio or instrument you wear or carry, for `COST_USE`; the kit is used up.

**The item placement** (`Kit.place`, M4; decided without play, revisit once M4 is playable). A pack with no gallery list takes the stand-in placement (below). With one, after your starting kit and the carry's items:
- each item the list names, in the list's order, lies in the room its gallery took (§5.5): a treasure Centrepiece's Signature piece listed in its hall's gallery (S.9's Lamp) in the treasure case (§5.5), or on the hall's floor if that case cannot hold it (the treasure validator then rejects the attempt); an item listed directly after a warden that guards a case (`guards: 'case'`) in the case that warden guards, when it guards one and the case can hold it, and otherwise as its own entry says; any other `case: true` item in an empty case of its room (shut, not on loan, not a mimic's, holding no item and no exhibit, a removed one included), or on its floor when none is left; any other on its floor (a tile on which no actor stands, a removed one included, and no item lies). Each place is drawn from the room's places on its populate stream, then a weapon's, garment's or instrument's tier; a listed item whose gallery has no room in the plan, or whose room has no place left, is placed as the stand-in places a piece. The log says each item that is not where its entry first asks;
- then, on `…/a<k>/items`, the rest of the counts above from the pack's items no gallery list names, never its Signature piece: `EQUIPMENT_PER_DEPT` weapons and garments and `CURIO_OR_INSTRUMENT_PER_DEPT` curios and instruments, each less the list's own of them (never below none), each list shuffled and taken in turn as the stand-in takes it; then `KITS_PER_DEPT` Restoration kits, the Highlights; each of these placed as the stand-in places a piece.

A Signature piece listed anywhere but as its pack's treasure is a content error: no Signature piece is loot. A.4's list names the Date Stamp (in a case of the Copying Room) and the Register (on the floor of the last section, where the Registry joins it), and one more weapon or garment is drawn; M.11's names the Signal Flag, and the Apron and the Hand Lamp are drawn, the only others but the Pocket Heliograph.

**The stand-in placement** (`Kit.standIn`, for a pack with no gallery list): on `…/a<k>/items`, `EQUIPMENT_PER_DEPT` of the pack's weapons and garments and `CURIO_OR_INSTRUMENT_PER_DEPT` of its curios and instruments (never its Signature piece, the item its Centrepiece names, which is no loot: M4), each list shuffled and taken in turn (from its start again if it is short), then `KITS_PER_DEPT` Restoration kits; then each piece's room, among the sections and studies (the next one in order if it has no place left). On that room's populate stream: its place, a floor tile on which no actor stands (a removed one included) and no item lies, or an empty case (shut, not on loan, not a mimic's, holding no item and no exhibit, a removed one included: a gallery list stands exhibits in cases, M4); then a weapon's, garment's or instrument's tier. Item uids are counted per room (§3.2). The kits are the department's Highlights, recorded in `dept.highlights` with their rooms: never where the entrance cannot be walked to without a staff door, and in a case only within the grade you came in with (`Cases.highlightOk`).

### 8.6 Consumables

*"A handful of kinds, unlabelled until you have used one or read about it."*

**Eight kinds.** Solutions are drunk or thrown (thrown: a 3×3 unless the kind says otherwise, soft noise); Ephemera are read. You use them from your tote, one of a stack at a time, for `COST_USE`. A throw lands on a tile you can see through, within `THROW_RANGE` (a disc) and in line of sight, and reaches every creature with a tile in its square, you included, except one inside an intact case, which is behind glass.

| Kind | Effect | Deck cards |
|---|---|---|
| Consolidant | Drink: heal 50% of max HP now and 25% over 5 turns. Throw: heals every creature in the 3×3 by 50% | 20 |
| Fixative | Drink (harmful): you are fixed (cannot move or act) for 4 turns. Throw: fixes every creature in the 3×3 for 4 turns | 8 |
| Fumigant | Drink (harmful): you cough, a soft noise each turn for 5 turns. Throw: a 5×5 cloud for 10 turns that blocks sight; sleeping exhibits inside lose 1 pip a turn | 10 |
| Solvent | Drink: removes your harmful statuses. Throw: dissolves case glass in the 3×3 silently | 8 |
| Catalogue Card | Read: identify one unknown seal, of your choice (with only one left, that one at once); a known Card cannot be read once every seal is known | 14 |
| Floor Plan | Read: reveals the department's plan (every tile counts as explored, remembered as it is when read) and the rooms of the remaining Highlights | 8 |
| "Quiet, Please" | Read: every exhibit within 8 walking tiles loses all pips; awake exhibits that are not hunting settle where they stand; hunters drop to awake | 8 |
| Re-admission Ticket | Read: return to this department's entrance (or, if someone stands there, the free tile nearest it by walking distance, then in row-major order), even while you are rooted, since it is not a step; hunters lose you; unseals a Centrepiece hall. With no entrance to return to, a Ticket not yet identified is identified and does nothing, and a known one cannot be read, so no refusal gives an unknown one away | 5 |

**"Quiet, Please" and the Ticket on exhibits.** "Quiet, Please" measures its 8 walking tiles from you to an exhibit's nearest tile, with no extra for doors. An exhibit awake but not hunting (awake, searching, going back or settling back in) goes to sleep where it stands, its home unchanged; a hunter drops to awake with nothing to look at, and its pending special is called off. It leaves alone the Night Watchman, a species that never sleeps, and a mimic in its disguise. The Ticket's "hunters lose you" means that each hunter but the Night Watchman searches where it last knew you were, and its pending special is called off.

**Seals.** Each run shuffles 4 wax seals over the Solutions and 4 ink stamps over the Ephemera. The seal carries identification through the run. Each department gives every seal a local name ("Rainwater, Collected at Slack Tide · verdigris seal"; its pack's `consumables.names`, by the seal's position), so a name says nothing about the kind from one run to the next; a stack keeps the name of the department where it was first taken.

**Identification:**
- **By use.** A first use of an unidentified kind never harms you: a harmful drink is identified and does nothing, and the game says what it would have done; a throw of an unidentified Solution is refused if a 5×5 area (the largest any Solution has) centred on the target would include you, with a sentence that names no kind. Otherwise a throw does what its kind does, healing hostiles included. Use identifies at once, and the kind is recorded in the Catalogue.
- **By label.** Each department shows at least 2 identifying labels for kinds you do not know, one in the Intro or the first section. Reading the full label identifies the kind. A label is a plaque on a wall, read with `read(x, y)` within `READ_RANGE` (a disc) and in line of sight, at no cost in time, while its kind is unknown.
- **By Catalogue Card.**

**The deck.** Ambient consumables are drawn from one run deck of the cards above (81), without replacement, refilled when empty. A department places (`Consumables.place`; M3's stand-in, which M4 keeps for every pack, since a gallery list names no consumables, §5.2: decided without play, revisit once M4 is playable) `CONSUMABLES_PER_DEPT` of them on free floor tiles of its sections and studies that you can walk to from the entrance without passing a staff door, and its labels on wall tiles each beside a floor tile of its Intro, a section or a study that you can walk to in the same way, the first by the Intro or the first section (on §3.2's placement streams; the walk is measured on the map as laid out, a mimic's disguise counting as the floor it stands on).

### 8.7 Grade

*"Each department's Certificate, earned by defeating or viewing its Centrepiece, raises your grade by one: more health, a perk chosen from three, heavier objects, higher-grade cases."*

**The grade:**
- starts at I and rises by one per Certificate, so VII at most in a six-department run;
- is printed on the badge: "Handling Clearance, Grade IV".

**Each Certificate gives:**
- +5 max HP, and heals 5;
- a perk chosen from three: two from the department's primary challenge family and one from its secondary, with a perk you hold offered at rank 2 where possible.

**Three ways to the Certificate:**
- **Defeat** the Centrepiece: the Certificate and its Signature piece.
- **View** it: four viewing turns standing on the lectern's reading tile while it sleeps; `view` is legal only there. The layout puts a lectern against a wall with a reading tile beside it exactly `VIEW_DISTANCE` (3) walking tiles from the Centrepiece (§8.2's rule for tiles you cannot step onto), with line of sight to it. Viewing is standing still, so a viewer's ring (2) adds no pips; but the steps that bring you within 3 do, and so does any noise. A pip that wakes it interrupts the view. A completed view earns the Certificate and closes the exhibit for the night (a dust sheet), so no fight follows. This keeps DESIGN default 9's rule and makes "inside its wake radius" precise: the lectern sits at the edge of the ring you have while moving.
- **Treasure** (S.9): carry the Lamp out of the hall alive for the Certificate and the Signature piece, or view for the Certificate alone.

**Viewing, exactly** (M4; `js/core/rules/viewing.js`; four views in a row, and a sheet that also closes S.9's Lamp case, are Q9's, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable):
- **A view** spends a viewing turn (`COST_VIEW`, standing still) on the reading tile (`dept.viewTile`), while every piece of the Centrepiece still standing sleeps and the visit has no Certificate yet (§4.2 gives its refusals and their order). Each is told (`viewed` `{who, of, n}`, `of` the first of the Centrepiece's pieces still standing, since an ensemble's first may have fallen, cause `view`) with the count and how many more turns of viewing in a row earn the Certificate ("You viewed the Great Board: 2 of 4 turns. 2 more turns of viewing in a row while the Centrepiece sleeps earn the Certificate.": the Centrepiece, every piece of it, not the piece `of` names, which for an ensemble is one of several with names of their own); `dept.view` `{n, turn}` keeps the row's count and its last view's turn (absent until the first view; `Viewing.of`).
- **In a row.** A view continues the row when your last action that took time was a view and no piece of the Centrepiece has woken since that view's turn; otherwise it starts a new row at 1. So any other action that takes time starts the count again (counted as your ring counts it, §8.2: a step a perk made free counts too; a read or a choice, which takes none, does not, nor does a refused one), and so does its waking: a pip that wakes it, from your ring or a noise, interrupts the count, and while it is awake every view is refused. The first view of a row says so.
- **Your ring at the lectern** (D4.18). Standing still on the reading tile your ring is 2 (before your perks, or a loud floor there), which does not reach the Centrepiece 3 walking tiles away; a lit Lamp's is 3, which does, so with the Lamp lit each turn of viewing adds a pip, and its third pip, counting those your steps there gave it, wakes it. The lectern says which, as you are (below).
- **The last view** of a row, the `VIEW_TURNS`-th, before the world acts, puts the Centrepiece under its dust sheet and then earns the Certificate (`Progress.award`, by view): its sentence says the view is complete, and the Certificate's sentences follow it.
- **The dust sheet is no defeat.** Every piece still standing is gone, wherever it stands, with no death, so no Signature piece drops and no Certificate comes by defeat; `dept.cp.sheet` `{turn, uids}` records it (`Viewing.sheeted`), and a sealed hall opens at the next turn boundary (`sheet`, §8.4) and never seals again. Its footprint, each piece's home tiles (where it was placed, though a piece may have been left asleep elsewhere), becomes low furniture (`f`), each tile with a sheet fixture `{k: 'sheet', x, y, of}`, in the pieces' order and then row-major (a tile someone else stands on, or one no longer floor or shards, keeps its tile, and stays open once they leave), so no one steps onto it, and sight, beams and bolts pass over it, striking nothing under it. Nothing under it can be viewed, struck (`attack`), stepped into or aimed at with an instrument: each is refused `sheeted` ("…you cannot view it, strike it, step onto it or aim an instrument at it, nor open, bash, take from or aim at the case of a treasure it guards, or throw a Solution onto or beside that case."). A Solution may still be thrown onto it, since a throw is aimed at a tile: its square (§8.6) reaches whoever stands in it, and nothing under the sheet. A treasure's guardian's sheet also closes the case it guards (M4 COMPOSITE-PHASES, `js/core/rules/treasure.js`; plan D3.6, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable), so no view earns the Certificate and then the treasure too: opening or bashing that case, taking from it, aiming an instrument at it, and throwing a Solution whose square holds it (onto it or beside it, whatever the Solution, so no refusal gives an unknown one away) are each refused `sheeted`; in S.9, the Lamp's case.
- **The lectern** is a fixture `{k: 'lectern', x, y}` on the plan's `l` once its Centrepiece is placed (`Viewing.place`, from `Dept.create`), read like a plaque (`read(x, y)`: free, within `READ_RANGE` and in sight; reading it changes nothing). Its words (`Viewing.words`, which LABELS-M4's label for it is to show): the pack's gateway label (`centrepiece.gateway`); the rule (`FIXTURES.lectern`'s clause); and what your ring does from the reading tile as you are now, your perks, a loud tile and a lit Lamp you wear counted (`Viewing.ring`, `Viewing.reaches`, with the ring pip's own measure): it reaches it (`CLAUSES.lectern_ring_reach`), or, with no lit Lamp worn, it does not and a lit Lamp's would ("Standing on the reading tile, your ring (2) does not reach the Centrepiece, so viewing adds no pips to it; a lit Lamp makes your ring 1 larger, enough to reach the Centrepiece, and then each turn of viewing adds a pip.", `lectern_ring_lamp`), or it does not (`lectern_ring_short`). Under the sheet it says the sheet's clause (`FIXTURES.sheet`) instead.

**A Centrepiece** (M4; `js/core/rules/centrepiece.js`; its phases, its Signature piece's tier and its line decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). Its record (§5.2) names its `form` (`fight` or `treasure`); its `archetype`, added with the shared rules around its own hooks (`Centrepiece.hooks`), or it cannot be placed; its `size`; the `specials` it has from the start, or instead `pieces`, an ensemble's one-tile bodies, each at its tile of the footprint (one on its top-left tile) with its own specials and, for its label, its own id, name, sprite, tombstone and paragraph where it gives them (§5.2); its `phases` (§5.3), each from the second giving at most one special; for one with a summons, the species it calls (`summons`, §8.1); and its `signature`, an item of its pack (a fight's drops when it falls; a treasure's is its treasure, which its own rules place and judge). `Centrepiece.place` stands it on the plan's footprint (`plan.cpl`, the top-left tile) in the room that holds it, its hall, asleep, with the `centrepiece` role's numbers at the depth and the role's health shared among its pieces (each a whole share, the first the rest too: `ROLE_CENTREPIECE_HP` is their total), its uids counting on from the hall's exhibits (§3.2), its openings every tile one steps into the hall from, staff doors included, and `dept.cp` recording it (§4.1); then `without` and each archetype's init apply to it as to any exhibit. It draws nothing.
- **Phases.** Its pieces share one phase: the first; the second once their health together falls to `CP_PHASE_PCT[0]` (67)% of their maximums together, or below; the third at `CP_PHASE_PCT[1]` (34)%. A phase never goes back, not when a Ticket heals it, and a blow past both thresholds enters each in turn. Each phase entered is told (`phase`, cause `damaged`, naming who hurt it; the Ordinance's own cause, `ensemble`, also says whether you see the piece that gains, §5.5), the first piece still standing gains the special that phase gives, and its archetype's own rules hear of it (`onPhase`); from the third its specials come every `CP_SPECIAL_EVERY[1]` actions (§8.1). A Centrepiece's rules may enter a phase for a reason of their own (`Centrepiece.advance`, with its cause).
- **Its specials take turns** (§8.1): at each of its actions, the one its turns give it, if it may mark it (`Centrepiece.act`, `Specials.markNext`), before anything else it does.
- **Its fall.** When its last piece falls (a death; the dust sheet is no defeat) it is defeated, once. A fight's Signature piece drops to the floor at its footprint's top-left tile, where it first stood (an ensemble's pieces always include one there; its line and the log say "where it first stood"), whatever your tote holds (`dropped`, cause `defeated`), as an item placed in the hall, a weapon, garment or instrument at the lower tier on offer at the depth (`TIER_LOW_BY_DEPTH`), so nothing is drawn; then the Certificate (`Progress.award`, by defeat, naming its first piece when that piece is called by the Centrepiece's own name; an ensemble's pieces each have a name of their own, so its Certificate names none: "you defeated its Centrepiece"), unless one was earned already; then its hall opens (`Hall.onDefeat`). A treasure's guardian falling opens its hall and does nothing else: killing it earns nothing, and its line says so. Nothing comes of a fall in a visit that ends with it.

**Earning it** (M4; `Progress.award`, which the Centrepiece's fall, a completed view and the Lamp carried out call). A department gives one Certificate a visit: the visit keeps it as `dept.cert` (`{how, turn}`), and the run as a record `{acc, depth, how, turn}` in `run.certificates`; a second way to it in the same visit, or one in a visit that is over, gives nothing (and a room for tests that names no department, `dept.acc`, cannot give one: asking it to is an error). What it gives comes in this order, each in the log:
1. the Certificate (`certificate`, its cause `defeat`, `view` or `treasure`);
2. your grade one higher, and your maximum health `HP_PER_GRADE` (5) higher (`grade`, whose line says both: "Your Handling Clearance is now Grade IV, and your maximum health is 5 higher.");
3. `CERT_HEAL` (5) health back (`healed`), up to the maximum just raised, so 28 of 30 becomes 33 of 35, and at full health nothing;
4. the perk offer (below).

At grade VII, the highest, the grade and your maximum health stay as they are and the log says so (`grade`, cause `highest`): the Certificate heals and offers a perk only. From your next decision everything that reads your grade reads the new one: your stats, which change once the action's ticks are done (§8.1), the case locks and the staff doors you can open (§8.3).

**The eight slice perks** (each has two ranks):

| Family | Perk | Rank 1 | Rank 2 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Guarded | Soft Soles | Your ring is 1 smaller | 2 smaller |
| Guarded | Vanishing Act | A hunter that loses sight of you skips its 8 turns and searches at once | and loses its next action |
| Guarded | Light Fingers | Opening a case costs 100 ticks and makes no noise | and opens locks one grade above yours |
| Swarm | Single File | An enemy that steps into a doorway beside you loses its next action, once per enemy | and takes 3 damage |
| Swarm | Elbow Room | +3 Padding while 3 or more enemies are adjacent | +5 |
| Artillery | Duck and Cover | When a special resolves and misses you, your next action costs 50 ticks less | and your next attack is a surprise attack |
| Artillery | Close the Distance | Your first step toward an exhibit targeting you costs 0 ticks, once per exhibit | two steps |
| Attrition | Second Wind | Once per department, falling below 25% HP heals 25% of max HP over 5 turns | 40% |

**The Guarded perks, exactly** (M4; PG-PERKS-A: `js/core/rules/perks/{soft_soles,vanishing_act,light_fingers}.js`, each registering its rules with `Perks.add`; their entries and clauses in `reg/perks-a.js`). A perk counts while you hold it at rank 1 or 2 and it is not switched off for the visit (`rulesOff`, §3.6). Its clause (`Registry.PERKS`, which the perk sheet and your label show) is its whole rule at both ranks, with its numbers of record.
- **Soft Soles.** Every ring of yours (§8.2: the ring drawn, the ring pip a boundary gives, the ring owed after a free or cheaper action, and the ring the lectern tells you of) is `PERK_SOFT_SOLES` (1, or 2 at the second rank) smaller, whatever your last action, your floor or your kit, and never below `RING_MIN` (1): standing still at the second rank it is 1, not 0.
- **Vanishing Act.** A hunter that would search for you `LOSE_TRACK_TURNS` (8) turns after it last saw you (§8.2's hunt; the healer's wait, §8.1, counts the same turns) searches at its first action out of sight of you instead, where it last knew you were (a clockwork hunter, A.4's healer, first makes the move its arrow showed, as its rule says, §8.1: if that move is not its search's first step, it searches at its next action out of sight, whose arrow shows it). Its `lost` event has the cause `vanished` (its turns those it had been out of sight; `how: 'case'` for one in an intact case, which says it stays there, §8.3). The Night Watchman is never touched, since it never searches (§8.4), and the perk's clause says so ("never the Night Watchman"). At the second rank, once that hunter has begun to search, it is also staggered (§8.1: for `STAGGER_TURNS`, its cause the perk), so it loses its next action, and that one only, whatever its speed. The Ticket's "hunters lose you" (§8.6) is the Ticket's rule, not this one.
- **Light Fingers.** Your opening of a case (yours only) costs `PERK_LIGHT_FINGERS_COST` (100) ticks, doubled while you are slowed as every action of yours is, and makes no noise. Nothing else of it changes: the touch comes first, so what wakes when its case is opened still wakes and a mimic still grips you and shows itself (§8.3); then the case opens and is told. At the second rank you also open case locks up to `PERK_LIGHT_FINGERS_ABOVE` (1) grade above yours (`Cases.lockGrade`); a staff door takes your grade alone, so it never opens one. While it counts, a case's rules on its label say so: their first sentence, how it opens, is the perk's (`CLAUSES.case_light_fingers`, at the second rank `case_light_fingers2`, put there by `Cases.rules`), "Opens for 100 ticks and no noise, with your Light Fingers, if your grade reaches its lock." or "… if its lock is no more than one grade above yours.", its ticks what opening it costs you; and a lock it opens above your grade reads "Your perks open its lock (Grade III), above your grade (Grade II)." (§8.8). The case's own clause (`FIXTURES.case`) states the opening without it.

**The Swarm and Attrition perks, exactly** (M4; PG-PERKS-B: `js/core/rules/perks/{single_file,elbow_room,second_wind}.js`, each registering its rules with `Perks.add`; their entries and clauses in `reg/perks-b.js`, the `second_wind` status in `reg/perk-status.js`; what a doorway is, which exhibits beside you count, and when Second Wind's first share comes, decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). Each counts as the Guarded perks do, and its clause is its whole rule at both ranks.
- **Single File.** After any step of an exhibit (its own walk, a darter's step back, a patrol's, a clockwork move, a rule's: `Perks.afterStep`), when the step brings a tile of it onto a doorway beside you that it did not cover before (an open door, `'`, or an archway, `,`: a closed door it opens counts once it steps into it; no exhibit steps onto a staff door or a shutter), it is counted, once for each exhibit for the rest of the visit, by its uid (`dept.perk.filed`): a split half or a summoned body has a uid of its own, and counts once when it steps in, never as it appears. Then, in order: the perk's event ("Single File: the Long-Footed Hopper stepped into a doorway beside you."); at the second rank `PERK_SINGLE_FILE_DMG` (3) damage (cause perk, its `raw` the 3, "… took 3 damage from Single File."), which no Padding reduces and which its archetype's rules take as they take any damage: a splitter left with 2 or more health splits, and a swarm body falls to it, losing all its health, which its sentence says with the 3 it was dealt, as a blow's does ("The Carbon Flimsies, in Bundles took 4 damage from Single File (3; it falls to any one hit: all 4 of its health)."); and, if it still stands, `staggered` (§8.1), so it loses its next action. A Centrepiece's pieces that never step (the Great Board, the Ordinance's) are never counted, nor is the Composite, which never leaves its hall (its openings are no tiles of it); the Night Watchman is, as any exhibit is.
- **Elbow Room.** Your Padding is `PERK_ELBOW_ROOM` (3, or 5 at the second rank) more while `PERK_ELBOW_ROOM_ADJACENT` (3) or more exhibits stand beside you (a tile of each within a king's move of yours), each not asleep and in no intact case, whether or not it ever strikes (a station, the healer and a Centrepiece's pieces count as any exhibit does, as the clause's "exhibits" says): a sleeper, an exhibit behind glass and a mimic in its disguise never count (so the perk never gives a mimic away), and a body of several tiles counts once. It is your perks' Padding (`Perks.padding`), which `Kit.derive` adds last, so it is counted when you act (PR4): when your stats are written at the end of each step (§8.1), from where all stand as you choose your next action, and it holds through that action and the world's turn after it. So every blow, shot and special between two of your decisions meets the same Padding, a mark shows what it deals, and the odds readout's figures are the real ones; an exhibit that comes beside you while the world acts raises it only at your next decision.
- **Second Wind.** Once a visit (`dept.perk.wind`), when damage you live through takes you from `PERK_SECOND_WIND_BELOW_PCT` (25)% of your maximum health or more to below it (exactly, in integers: 100 × health < 25 × maximum; with `invulnerable`, §10.2, kept at 1, you live), it comes: its event says what it heals in all, `PERK_SECOND_WIND_PCT` (25, or 40 at the second rank)% of your maximum health then, rounded down ("Second Wind: you fell below 25% of your maximum health, so from your next action you heal 5 health (25% of your maximum health) over 5 turns."), and it puts `second_wind` on you for `PERK_SECOND_WIND_TURNS` (5) turns (§8.1), which heals that total in shares, one for each of its turns (`healed`, cause `second_wind`). Damage that defeats you, or that finds you below the line already, brings nothing. It is healing, not regeneration, so it heals in the night watch and in a sealed hall, where regeneration stops.
**The Artillery perks, exactly** (M4; PG-PERKS-TIMING: `js/core/rules/perks/{duck_and_cover,close_the_distance}.js`, each registering its rules with `Perks.add`; their entries and clauses in `reg/perks-timing.js`; the time they change is §8.1's floor and §8.2's ring, plan A6; the choices below the table leaves open are decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable).
- **Duck and Cover.** A miss is a damaging special whose mark covered your tile when it was marked (§8.1, `Specials.atYou`: never a call, a summons or a restore) going off with you on none of its tiles while the visit goes on; a special that strikes you, or one never marked on your tile, gives nothing. A miss leaves a discount waiting (`dept.perk.duck`; the log says so once, `perk`, cause `duck`), and your next action that takes time (`Perks.adjust`: any action that costs a turn or more, so a wait, a view, an opening or a blow) costs `PERK_DUCK_AND_COVER` (50) ticks less and spends it, so a wait costs 50 and a Heavy blow 100. An action that takes no time (a read, a choice) or a step Close the Distance made free leaves it waiting, and misses before it is spent leave one discount, not several. At the second rank a miss also leaves a surprise attack waiting (`dept.perk.sure`; `perk`, cause `sure`): your next blow or shot at anything but the Night Watchman is a surprise attack (§8.1) whatever its target is doing, a hunter included, and spends it; against a Centrepiece it hits for certain and rolls its damage, as every surprise blow on one does, and a shot a reflector sends back is no surprise, as ever. The Night Watchman is never caught by surprise (§8.4), so a blow or shot at it is none and spends nothing. Each lasts until it is spent or the visit ends. What waits counts wherever your actions are judged: the threat (§8.1, `Perks.adjust` on its copy), the odds readout's surprise figure and the Attack button (`Perks.surprise`, which `Combat.surprised` asks with the state), and your perks' charges (`Perks.charges`: `{ticks, surprise}` while either waits). A discounted action's own words (a case's "Opens for 200 ticks") state that action's rule, not the discount; the discount is told when it comes, by the log's line at the miss (decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable).
- **Close the Distance.** An exhibit targets you while its pending damaging mark (a due line's included) covers the tile you stand on. A step of yours (a move that changes your tile; a bump into a door, a case or anyone is none) toward it, one after which your walking distance to its nearest tile (§8.2's measure, as the ring's) is less than before, takes no time, `PERK_CLOSE_STEPS` (1, or 2 at the second rank) times toward each exhibit in a visit (`dept.perk.closed`), and the log says so (`perk`, cause `close`, naming the exhibit as `who`, never `by`, since the step is yours, and saying which of its free steps it was); a step away from it, or one beside it that leaves the distance as it was, takes its time. No two damaging marks share a tile (§8.1), so at most one exhibit targets you at a time and the step is counted for it, which its clause says ("Damaging specials are never marked on the same tile, so the step is counted for that exhibit alone."); were there several, the first in spawn order with free steps left would pay. The step is a step in every other way (§8.2: your ring is the moving one, which the next boundary applies; it unseats you and starts a row of views again): only its time is gone, so your next decision comes at once, before anything else acts, and nothing can go off in it (the threat is none). Your perks' charges say who targets you and the free steps toward it you have left (`{who, steps}`). A step made free takes no time, so it leaves Duck and Cover's discount waiting: `Perks.adjust` asks Close the Distance first (`index.html`'s order, pinned by `test/m4-skeleton.test.js`).

**Families in the slice.** Placards show the challenges each visit actually holds: S.9 guarded only (its sample secondary, darkness, rests on lamps the slice cuts, §1), A.4 swarm and locks, M.11 artillery and hazard. S.9's missing secondary, and locks and hazard, have no slice perks, so their offer slot is filled from attrition, then guarded. If a slot's family has nothing left to offer (every perk held at rank 2), it is filled from the other family, then from any family. An offer never shows the same perk twice.

**The offer, exactly** (M4; `Progress.offer`). A perk can be offered while you do not hold it at rank 2, at the rank above yours.
- **The slots.** Two for the primary family, then one for the secondary (`PERK_OFFER`). Each in turn tries its own family, or, for a family with no slice perks (or none, as S.9's secondary), attrition then guarded; then the department's other family the same way; then any family, in the table's order (guarded, swarm, artillery, attrition). It goes to the first of these that still has a perk to offer once the slots before it have had theirs. So A.4 with Single File at rank 2 offers Elbow Room, Second Wind (its other family, locks, standing in as attrition), and one guarded perk; S.9 with every guarded perk at rank 2 offers Second Wind, Single File and Elbow Room.
- **The perks.** A family given slots offers the perks of it you hold at rank 1 first ("where possible", decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable), then those you do not hold. Where a family has more perks than the slots left for them, those offered are drawn on `perks/<acc>/d<depth>` (§3.2), one pick at a time; with no perk held that is only S.9's two guarded slots, so A.4 offers Single File, Elbow Room and Second Wind, and M.11 Duck and Cover, Close the Distance and Second Wind, every time. A family's perks fill its slots in the table's order.
- **Fewer than three.** With fewer than three perks left to offer, the offer shows what is left, one or two, as it is. With none left (every perk held at rank 2) no perk is given, no choice waits, and the log says "No perk was offered: you already hold every perk at its highest rank."
- **The choice.** The offer waits for your answer (`choose(index)`, §4.2; `ui.pendingChoice` `{k: 'perk', options: [{id, rank}]}`), which costs no time and makes that perk yours at the rank shown (`perk`, cause `chosen`; a perk you hold goes up in place, a new one joins the end of `run.perks`). While another choice waits, which happens when a Certificate comes as the world acts after you read a Catalogue Card, the offer waits behind it (`dept.offer`) and is shown once that choice is answered; while the perk choice waits, nothing else can be done, so no Card can be read then, and a Card's choice never waits behind the perk's. Neither choice can lock the visit: the one waiting always has an answer. A visit that ends while either waits (you die as the world acts) ends with it unanswered.

**In a single visit** (M4, until M5 builds the run; decided without play; revisit once M4 is playable). A Certificate's grade, health and perk last for the rest of the visit it was earned in. "Another visit" (§7.2) starts again with the visitor the visit was made for (§5.5: the median visitor, unless a carry says otherwise), never with what a Certificate gave it: carrying the grade and the perks from visit to visit is M5's run (§8.9).

### 8.8 Labels

*"Every exhibit and object has one: a plain Behaviour line that states exactly what it does, and a paragraph that is only for reading."*

**Examining** anything visible is free. The strip shows the nearest readable thing within 2 tiles (as Distance, below, counts them), in this priority: hunting or awake exhibit, sleeping exhibit, case contents (a shut case, by what it holds, an object on loan elsewhere, or nothing; an opened, broken or dissolved case only while it still holds its object, since an empty one has nothing left to read), plaque; nearest first within each, then the actors' and the fixtures' order. A mimic in its disguise is on it only as its case, which is shut and reads as a real empty shut case does.

**The full panel shows:**
1. the title, with the state and pips (the state icon is the board's glyph, §8.2's "Shown as": searching shows as hunting, reinstalling as returning);
2. the tombstone fields (maker, date, medium, dimensions, credit line, accession number); an exhibit's accession number is its pack id read as family letter, department and item (S.9.5), and a shared species (the Night Watchman), which no department accessioned, shows none;
3. the pictogram row; an exhibit's begins with its wake pictograms, from the same entries as its Behaviour line's wake and trigger parts: the ring ("ring") only beside the generic wake clause (never for a mimic, whose pipless clause the ring never touches, nor for an exhibit that never sleeps), then its trigger's icon, "never sleeps" for `awake` and "wakes" for the others;
4. the Behaviour line;
5. the odds readout;
6. the paragraph;
7. action buttons.

**Distance:** the paragraph and identification need you within 2 tiles (`READ_RANGE`). For an exhibit (from its nearest tile), a case and an item, the tiles are counted as a king moves, a diagonal step counting one, so the far corners 2 tiles off on both axes are within it. A plaque is read with `read(x, y)`, whose reach is the disc of "within r" (§8.2, §8.6), so a plaque in those corners is not, and its label offers Read exactly where `read` is taken. Farther away, the panel shows what sight gives and "Come closer to read".

**What each label holds** (`js/core/labels.js`). Every label has the panel's fields, and its buttons are only actions `step()` would take now: each is checked against its action's own legal list and your statuses, as `Step.legal` checks them.
- **An exhibit:** as above. Its Attack button names the blow you would make now (beside it, or with Reach) and what it would do, a surprise blow's certain hit included. A body split from another (a splitter's half, known by its uid `<body uid>/<k>`, §3.2) is named "a split of" its species, on its label and in every sentence, however often it settles back and is woken again.
- **A case:** what it holds, that its object is on loan elsewhere, that it is empty, or the exhibit standing in it; its glass, its lock and its cracks as pictograms; its rules (its fixture's and its glass's clauses, the first sentence Light Fingers' while it counts, M4, §8.7); whether your grade opens its lock (or your perks do, above it), and in how many blows your weapon breaks it; its accession number on its tombstone. Its buttons are "Open: Grade III" and "Break the glass: 2 blows, loud, the last a crash", or Take once it is no longer shut. A mimic's case gives exactly the label of a real empty case with its glass, lock and loan, but no number (§8.3).
- **A staff door:** its grade, and whether yours opens it; Open (a step into it) or Close.
- **An identifying label** (a plaque): the seal of the kind it describes (the pictogram of its shape, `seal_<shape>`, one for each of the eight seals, as on a consumable's label and in a case), and its rule; Read, within reach. Its text, which names the kind, shows only once the kind is identified.
- **An item:** a consumable's local name, its seal and its line (its kind's clause once identified, else the rule for a first use); any other item's name, tombstone and Behaviour line, its own numbers at your grade before any bonus, the grade it needs and how far short of it you are, its bonuses, and an instrument's charges. Its buttons are the actions that need no target (take, equip, take off, drop, drink, read); one that needs a target (a throw, an instrument's tile, a Restoration kit's item) is aimed in the Bag.
- **You:** your grade, your numbers, your ring now, and your statuses with the turns each still covers; Wait, Rest, Sit on a bench, and Leave on an exit.

**Pictograms:**
- about 55 in the slice, among them one for each status and each surface, keyed by its id, so the top bar and a label show what is on you or on a tile, one for each item kind, and one shape for each seal;
- each a fixed shape;
- colour from the reserved semantic set: hostile, hazard, readable, item, player.

### 8.9 Doors, and the run

*"Each department ends in doors whose placards say what kind of trouble lies behind each."*

**A placard shows:**
- the department's number and title;
- a one-line blurb;
- the challenge icons;
- the Centrepiece's name and form (fight or treasure).

**The slice run:**
- **Entrance Hall:** one tap to start; Your Collection on show. On the first run only, a one-room Orientation that teaches moving, examining, the ring, pips and bumping (about a minute), and opening a case and taking what it holds.
- **The Orientation** is built now (`content/orientation.js`), pulled forward from M5 at Julian's request on 31 October 2026, because people are playing the shared build; its case lesson is a change to this plan that Julian approved, since cases and items are now basic actions. Until M5's Entrance Hall it starts in place of the first visit. One authored room, the same every time (a fixed seed, depth 1, grade I, the starting kit), played by the game's real rules and nothing else: you at its west entrance; one sleeping Logbook Trolley (M.11's fodder, whose Behaviour line is short and plain); one case your grade opens, holding the Linesman's Smoked Apron, which needs no identifying; and an exit door on the east wall. It has no clock, so no bell and no night watch can come, and pips always fade (§8.2). Seven steps, each one sentence that waits until you have done it (§7.2's coach): move; read the exhibit's label, which says exactly what it does; bring it inside your ring and watch a pip fill; it gains a pip each turn inside your ring, three pips wake it, and one fades every 10 turns it gains none; strike it, since a hit wakes a sleeper at once, hunting, until it falls; open the case and take what it holds, which goes into your Bag; leave by the door. Leaving starts the visit waiting: on a first visit, the department, depth and seed the page asks for. Skip starts it at once. Finishing or skipping sets a flag in the browser (`localStorage`, `accession.orientation`), so the Orientation starts on a first visit only; Practice room in "How the museum works" plays it again, and `?orient=1` or `?orient=0` force it on or off (every browser check says which it wants). Practice room from a visit ends that visit, so while it is under way the confirmation sheet asks first ("Go to the practice room?"; Enter, Escape or a tap beside it, as every confirmation); the visit waiting after the practice room is then a new one, the next seed, as "Another visit" would start, and from the Orientation itself it starts again with the same visit waiting. Death is possible, since the rules are real: the usual recap shows, with Practice again beside Start the visit. The room is no department, and its spec says so (`Setup.room`'s `place`, `dept.place`: "practice room"; no department has one): its leave prompt asks "Leave the practice room?" ("A visit starts when you go through this door."), its case's label gives a department's locks at its depth ("in a department at this depth its lock is …", the rule being the same), and its floor plan says "This is what you have seen of the practice room."; every department's words are as they were.
- **Three floors of two departments,** with a Stair Hall after floors 1 and 2. The Stair Hall offers one of three: rest to full health, a free Restoration level, or one of three consumables shown by kind (taking one identifies its seal for the run; the two left identify nothing).
- **The Exit:** one hall at depth 7 with two exhibits recalled from this run, sleeping on pedestals, then the revolving doors and the run's summary label.

**The slice sequencer** (pool of 3):
- each department appears exactly twice;
- never twice on one floor;
- never the same department twice in a row;
- the doors from one room always show different departments.

The first department is drawn on the `doors` stream from all three. After that, each coda offers every department that fits, one door each; a department fits if some completion of the run satisfies every rule. Of the 48 sequences the first four rules allow, 30 remain; every prefix extends, and the doors after departments 1–5 number 2, 2, 1–2, 1–2 and 1. A test enumerates this.

**At most one new system per visit** (DECISIONS: "in early runs brings at most one the player has not met"). If both of a department's modules are unmet, its secondary is off for that visit, logged as an option, and the placard shows one module icon. The visitor's Catalogue records the modules met. In a slice run each department comes twice, so the second visit adds its secondary.

**Departures from DESIGN's run shell, made here:**
- The Orientation is one room of about a minute, not DESIGN default 23's five rooms with the Guard at Post One, because staff guards are out of the slice. It is excluded from the run time.
- The Stair Hall's third offer is one of three identified consumables, replacing the kiosk, because coins are out.
- DESIGN default 22's first-department rule for first-time players is deferred: the gate records how first departments go, and decides.

**Accession.** Leaving a department visit whose Certificate you earned lets you accession one piece of equipment you acquired there into Your Collection (D11). In the slice the kind does not yet join future loot pools.

**Second visits.** A second visit uses the same pack at the new depth with a fresh layout, and its Certificate counts again. If you hold, or have accessioned this run, that department's Signature piece, defeating its Centrepiece (or carrying out the Lamp) gives a Restoration kit in its place.
